# Decisions

Dated one-liners. Each names what lost, so nobody re-argues it without new evidence.

## 2026-09-16

- **Rebuild from scratch, not refactor.** HL_Playground's engine is good (2,974 passing tests); its interface and working method are not. Lost: another redesign pass on the old tree (five had been rejected on sight).
- **Nothing visual or rule-like comes from the old repo.** No stylesheet, token ramp, font, tile rule, entry frame, `.tsx`, CLAUDE.md constitution, px ratchet or lint baseline. Lost: "reuse the design system to move faster".
- **Local-first, backend-ready.** Dexie behind `src/data/repository.ts`; Supabase is Milestone 6 as an adapter. Lost: Supabase from day one (single user today; the seam costs nothing).
- **The repo is public and the full seed is committed**, cost columns included. Lost: a private repo or a redacted seed (the old repo already publishes it; the owner accepted).
- **Selling flow first.** Entry, home, picker, place, configurator, cascade, document, quotes register; then Cockpit. Lost: breadth-first.
- **Directions first, per major screen, after a reference sweep.** Two or three static directions on real seed content; the owner picks. Lost: build-then-review.
- **Imagery is a first-class workstream** with provenance for every picture. Lost: "pictures later".
- **The original HelmLogic's modules (with marks and heroes), organisation and user management, and document templates are in scope** (Milestones 4–5). Lost: leaving them on the later list.
- **Tailwind 4 with `@theme` tokens** for styling, with per-screen CSS for bespoke work. Lost: plain CSS with cascade layers (all three design proposals preferred it for guard reasons; a one-row swap if the owner prefers).
- **`@base-ui/react` 1.8** for headless primitives, not the older `@base-ui-components/react` release candidate.
- **The packer is Node (`tsx`) importing the old repo's committed `buildNorthsideProject()`.** Python is not installed on this machine; the committed TS seed is byte-reproducible from the extracts, so packing from it loses nothing. Lost: porting the Python generator.
- **Deterministic ids**: table = seed key, row = `key:ordinal`, field = column key. Lost: minted nanoids that would not survive a Postgres import.
- **`freeze.ts` and `pairs.ts` are ported near-verbatim with an injected `Ctx`**, never rewritten. Three judges were unanimous: re-deriving the freeze is where a settled number or refusal gets lost.
- **Quotes change through commands with inverses and `QuoteEvent`s**; an issued quote refuses with a sentence. Lost: mutators on the store.
- **The cascade is a route** (`/quote/$id/cascade?fix=&from=`), so Back, refresh and a shared link behave.
- **One configurator that contains Address.** Lost: two generations behind a hash switch.
- **`priceLevels` are declared per table by the packer.** No Northside column name lives in app code.
- **Chapters ARE the quote's sections**; `buildSteps` derives them from the frozen document. A direction that needs a chapter with nothing behind it draws from the frozen specs and colourways, never by widening the model.
- **`fitmentCascade` is the only cascade builder without a caller in the old repo; Milestone 1 wires it.**
- **The sixteen workbook rules are all blocked on the seed and are carried as data with their measured rates.**
- **A sheet wipe never clears quotes.** A quote is a photograph of what was offered on the day. A separate, explicit "forget everything" in Admin does.
- **No boot-time migrations or adoptions.** A fresh schema with `orgId` on every record and one versioned pack needs none; the first one added is a design smell to argue about.
- **Light theme only until a theme is first-class.** The old toggle produced a light app with dark scrollbars.
- **Sign-in is a name until Milestone 6.** A password that protects nothing is fake data.
- **Ports live in `tools/ports.ts`** (dev 5100, preview 5101). Lost: a port per config file.
- **The scaffold was hand-written.** `npm create vite` cancels itself on a non-interactive shell; the files are the same ones it would have written.
- **Four cost names the old repo never flagged are cost columns in the pack**: `Total Nett CTD` (the workbook's own "true cost"), `Settlement` and `Discount` (two deductions in the dealer's buy ladder, not a customer discount), and `GP` (gross profit, in every spelling). 24 columns across eleven tables were reaching every surface that trusts `costColumns`; 139 are now named. `RRP` and `Sell` stay visible: they are the customer's prices. Evidence in `tools/seed/levels.ts` and `docs/reference/MPF_GROUND_TRUTH.md`. Lost: porting the old list verbatim and calling it safe.
- **The seed's held pictures are 12.1 MB after fetching 109 more addresses** (329 held, 118 unheld, 6 refused). The old repo pinned a 12,000,000-byte ceiling in a test and said whoever crosses it must decide about size first; HL_2.0 carries no such ceiling. The owner decides if a smaller long edge or quality is wanted; nothing is discarded meanwhile.

## 2026-09-17

- **Stage pictures are a second tier, fetched at stage size.** `public/seed-images` stays capped at long edge 1100, which is right for a row or a tile; `public/hero-images` holds the few pictures that fill a window, at long edge 2560, chosen in `data/northside/heroes.json` and recorded in `heroes-ledger.json`. Measured: of 329 held catalogue copies only 39 reach 1100px, and the Stacer 529 Assault Pro's on-water photograph is 1024×676 because the address the price file carries is itself a WordPress resize. Drawing a stage from those meant upscaling. Lost: one tier for everything.
- **A hero cannot be chosen automatically, and the code now says so.** The scene/studio judge answers "is this a render on a white ground", not "is this a wide shot of the whole boat". Picking the largest verified photograph for four Stacer models returned a transom on white, a seat locker, a floor locker and a steering console: right model, right source, wrong picture. `tools/seed/pick-heroes.ts` now reads the maker's own file name first (Stacer marks on-water frames "Lifestyle" or "EXT" and cabin frames "INT" or "Internal"), keeps the judge as a second gate, and every pick was looked at on a contact sheet before it was kept. Lost: trusting a label.
- **Stacer publishes its on-water photography at web size.** Its lifestyle frames are mostly 1180×600; the widest are the 519 Sea Ranger at 3000×1985 and the 481 SeaMaster at 1771×1183. Highfield's media library, by contrast, carries 5,000 to 9,500 pixel originals per model. So a photographed direction drawn on a Highfield fills a window and one drawn on most Stacers does not, and a board must say which it is standing on.
- **Customisation is a first-class requirement, and bounded.** The owner: "things should be so dynamic so users can control everything and set their own backgrounds… full massive customisability". Every token is already emitted to `:root` under `@theme static`, so an organisation layer and a person layer override by setting the same custom properties at boot; no screen reads an appearance record. Every choice is measured rather than hoped: a chosen photograph gets a scrim computed from its own pixels, a chosen colour is checked against the ground it will sit on, and a choice that cannot hold contrast is refused with a sentence and the nearest thing that works. Not settable: anything that puts cost on a customer surface, the refusal sentences, the price figure's behaviour, the contrast floor. Written up in `docs/CUSTOMISATION.md`. Lost: a settings page bolted on at the end, and an unbounded page builder (named as an open question for the owner).
- **Responsive on every screen size, and it is measured, not promised.** The owner, 2026-09-17: "reminder that responsive on EVERY screen size". The plan had promised only that "phone width does not break" in Milestone 1, with tablet and phone parity deferred to Milestone 2. That is now wrong: every ruler runs at six viewports — phone 390×844, phone landscape 844×390, tablet 834×1112, laptop 1280×800, desktop 1440×900 and wide 1920×1080 — so a layout that only holds at the size it was drawn at fails in CI rather than in front of a customer. Playwright's own device presets are not used: `Pixel 7` carries a 2.625 pixel ratio, which breaks the screenshot determinism recipe a ruler asserts, and `iPad (gen 7)` selects WebKit, which this project does not install. Each project states its own viewport on Chromium and whether it is a touch screen. Measured on this change: 32 checks pass across all six. Lost: treating small screens as a later pass.
- **Brand marks are held, with the two gaps named rather than filled.** The home sweep flagged `public/brand-marks/` as an outright blocker: no board could draw a brand shelf. `tools/seed/marks.ts` now holds seventeen files covering twelve of the thirteen brands the seed carries, chosen from what the imagery sweep verified live, SVG where the maker publishes one. Two rules earn their place. A candidate whose file name says badge, series or heading is refused, because Mackay's "KRX-Badge-01" and Dunbier's "SupaRolla" are product names, not marks. And a raster mark is measured rather than trusted: Mercury publishes exactly one mark, calls it a footer logo, and every opaque pixel in it is pure white, so filed as the dark variant it would have shipped as an invisible logo on a white page. It is filed as `mercury-white` and the absence of a dark one is recorded. Stabicraft publishes nothing but a 180px touch icon, so it has no mark and a screen that wants one says the name in type. Every pick was looked at on a contact sheet. Lost: trusting a file name.
- **One comparator, and the solver now uses it.** `src/domain/rules/configure/values.ts` carried a second copy of the rule engine's ordering with a comment saying it "deliberately mirrors" the first; the copies had already drifted, because the engine learned to read a measurement ("10 HP" is ten horsepower) and the solver's copy had not. So the configurator's preview pruned values the run would have kept, on the exact column the defect was measured on. `compare` now delegates to `@/domain/compare` and keeps only what is genuinely its own: the three-valued answer (a `mismatch` on the shared comparator is `undefined` here, which is what lets a rule fail open) and the two image cases (an empty picture list reads as blank, a non-empty one orders against nothing). Pinned by `configure/sameComparator.test.ts`. Lost: two copies kept in step by hand.
- **The nine places are minted when the pack lands, and their ids are derived from their names.** A module name is a business string, so the plan keeps the places out of the data; `@/data/pack/boot` is the app's one caller of `mintModules`, and `src/test/fixtures/pack.ts` is the other, so a suite and the browser see the same nine. The id is `place:` plus the slug of the name rather than a fresh one, because a sheet wipe keeps the quotes and the file then lands again underneath them — a quote that named a place must not find nothing where it looked. Stamped with the pack's own `packedAt`, so a second landing writes identical records. Lost: a fresh id per load, which is what the old store action minted.
- **`meta` still means "the whole sheet is down there".** The pack's tables land one transaction each, then the places, and only then is `CatalogueMeta` written — so a browser closed at the wrong moment comes back to a sheet it knows is incomplete rather than to one with no doors and nothing saying so. And a failed WRITE is not a failed READ: the sheet is handed to the store anyway with the sentence beside it, because a private window or a full disk does not make the 15,691 rows that were just read wrong. Only the speed of the next open is lost. Proven end to end in `src/data/pack.dexie.test.ts` and in the browser by the second `pack-loads` flow, which counts the JSON requests a reload makes: zero.
- **`src/domain/index.ts` names the doors, not the folder.** One published entry point per area with the types its answers come in, and three words deliberately left unpublished because two areas answer different questions with them (`buildGroups`, `optionsOf`, and discovery's own `Candidate`/`Verdict`, which the contract already owns). Importing from the barrel is never required and nothing inside `src/domain` does it. Lost: `export *`, which is a second copy of the folder listing and a cycle waiting to happen.
- **A test moves with its subject.** The three "registry seams" cases in `rules/adopt.test.ts` drove `constraintDefs` as a module-level registry mirrored in browser storage, which `src/domain` cannot have; they now stand in `rules/constraints/deleteRule.test.ts` against the pure API, under the same name, with the same assertions word for word and a fourth beside them. `adopt.test.ts` carries the note saying where they went. Lost: keeping a passing copy in both files, which is two answers to one question again.
- **A dropped test is a defect, and six of them were dropped.** The round-1 critique counted node suites folder by folder against `HL_Playground` and found six that came across as nothing at all, with no note anywhere saying so — `issue.test.ts` (19), `link.test.ts` (19), `moduleBlocks.test.ts` (11), `newRule.test.ts` (19), `referenceDay.test.ts` (6) and `quoteTerms.test.ts` (5). Two of the six left plan-named modules with no coverage whatsoever: eight of `totals.ts`'s ten exports and all four issue-blocker sentences, and `freezeCustomer`/`customerBook`/`hasCustomerRegister`/`fileCustomer`. All six are in the tree now. Of their 79 cases, 70 came across with their assertions word for word; four could not, every one an assertion about `ensureCustomerRegister` — five project-store writes collapsed into one history step, which is M2 — and they are named in `src/state/customerLink.test.ts` with what they wait on. The remaining five are `quoteTerms`, and they are the one suite in this round whose assertions had to be rewritten rather than moved: what it drove was `useProjectStore.setQuoteTerms`, and the behaviour now lives at the mint, where `freeze.ts` copies `ctx.org.quoteTerms` onto the document. Its header says exactly that, and names the two promises that belong to the admin screen instead. Lost: the idea that a suite whose subject changed shape may be left behind quietly.
- **A store-backed suite is ported, never dropped: the registry becomes a shim, a context or the store itself.** Three different answers, one rule. `issue.test.ts` drove a module-level quote registry, so the six registry calls are re-created over `apply` at the top of the file in nine lines and every test body is the old one unchanged. `link.test.ts` drove the project store and the registry at once, so it sits in `src/state` — the one place that may import both — with the four catalogue writes expressed as the next `CatalogueCtx`. `moduleBlocks.test.ts` drove `createModule` over the demo seed, so it reads `loadPack()` and calls the same `defaultBlocksFor` the store action called; every figure the old file measured is still true — Stacer 4, Stabicraft 6, Surtees 5, Jeanneau 6, Haines 3, Highfield 6, Formosa 4. Lost: "it needs a store, so it cannot be ported".
- **`nthToday` came back, and nothing could mint a reference without it.** It was the one export of the old `quotes.ts` with no caller in the new tree, so `mintQuote` demanded a `reference` that no code could compute — and the timezone defect it guards, two halves of `20260818-01` reading two different calendars at UTC+10, had no test at all. It is `freeze.ts:nthToday(quotes, day)` and `referenceForNow(quotes, at)` now: the registry arrives as an argument and the instant is read exactly once, which is the property the old function took `new Date()` itself to protect. Lost: a pure module calling the clock.
- **`vitest/expect-expect` is off for four named files, not for every test in the repo.** It had been off repo-wide to accommodate ported suites whose assertions the rule cannot see statically — through a helper (`expectPreviewMatchesQuote`, `roundTrip`) or inside a loop over the real pack. Off everywhere, a test that asserts nothing at all passes the linter in silence, which is the shape of hollow test this whole port exists to prevent. Lost: one line of config in exchange for a blind spot over 143 files.
- **Four dependencies were floating on `"*"` and are now pinned to what the lockfile holds** — `lucide-react` ^1.46.0, `@testing-library/{jest-dom,react,user-event}` ^7.0.1/^16.3.3/^14.6.7. CI runs `npm ci && npm test`; with a star spec, the next `npm i` would have floated any of the four and the gate would have been measuring a different tree than the one that was green. Lost: nothing — the resolved versions did not move, and `npm ci --dry-run` agrees.
- **Every motion value names its source, because four numbers in a cubic-bezier carry no provenance.** The round-2 audit found three easing tokens in `src/styles/tokens.css` byte-identical to `HL_Playground/src/styles/system.css` — same three names, same order — with the duration ladder beneath them the old `D_PRESS/D_FAST/D_MED/D_SLOW` renamed. Nothing in the file said where they came from, so a reader could not tell a curve chosen from a published spec from one lifted off the old ramp, and the standing rule is that nothing rule-like crosses. The curves and the ladder are now Material Design 3's published motion tokens, each line naming the token it is (`md.sys.motion.easing.standard`, `.legacy`, `.emphasized.decelerate`, and short2/short3/short4/medium2 for 100/150/200/300 ms), so the check is against the spec rather than against this file. `src/ui/motion.ts`'s springs were 0.16/0.2/0.26 — the same old ladder in seconds — and are now the same three durations as the CSS, one ladder with one citation. The focus ring keeps its two-ring recipe and now says why: WCAG 2.2 SC 2.4.13 wants 2px at 3:1, which a single ring cannot promise against an unknown ground. Lost: values that were probably fine and could not be shown to be.
- **Three suites deferred one property to the same absent screen, so the property was built instead.** `constraints/tenantKey.test.ts` (14), `store/undo.test.ts`'s "when the business was set up" (4) and `quote/quoteTerms.test.ts`'s "SURVIVE A RENAME" (1) were each about one sentence — *what a business wrote is still there after it changes what it is called* — and each was pushed onto the admin screen that types the organisation, which does not exist. Nothing in 2,267 tests measured it. The act is `src/domain/people/organisation.ts` now: `setOrganisation` and `setQuoteTerms` as pure functions over the profile, with the old store action's argued comments intact and the clock injected. Fourteen cases in its suite. Ten of `tenantKey`'s cannot ever come across and the file says so — they drive `orgKeyOf`, `legacyOrgKeyOf` and `adoptSlugKey`, and `orgId` on every record retires all three (and "no boot-time migrations" retires the fourth). Lost: a fourth deferral.
- **`keepingOrganisation` put back two of the profile's five fields, and the port was faithful to that.** The old wrapper called `setOrganisation(keep.name, keep.industry)` into an action that fell back to `meta.org?.createdAt` — which the `replaceProject` it was wrapping had just deleted. So the one function written to make a project swap invisible re-dated the business to the moment of the swap, re-derived the tenant key off whatever the business is called today, and dropped the dealership's standing quote terms: TENANCY §4.6 happening inside the single caller the rule was written for. `ApplyPorts.setOrganisation` now takes the whole `OrgProfile`, which is the only shape that cannot forget a field. The memory harness was hiding it — its `replaceProject` kept the profile, so the wrapper would have passed whether or not it put anything back — and now clears it exactly as the store does. Four cases in `src/domain/io/keepOrganisation.test.ts`. Lost: a port so faithful it carried the defect and no test.
- **Two more guards, and both close a hole rather than measure tidiness.** `domain-is-pure` matches import lines, so `document.querySelector` or `new Image()` written inside a domain function passed it — the plan put the globals on the agent, which holds for one port and not for the many sessions that will touch `src/domain` next. `domain-touches-no-dom` reads the masked source the way the two text rules do, so a comment explaining the constraint does not trip it. And `source-is-text` refuses a raw U+0000 or a CRLF line ending: `HL_Playground/src/features/search/encoding.test.ts` records a `.tsx` that carried three NUL bytes and 931 CRLF lines while `tsc`, 1,914 tests, the linter and the style checker were all green, because a NUL in a template literal is a valid separator — it only broke `grep` and git's `text=auto`. `.gitattributes` and `prettier --check` cannot see it. Thirteen rules now, every one with a fixture test; `data/` is out of the text rule's scope on purpose, being 11 MB nobody types. Lost: a guard set that could not see the one defect class that had already shipped once.
- **"Nothing from the old design system" is now a guard, not a promise.** It was in the brief of every agent that has touched this repo and it was broken anyway: three easing tokens in `src/styles/tokens.css` were byte-identical to `HL_Playground/src/styles/system.css:820-822`, same names, same order, found by the second Milestone 0 critic. The first fix replaced one of them with a curve that is a real declaration in the old repo's `ds.css:518` under the same name, which the new guard caught immediately. `tools/check/oldSystem.ts` reads every authored value in the old repo's stylesheets — curves, shadows, gradients, font stacks, 67 of them — and refuses any that reappears in ours. It compares values, not names, because `--ease-out` is a name anyone would pick and `cubic-bezier(0.23, 1, 0.32, 1)` is a decision somebody made. Values nobody could choose differently are never compared. One narrow exemption exists as an auditable list: a published standard is not the old repo's decision even when the old repo cited it too, so a value may pass only when it is named in `CITED` with the spec it comes from, and a test holds that list to eight. If the old repo is absent the rule finds nothing and passes; a clone on a fresh machine must still build. Lost: trusting that a rule in a brief is a rule.
- **A ruler is a module the app walks and a fixture page proves.** The five rulers each guarded their own non-vacuity (`expect(r.measured).toBeGreaterThan(0)`) and none of them could be shown to find a defect: the measuring functions were closures passed to `page.evaluate`, unexported and unimportable, so the contrast sweep's five hard-won corrections — the `color(srgb …)` parse, the browser-side oklch conversion, compositing the WHOLE ancestor chain, compositing translucent text, and a leaf being "an element with a text node of its own" — were arithmetic nobody had watched fail. On one placeholder route with two measurable text nodes, a parser returning white for every colour would have reported the same clean green. Each measurement is now its own module under `e2e/rulers/measure/`, moved with every line of its reasoning, and `e2e/rulers/fixture.spec.ts` points all five at pages built to fail one named way at a time: known ratios (2.85, 1.4 through two tints, 3.98 composited), a planted overlap beside its three exemptions, a cut beside an elision on both axes, tokens declared inside an `@media` so the recursive rule walk is exercised, and twenty rows in view beside one below the fold. Lost: "it guards that it measured something", which is the floor, not the proof.
- **A guard says how many files it read, and reading none is a failure.** `no-cost-column-in-a-screen` — the rule that keeps cost and margin off a customer's page, the honesty CLAUDE.md states first — was scoped to `src/screens/`, which does not exist on this tree. It read zero files while `check` printed `14 rules, no failures`, and its fixture test could not see that, because a fixture hands a rule a fabricated `src/screens/…` path and passes either way. Only a walk of the real tree knows. So `runRules` returns a per-rule file count, `tools/check.ts` prints every rule with its count and exits non-zero on any rule that read nothing, and a suite in `rules.test.ts` runs the same walk. The scope of both surface rules is now every folder that renders — `src/screens/`, `src/routes/` and `src/ui/` — because the plan's own layout puts the quote DOCUMENT at `routes/quote.$id.document`. Lost: a fixture test taken as proof that a rule is pointed at something.
- **The hero and brand-mark ledgers are measured the way the image ledger is, and the bytes are hashed.** `images.json` had 8 suites over it; `heroes-ledger.json` and `marks-ledger.json` had none — nothing in src, tools or e2e read either file except the scripts that write them. So nothing asserted that a hero's file is on disk, that a mark's artwork is the artwork recorded, or that a hero's `table` and `model` resolve to a real row, which is CLAUDE.md's "a picture belongs only to the exact model it depicts" stated as arithmetic. `src/data/imagery.test.ts` is 13 cases over both: every entry accounted for one-for-one against the folder, every recorded `sha256` recomputed from the bytes on disk, no hero larger than the source it came from, every hero's model found in its table's display field, every mark's brand named by a table or by a row of the price file, and the one refused mark carrying a measured reason with no stand-in. Lost: provenance that was true when a person last looked.
- **`no-old-design-system` reads code as well as stylesheets, and the run says how much evidence it had.** The round-2 defect had two halves — three easing tokens in `tokens.css` AND `src/ui/motion.ts`'s springs, the same ladder in seconds — and the rule as written saw only `.css`. It now also searches `.ts`/`.tsx` under `src/` for the old values as literal strings, which is the shape a curve takes when it is lifted into a transition template; test files are out of reach, because the fixture that proves this rule fires has to fabricate an old value. And because CI runs on ubuntu-latest where HL_Playground has never existed, the rule is silent there on every run: `tools/check.ts` now prints how many old values it compared against and from where, so "compared 67 values" and "compared none" are different lines rather than the same green tick. Lost: a guard that could go dark without anybody noticing.
- **A refusal is reachable, and that is asserted rather than inherited from a library.** `Menu` and `Select` proved only that a refused item CARRIES its sentence (`toHaveAccessibleDescription`), which is a DOM fact; whether a keyboard can land on the item — the thing that makes the sentence audible — rested on two Base UI internals (`useMenuItem`'s `focusableWhenDisabled`, `SelectRoot`'s empty `disabledIndices`). "Base UI APIs move — each wrapped once in `src/ui`" is the plan's own reason for the wrappers, so a minor bump could turn the refusal silent on exactly the control a dealer meets it on with the suite green. Both suites now walk the arrows onto the refused row, assert focus lands there, and assert it still refuses to act. Lost: a behaviour guaranteed by someone else's implementation detail.
- **A guard that reads no files now fails, because a fixture test structurally cannot see that.** The round-3 critic found `no-cost-column-in-a-screen` scoped to `src/screens/`, a folder that does not exist on this tree, so the guard protecting "cost and margin never reach a customer-facing surface" read zero files — and the run printed `check: 14 rules, no failures`, which is exactly what it prints when fourteen rules have really looked. A fixture hands the rule a fabricated `src/screens/…` path, so it passes either way; only a walk of the real tree knows. `runRules` now returns a per-rule file count, the run prints it, and `blindRules` turns "read nothing" into a failure with its own fixture. The cost rule went from 0 files to 34. Lost: trusting that a green guard looked at anything.
- **The five rulers now have a page built to fail in front of them.** `e2e/rulers/fixture.spec.ts` points each measurement at a known defect and watches it come back. Each ruler already guarded its own non-vacuity, but on a tree with one placeholder route "0 below threshold" is also what a silently broken parser returns. The measurement half of each ruler moved into `e2e/rulers/measure/` so the fixture can drive it directly. Lost: a ruler that has never been seen to fail.
- **The owner handed the design decisions over: "go for what u think is awesome and build literally everything please before i review it" (2026-09-17).** So every remaining Milestone 1 screen is researched, given directions, decided here and built, without stopping for a pick. The boards are still drawn and kept, because they are the record of what was considered and the thing he can point at to change his mind cheaply; what changes is that the recommended one is built immediately and the screen is marked **provisional** in `docs/SCREENS.md` until he has looked. Nothing else relaxes: the sweep still precedes the board, the board still cites its frames, the critic still judges the built screen, and no figure is ever invented. Lost: waiting.
- **The token set is the two picked boards, measured off them value by value.** `src/styles/tokens.css` was a placeholder written before any direction existed, and its own header said the first pick replaced it wholesale; entry B "Veil and card" and home B "Cinema day" are picked, so it is theirs now. Every value is cited on its own line as a byte off one of the two boards, or as a published spec (Material Design 3's motion tokens, WCAG 2.2 SC 2.4.13's focus ring), or as one of nine interpolations that say which two cited anchors they sit between. The neutrals are the boards' navy, hue 232 to 245, not grey; the blue means the file and nothing else (`--color-blue-600` is entry B's `#1A65BD` door); the amber is home B's act and has exactly the two steps that board draws. Where two board values sit closer than the eye reads them the comment names the one that folded, with the distance — the boards set 34 colours and 19 type sizes and a ramp cannot carry that. All 26 opaque colours are inside the sRGB gamut, read back through the cascade, which answers the provisional file's nine clamped blues; the four white lines are `rgb(255 255 255 / n%)` rather than `oklch(100% 0 0 / n%)` because the ramp ruler caught the latter resolving to 1.00007 and flagging itself outside the gamut. Lost: `--color-black`, `--radius-xl`, `--radius-2xl`, `--shadow-xs`, `--shadow-sm` and `--text-7xl`, which nothing reads and no picked board draws; and a mid-scale control border that clears 3:1 on white, which neither board has a value for and which is written down as measured rather than invented.
- **Home is built from board B, "Cinema day", and is provisional until the owner looks (2026-09-17).** Recommended by the independent critic as the only board that "looks like a boat business rather than a spreadsheet with a greeting on it". Lost: A "A shelf of kinds" (its brand shelf survives as the levelled maker cells), C "Rails of counts" (the shape the owner has already rejected five times), D "The file, open". Three things the board draws could not survive contact with what is true on this build, and each is answered in a sentence rather than by drawing it anyway: **New quote refuses**, because the picker is the next screen and does not exist — so the act keeps its focus, its name and its reason, and loses its amber, since the one thing amber says is "press this"; **neither plate is a door**, because a register has no screen yet, so each caption names its register and counts it and one line under the fold says why it does not open; and the desk's picture-provenance sentence moved under the fold, where the pictures are. Measured: the page is exactly 1440×900, 1280×800 and 1920×1080 with nothing scrolling and nothing dropped, which cost the fold 20px at 1440 and 28px at 1280 — the board's own trade ("the fold gives up the height, not the sheet"), made twenty pixels earlier because a built screen carries sentences a picture did not have to draw.
- **The two picture ledgers ride in the bundle; nothing on home fetches.** `heroes-ledger.json` and `marks-ledger.json` are 26 rows between them and are imported through Vite's `?raw` from the one source in `data/northside/`, because the promise the second visit makes is that it reads nothing over the network — `pack-loads.spec.ts` counts the requests. That collided with the dev middleware, which answered Vite's own module request for those files with `application/json` and broke the route; `servePack` now passes any request carrying a query to Vite, since the app fetches a pack file by its plain address and never adds one. Lost: fetching two more files on every paint, and a second copy under `public/` that could go stale.
- **A kind is named as the dealership names it, and "Custom table" never reaches a reader.** Home's six figures are the table kinds, but `TableKind` is a word from the contract: `custom` is the absence of a kind, and its three registers are Labour Rates, Oils & Consumables and Registration Costs. So where exactly one place holds a kind the panel uses that place's own name ("Parts & Accessories", not "Accessories"), where several do the kind's noun covers them ("Packages"), and `custom` names its registers. Lost: printing the model's word at a dealer, and the board's hand-written labels, which would be false of a second dealership's file.
- **Entry is built from board B, "Veil and card", and is provisional until the owner looks (2026-09-17).** Recommended by the independent critic as the only one of the four "alive on sight": real dusk water, a mark treated as an object instead of a caption in a corner, one real blue doing one job, and the file's own counts printed inside the door rather than captioned under it. Lost: A "Water and column" (Porsche's frame with the colour drained out of the half that was designed), C "Two doors mirrored" (no photograph anywhere — the database picture the owner has rejected five times), D "The sheet already open" (53 leadered rows as the first thing the eye meets). Four decisions the board could not make for itself, because it is a picture drawn at one size with its state faked: **the panel naming the photograph's own row is read at runtime** from `manifest.json` and `heroes-ledger.json`, so `boat_stacer · 91 rows · Stacer 481 SeaMaster` is counted rather than typed and a second dealership's file renames it; **the photograph is capped at the ledger's own pixels** and the caption prints the size it is painted at, measured off the element, which is how "never enlarged" stays checkable at 1920 (where it is drawn at exactly 1,771 wide and the room shows on the left, under the deepest part of the veil); **a load says what it is doing as two named steps that tick**, because the sweep found no frame anywhere showing a determinate bar for a load like this and did find the machine naming the one thing it is doing (`research/refs/entry/notes.md` §7); and **the empty state is read out of the quote repository** rather than asserted, because "no quote exists" is a claim about this browser. Lost with them: a spinner, a progress bar, a hard-coded picture path, and the board's own "drawn here at 1600 × 1069", which was true of one window.
- **Entry's reflow is the builder's, because the entry boards predate the requirement, and the rule that decides it is that the photograph is the WINDOW's ground.** Fixed behind everything, it costs no layout height at any width, so nothing the picture does can push the doors under the fold — the owner's condition, held by construction rather than by tuning. At 390×844 one column, 16px gutter, the pennant, the ask and both doors above the fold and the evidence panel below it (measured: the second door's foot at 813 of 844); at 844×390 the doors take a column of their own beside the ask, because 390px of window is less than the card is tall and the act must be on the first screen; at 834×1112 one column with the panel back above the ask, which is the board's own reading order; at 1280×800 and 1440×900 the board's composition, with the drop between the panel and the card given back to a window under 880px tall rather than letting the empty state scroll; at 1920×1080 the band caps at 1440 and centres. The primary act is never a floating bottom bar at any width. Lost: a layout that only holds at 1440×900, and the tablet order at 844×390 (834 and 844 are ten pixels apart in width and 722 apart in height, so the order is a question about height).
- **`no-old-design-system` compares what was written, and a fragment is not a value.** It read every declaration through `declarationValues`, which blanks out `var(…)` — right for the literal-colour guard, wrong for a rule that says "byte-identical": a veil written entirely out of this repo's own tokens strips to `linear-gradient(to bottom,  , transparent)`, the shell of a gradient with the decision taken out, and that shell matches the old repo as it would match half the stylesheets on the web. Three findings on a screen that had lifted nothing, which is how a guard gets routed around. It now reads the raw value, which also makes it stricter (a `var(--t-ink)` lifted off the old ramp reads as the old ramp instead of as a blank), and skips any value whose brackets do not close, since a multi-line declaration arrives one line at a time. Four cases in `tools/check/rules.test.ts` hold both halves, including one proving the same gradient still fails when it really is the old repo's. Lost: an exemption in `CITED`, which is for published standards and not for false positives.
- **The first-visit rule is two `beforeLoad` redirects and nothing else: no name lands on Entry, a name lands on Home.** `/` sends a nameless browser to `/sign-in`, `/sign-in` sends a named one to `/`, and both run before a screen is drawn — a component that redirected from an effect would paint Home, discover it had no name, and dismiss the screen it had just painted. It is not authentication and never claims to be: the name is a per-browser convenience in prefs and anyone can edit it there, so what the rule protects is the order of the two screens, not the app. Lost: a guard written once on one side (which leaves the other address reachable by typing it), and a `<RequireName>` wrapper, which is a shared page component by another name.
- **Home reads this browser; Entry reads the file — structurally, not by convention.** `src/routes/index.tsx` calls `catalogue.load(repository)`, which cannot fetch; `openCatalogue`, the one function that can read the pack over the network, is called only behind Entry's blue door. Before this, Home opened the pack itself whenever the store was empty, which quietly falsified the blank door's own promise — a person who pressed "Start a blank sheet" got all 53 tables one navigation later, and the entry suite's `expect(packRequests).toBe(0)` passed only because it counted before the fetches landed. Now a blank sheet stays blank across a reload and the count is zero because there is no code path to make it anything else. Lost: a screen that helpfully loads what its visitor just declined.
- **The catalogue store says where its sheet came from and what that source called the business.** `from` is `'pack'` or `'repository'`, `business` is the manifest's name or the name filed beside the sheet; both are derived from the source `load` was already handed, so neither is a new fact — they are two the store was throwing away. They exist because the screen that reads the file and the screen that prints what was read are two different screens with a navigation between them, and Home was being handed nulls for both. Lost: threading provenance through a search param (a position is a search param; a provenance is not), and a second store holding a second truth.
- **`/sign-in?again` is the way back to the door, so "the file can be loaded later" is a promise and not a line.** The blank door leaves a desk with no price file in it, and Home now says so and offers the door; pressing it reaches Entry with the remembered name already in the field, and the closing sentence says why the screen is open again. The flag is compared with `=== true` rather than for truthiness: measured on this router, a key `validateSearch` drops is handed to `beforeLoad` as the RAW value, so `?again=later` arrives as a truthy string — only the validator can produce the boolean. Lost: a blank sheet with no way out but clearing the browser's storage, and weakening Entry's sentence to match what the app could do.
- **Milestone 0's proof page is deleted, and its evidence moved into the flows.** `src/routes/index.tsx` was a page whose whole job was printing 53 · 15,691 · 28 and which of the two places the sheet came from. `e2e/flows/pack-loads.spec.ts` now walks the real door in a real browser, reads the same figures off Home's own stamp, reloads and reads them again with the network counted — the same claim, made about the screen a dealer actually uses. Lost: a page nobody sells a boat from, and `smoke.spec.ts`'s assumption that `/` is Home.
- **Every browser-side test walks through the door, and a route says so in `e2e/routes.ts`.** `arrive: 'through-the-door'` makes `open()` give a name on Entry and press the blue door before it measures Home; seeding `hl2.session` into localStorage would have been quicker and would have proved nothing. The handover — the name and the sheet surviving a navigation — is now asserted by all five rulers at six viewports as a side effect of their arriving at all. Lost: a ruler that measures a screen no visitor could have reached.
- **A refused act keeps its amber, two steps down the same ramp (2026-09-17).** The entry above decided the opposite — "loses its amber, since the one thing amber says is 'press this'" — and a measurement beat the argument. `--color-act` then appeared nowhere on the built Home: the picker does not exist, so the one act on the screen rendered drained to `--color-panel`, quieter than the search field beside it, and the loudest object on a boat dealership's main screen was a white card of other companies' logos. Board B was recommended in one sentence — "one amber button that is unmistakably the thing you press… the only board with any colour on it at all, which is what 'blue and white, and a bit more colour usage' asks for" — and that sentence had become false of the thing built from it, for the length of a whole milestone. The rule `button.css` already stated twelve lines above is the right one: a refused primary DOOR keeps the file's blue two steps down, because draining it says "this is dead" about an act that is merely not built yet. So `--color-amber-600` is extrapolated two steps down the amber ramp on the ramp's own step (−8.4% lightness, −0.0168 chroma), and the ink moves from neutral-900 to neutral-950 because neutral-900 on that fill measures 4.39:1 and would be the one pair in `tokens.css` under the floor; neutral-950 measures **4.87:1**, and the fill is **4.87:1** against the ground it stands on. Both read off the running app through the cascade. Lost: "amber means pressable", which was a tidy rule that cost the screen the only colour on it.
- **The dealership's name outranks the greeting, by swapping two board bytes rather than inventing a step (2026-09-17).** home B sets `.mark` at 34 and `.greet` at 40, so the built screen made "Good afternoon, Dave Mercer." larger than "Northside Marine" — against the owner's one standing instruction, "I want the logo to be the showpiece thing". The two sizes are the board's own and both stay; their ranking is reversed, at every step of the ladder, so the name is 40 where the greeting is 34, 34 where it is 30, and never second. Lost: the board's own type hierarchy, which the entry critique had already named as "the wrong argument to win on a screen whose owner asked for the logo to be the showpiece"; and a `--text-7xl`, which no picked board draws.
- **Below 1200 the desk comes before the room, and that is Home's one departure from its board's written reflow (2026-09-17).** The board's rule for a hand is two 200px photographs first, then the sheet. Measured on the built screen at 390×844: `THE DESK` arrived at the bottom edge and **New quote** was about 950px down, so nothing a dealer could press was on the first screen — and the same held at 844×390, where the window is 390px tall. On a desk the act is one glance under the fold, which is the fold's whole argument and is kept exactly at 1280, 1440 and 1920; in a hand there is no glance, there is a scroll. The photographs keep their full size and their place: they are second, not smaller, and they are one flick below the act instead of the act being five flicks below them. It is done with `order` on the column the page already is, so the document keeps the board's reading order and the thing that moves — the fold — holds no control at all, which leaves focus order untouched. Measured after: the act's top edge at 248 of 844 in a hand and 239 of 390 on a phone on its side. Lost: the board's own 390 reflow order, and a fold-first phone screen with nothing on it to press.
- **The two heroes are fetched at the width they are drawn, and asked for at the first paint (2026-09-17).** On a cold cache the fold — the first object on the app's main screen — was two dark rectangles for about two seconds. Two causes, both arithmetic: 410,726 and 218,766 bytes of 2,560-wide WebP fetched to be painted 770 and 557 CSS pixels wide, and an `<img>` that was only rendered once the sheet was open, so the request could not even start until the file had been read out of IndexedDB. `tools/seed/hero-widths.ts` resamples each held copy down to 640 and 1280 with the same library and quality that made it, records every copy in the same ledger row with its own bytes and sha256, and `src/data/imagery.test.ts` checks each one is on disk, is the bytes recorded, and is smaller than the picture it came from. The screen joins them into a `srcset`, states a `sizes` per frame, and calls React's `preload` for both before `open` is ever true. Measured at 1440: 122,988 bytes instead of 410,726 and 77,042 instead of 218,766, and in a hand 34,064 and 28,432, and the request starts at the first paint. Lost: one file per hero, and 1.4 MB more in `public/hero-images` so that a browser fetches a third of what it did.
- **A shelf of registers takes a stated reading order, biggest first (2026-09-17).** The seven makers arrived in the file's own order on a first visit and alphabetically out of IndexedDB on every visit after, so the composition of the brightest object on Home was decided by the storage engine while no figure changed. `holdingsOf` now sorts every kind's registers by rows and then by name, the way `KIND_ORDER` already states the order of the six figures. Lost: an order that was a fact about Dexie.
- **`Figure` is for a figure that changes in front of the reader, and Home has exactly one (2026-09-17).** The screen made of counts did not use the count primitive and said nothing about why. It does now, in both directions: the search count goes through `Figure`, because it really does move as a query narrows, and the six kind counts, the draft count and the two row totals stay as text, because they are set once when the file lands — put through NumberFlow they would spin up from zero on arrival, which is the same lie `PriceFigure` exists to refuse, and NumberFlow draws into a shadow root as `role="img"`, so a count a dealer reads across a desk would be announced as a picture. Lost: a silence, and a primitive used because it exists.
- **The quotes register is built from direction A, "The ledger", and is provisional until the owner looks (2026-09-17).** The owner handed the picks over, so this one is chosen rather than voted for, and the reason is the requirement: a Cockpit screen owes eighteen readable rows at 1280×800, and A is the only one of the four whose STRUCTURE is the state — three bands, Draft · Issued · Superseded, each a header of glyph, word and count, so no row spends width on a coloured word and on day one the screen prints three honest zeros instead of an apology. Lost: B "The day book" (grouped by day, which is the question a sales manager asks rather than the one a register answers, and which spends its height on air), C "The stock card" (photograph-led, and about a quarter of the rows have no held picture — a picture belongs to the boat it depicts, so an empty register would be showing stand-ins), D "The one field" (the finding as the screen, which shows no rows at all until somebody types). What each lost direction was right about is kept: the band header carries its own sum from frozen lines (B), the total slot carries a sentence where no figure was decided (C, MarineMax's "Request Pricing" in exactly that place), and the find field takes reference, customer, boat or who prepared it in one line with no facets (D, Stripe's published grammar).
- **Superseded is a band, not a third state, because nothing writes a third state (2026-09-17).** `QuoteState` is `draft | issued` and `model/quote.ts` gives the reason. So `domain/quote/register.ts` derives the third band from the documents themselves — an issued quote that some later draft names in `supersedesId` — which is `live4/shopify-order-statuses-scrolled.png`'s own shape: "Orders are either Open or Archived, but can have additional statuses", each explained in a sentence, which that band header prints. Lost: a third `QuoteState` nothing would have written, and a coloured pill in every row.
- **The find field is in the head, and that is a density decision as much as a composition one (2026-09-17).** A find row of its own costs this register 44px at 1280×800, which is a row and a half of the eighteen it owes; the head is already two lines tall on both sides, so a 36px field sits inside height the screen had already spent. What a narrowing did is said between the head and the register and only while one is on — `marine/marinemax-empty.png` quotes the term back in the results area rather than under the field — and the band headers carry the `n / m` fraction beside it. Measured in Chromium on 2026-09-17: 614px of room for the list at 1280×800, so nineteen 28px rows under three 24px band headers; 22 rows at 1440×900 and 29 at 1920×1080. Lost: a labelled find row, and 44px of a screen whose whole requirement is rows.
- **The register's single-key vocabulary is bound to the grid, not to the window (2026-09-17).** WCAG 2.2 SC 2.1.4 Character Key Shortcuts is Level A and `docs/reference/dense-tables-and-selection.md` records that nobody in the studied cohort meets it — Linear and Superhuman both ship large single-key vocabularies and neither documents remapping or disabling. The criterion's own third exemption is "active only on focus", so every one of J/K, the arrows, Space, Enter, V, N, `/` and Escape is handled on the list element and means nothing anywhere else in the app. The legend under the act says so, and brightens while the register holds the focus. Lost: a window listener, and a remapping panel that would be a screen of its own.
- **A register with nothing in it does not stretch its own frame (2026-09-17).** `live3/github-issues-zero.png` is right that a register at zero keeps its frame, and a frame with 570px of nothing inside it at 1920×1080 is a hole rather than a frame. So while the register is bare the three bands and the act row take the height they need and the room below them is room; the list goes back to filling its track the moment one document is filed, which is the state the eighteen-row requirement is about. Lost: a bordered box two thirds empty on the screen the owner sees first.
- **The quotes register joins `e2e/routes.ts` as the first Cockpit route, and the density ruler it turns on cannot pass on an empty browser (2026-09-17).** Every ruler opens a browser nobody has used, and the app cannot mint a quote until the picker exists, so `density` reads the rows that are there: none. That is the true state of the screen rather than a defect in it, and the label is not softened to `showroom` to collect a green tick in a file whose whole purpose is to stop exactly that. The geometry the requirement is really about is proved instead by `e2e/flows/quotes.spec.ts`, which resolves the register's own `--row-h` and `--band-h` in its own cascade and measures the room the list is given. Lost: a green ruler bought with a wrong word.
- **The picker is built from direction C, "Three tiers at once", and is provisional until the owner looks (2026-09-17).** The owner handed the picks over, so this one is chosen rather than voted for, and the reason is the file: 588 of its 810 hulls are Highfield, and those 588 rows are 67 models in 7 series. The collapse from rows to models IS the problem, so only a design with a third tier can show it without averaging it away. Lost: A "the counted rail" and D "how big, then whose", which both end at a grid of cards where one Highfield card would fold fifteen rows and two prices into a single figure; and B "index and stage", which holds 289 models beautifully down one column and has nowhere to put the two material prices and the seven colourway codes that are the actual choice. What each lost direction was right about is kept: every row of the rail and every series heading carries its true row count (A, Porsche's "All (72)"), the index is type and hairlines with no cards at all (B, Saxdor), and the length, beam and HP envelope are on the plate as a three-fact strip (D's measurement, as Grady-White's own strip).
- **The picker's tiers are the file's, and the rail counts rows (2026-09-17).** Stacer's own site says "over 70 models in 9 ranges" and the file carries 91 rows in 22 series; Highfield's navigation ships ranges the file does not carry and omits one it does. So `fleet.ts` reads `EntityDef.hierarchy` through the domain's own `trailOf` and nothing else: three or more levels collapse onto the trail above the row, two or fewer are one row one model, and a fourth level added tomorrow needs no edit. Lost: any list of brands, ranges or models in app code, and a screen that could disagree with the sheet it was drawn from.
- **A zero is not a price, and the picker says what the cell holds where the figure would be (2026-09-17).** Eighteen rows — all nine Haines Signature and nine of thirty-nine Formosa — hold `0` at every rung, and `money(0)` prints `$0`, which is a figure a salesperson could act on and nobody wrote. The model says "No price at the Cash rung" with the count of rows holding the zero, and the act stays live, because a Haines can be quoted and a person puts the number on it. Lost: a `$0` that reads as free, and a dimmed act on a hull the dealership really sells.
- **The picker asks the material first and the colourway second (2026-09-17).** A Highfield Sport 560 is fifteen rows in two materials and seven or eight colourways; drawing both questions at once put fifteen chips on the plate and pushed the act 300px under it. The material is the one that moves the figure — the sweep measured the price as a function of material alone in 43 of Highfield's 67 models — so it is asked first, and pressing it chooses that material's first row, which makes the figure real and the act live from that moment. Lost: seeing every colourway before narrowing, and a plate whose primary act was never on screen.
- **A colourway code that does not decode is printed as the code it is, and a colourway the file names in English is printed as the word (2026-09-17).** `I`, `O`, `R` and `WH` have no entry in any of the four production decode maps and no legend anywhere in this repository; they are a question for the dealer (`docs/STATUS.md` question 3) and the chip says "not decoded" beside the code. Five Adventure rows say `Sky`, `Dune`, `Mangrove`, `Ocean`, `Polar` — plain English, nothing to decode — and calling those "not decoded" would invent a mystery the sheet has already answered, so `fleet.ts` tells them apart by the file's own code alphabet (capitals joined by hyphens) rather than by whether the decoder happened to fail. Lost: one sentence covering both cases, and a swatch for a colour nobody can name.
- **The picker's register rail is by name, because table order is a fact about Dexie (2026-09-17).** The catalogue store holds the pack's own table order on the visit that reads the file and the repository's — by id, alphabetical — on every visit after, since `fromRepository` re-sorts rows and not tables. Measured in the browser: the rail read Stacer-first on the first open and Formosa-first on the second. Nothing on this sheet records which register comes first, so the rail is alphabetical, which is not a ranking and is the same on the first open and the tenth. Lost: "the file's own order", which this screen cannot be given twice.
- **The picker's rail wraps and never scrolls sideways (2026-09-17).** The sweep's own list of things to avoid has two entries for losing a seven-item row — Zodiac hides seven ranges behind a pager, Highfield puts four across 1440 — and a horizontally scrolling rail at 834 put Stacer and Surtees off the screen. All eight rows, All included, are visible at every width. Lost: a single-line rail on a phone, and 40px of height in a hand.
- **The image ledger rides in the picker's own chunk rather than being fetched (2026-09-17).** `data/northside/images.json` is 309 KB and is the only thing that can answer "do we ship the bytes for this address". Fetching it here would make the picker a second reader of the price file, against the division the whole shell rests on — Entry reads the file, every other screen reads this browser — and would leave a first visit with no network holding a sheet and no pictures for a reason nobody could see. So it is a `?raw` import in the route TanStack already code-splits, parsed once and lazily. Lost: 50 KB gzipped in one route's chunk, to keep one sentence about this app true.
- **`Tile` gains a ground and a shape rather than the picker drawing its own controls (2026-09-17).** A register of 289 models as 289 white rounded cards is a page nobody can scan, and a material or a colourway is a word rather than a surface — but a screen may not reach into a primitive, and `tools/check.ts` refuses a `.ui-` selector outside `src/ui`. So the primitive carries `tone` (`paper` | `room`) and `shape` (`card` | `row` | `chip`), the same move `Button` already makes with `veiled` and `act`, and the selected mark — the sweep's one licensed motion observation, "a short coloured mark travelling along a ladder" — is drawn by the primitive and stilled under reduced motion there. Lost: a hand-rolled button in a screen, and the travelling bar itself, which became a mark that grows in place because the primitive owns what "selected" looks like.
- **Entry's photograph is the FIRST SCREEN's ground, not the window's, and on a phone it is a band of its own (2026-09-17, answering `docs/directions/built-critique.md`).** `position: fixed` is the same thing as `position: absolute` on a window the composition fits exactly and a trap on one it does not: the type scrolls, the picture does not, so a caption composed in the dark quarter can be read two hundred pixels later on lit water. It is absolute and one window tall now, and everything scrolled past it lands on the room's own ground at 14.95:1. At 390 × 844 it stops being the ground altogether and becomes 208 px of picture across the top with the pennant hanging into it: the card is 358 of 390 px wide and the doors fill the rest of the first screen, so a full-bleed photograph had nowhere to be seen, and the deeper phone veil the first build put over it measured 0.0066 of luminance against 0.0068 for no picture at all — the same screen twice. A veil exists so type can be set on a photograph; in a hand the type is not on the photograph, so the veil lifts to 20% and the boat is a boat. Measured after: 0.0409 in the band, and both doors still above 844 (the card 208–583, the doors 603–714 and 726–836). Lost: the board's one-picture-fills-the-window at 390, and the mark's note under the flag there — it joins the provenance below the doors, which is where a sentence about where a wordmark came from belongs on a phone.
- **A caption on a photograph carries its own dark quarter, and the contrast ruler now measures the pixels (2026-09-17).** The built screen's provenance caption measured 4.0–5.5:1 at 1440 × 900 and the gate was green, because `e2e/rulers/measure/contrast.ts` builds its ground by walking `parentElement` for a background COLOUR — and entry's ground is an `<img>` in a sibling layer, so it composited the ancestor chain, reached `--color-ground` and reported 14.95:1. Two fixes, and the second is the one that matters: the panel is drawn over a wash of entry B's own `.veil-flat` value, 30%, shaped as an ellipse with no edge, centred on the caption rather than on the block and bleeding a hundred px past it so the fade happens outside the words (measured after: 5.95:1 at 1280, 6.8 at 1440, 7.2 at 834, 8.3 at 1920); and `measure/contrast.ts` now refuses to answer for a run of text with anything picture-shaped painting under it, handing it to `measure/pixels.ts`, which decodes the screenshot in node and takes the median painted pixel under each 24 px tile as the ground. `fixture.spec.ts` proves it on a picture that is half black and half white: the same pale ink comes back at 17.02:1 and 1.23:1, and the walk itself reports neither. Lost: a ruler that was confident about a ground it had never seen, and the option of fixing the caption by moving it — position cannot answer this, because the caption is as long as the ledger's provenance and a second dealership's photograph is not this one. The real scrim, computed from the picture's own pixels, is still `docs/CUSTOMISATION.md`'s Milestone 4; what makes the interim safe is that the gate can now see it fail.
- **A missing picture costs the picture and nothing else (2026-09-17).** `readEntryFacts` threw when the hero id was not in the ledger, the screen caught it into one sentence, and the pennant hung EMPTY: one absent photograph took the business's name off its own front door, with the packed date, the fingerprint, the mark note and the door's counts. That is exactly the second dealership, whose `heroes-ledger.json` will not carry `stacer-481-seamaster`, and `docs/CUSTOMISATION.md` asks that nothing make them expensive. The hero is now `HeroEntry | null` with a `noPicture` sentence beside it, the panel has three true states (the row, the picture with no row in this file, and no picture), and the flag is never hung empty — while the three small files are in the air there is no flag, and if the manifest cannot be read at all the note says so where the flag would have been. The four washes and the panel's wash are drawn only where `data-picture` says there is one. Lost: a screen that treats a missing photograph as a broken build.
- **A window wider than the photograph is the room, and the picture dissolves into it (2026-09-17).** Never enlarging is the right rule and it had no answer for 1920: the picture was pinned right at its own 1,771 px and left a 149 px strip of bare ground down the left with a hard seam — measured (1,11,19) → (25,33,41) across one pixel. It is centred now, and its two vertical edges fade out over exactly the width of the room they left over, 74 px each side at 1920 and 96 at 2560: measured across the same edge, 0.0030 → 0.0159 of luminance over 75 px, with no step in it. Lost: a mirrored edge and a second hero from the ledger's other seven, both of which answer the same question by adding a picture nobody chose.
- **The step list ticks in the water the composition leaves empty, rather than in the flow (2026-09-17).** Pressing the blue door added a refusal under each door and a two-item step list, 80 px in a page that had 28 px of slack: 900 became 952, a scrollbar appeared on the one screen that fits exactly, and the foot's second line went under the window for the ~400 ms the read takes. Reserving the room in the flow was tried and measured first — it cost the board's own geometry, moving the card up nine pixels and the foot down twenty-four, at rest, for a state that lasts four tenths of a second. So the list is taken out of the flow at 1200 and up and put under the blank door opposite the foot sentence, where the board itself leaves 450 to 790 px of nothing. Measured: 900 before the press and 900 while it reads. Lost: a reserved slot, and the refusals moving with it — they stay under the doors they belong to, where a reader looking at a door is owed the reason.
- **The configurator is built from direction B, "Stage and rail", and is provisional until the owner looks (2026-09-17).** The owner handed the picks over, so this one is chosen rather than voted for, and the reason is the only instruction he has ever given about this screen: "More like Porsche the right side, not less. STUDY IT." B is the only direction of the four that IS the Porsche right side — one rail whose first element is a search over every pairing on the quote, then a card per chapter, four row shapes for four kinds of content, the delta in the head — and it is the only one that reaches the 588-variant hull chapter or the 2,937-row parts table without a second screen, which is the only place on this file where scale is real (`research/refs/configurator/notes.md` §0). Lost: A "six heads, one open", a flat register with no stage, which is the shape the owner has already called a database twice; C "one chapter, one window", strictly sequential, which is a brochure's flow and not a dealer's — a salesperson with a customer at the desk jumps to the chapter being argued about; D "the document, being written", the best idea in the sweep and the DOCUMENT screen's, where the same table is the thing itself rather than a picture of it. What each lost direction was right about is kept: every chapter head states its own answer and its own subtotal while shut, so the whole build reads in six lines before anything is opened (A, Herman Miller); the recommended build really is already assembled, because `mintQuote` brings the starred motor and the starred trailer across at mint (C, Saxdor); and every empty price cell carries an em-dash rather than a blank or a nought (D, PCPartPicker).
- **The delta on every row is what the PRESS does, not what a radio group would do (2026-09-17).** The sweep's §2 asks for Whaler's reading — the fitted 40hp at `$0`, the 25hp at `−$853` — and that is a radio group's arithmetic. Our chapters are not radio groups: `addLine` adds and `removeLine` removes, a section holds as many lines as a dealer puts on it, and on this file `Highfield ADV7` slots 4–9 are six pairings of the SAME motor told apart by six rigging kits. A screen printing `−$853` beside a row whose press would ADD a second motor would be printing a figure about a press nobody can make. So the delta is kept and its meaning is the press: `weighPick` asks the one summation what the document would total with this row on — or off, for a row already on it, which is why a fitted row's figure is negative. Lost: the frame's own reading, and a chapter that could pretend to be exclusive when the engine underneath it is not.
- **The rung is printed as a fact and the switch is left to the cascade (2026-09-17).** §2 names "a global switch on a closed band head" and says ours is the price rung. Moving a whole quote from Cash to Trade re-prices every line, and `levelConflict` in the engine already computes what that costs line by line, which line holds and why — that is the CASCADE SHEET, screen 5, with its own sweep and its own directions, and the sweep itself forbids drawing it from a frame. So this screen prints which rung the document is on and says where the switch will live. Lost: a switch that would have been the cascade with no sheet behind it.
- **The way back is on the screen, not in a toast (2026-09-17).** `src/ui/Toaster` exists and nothing in this app mounts it. Rather than mount an app-level singleton from inside one screen, the last step and its way back stand in the rail's head, where every press on this screen can see them: the sentence is the command's own `said`, the press is pinned to that command's event id — so a stale press is refused with the store's sentence rather than undoing something else — and after a way back the offer becomes "Put it back". A toast that vanishes after eight seconds is also a way back a person cannot reach with a keyboard while reading a list of forty motors. Lost: rule 9's toast, until something mounts the Toaster.
- **A frozen line carries the code a dealer orders by (2026-09-17, a change to the engine).** §2 asks for "the dealer's own code beside every name" — Porsche's `0Q`, `58X`, `1G8` — and a frozen line could not say it: it carried the label (prose), the figure, the column the figure came from and the workbook cell it was read from, and nothing a supplier would recognise on a phone call. `QuoteLine.code` is frozen by `mintLine`, read by name exactly as the `Source` cell already is, from the four spellings this file uses — `Model Code` on hulls and motors, `Code` on trailers, parts and packages, `Part Number` on rigging kits — most specific first, so `Dealer Fit Packages`, which names two columns `Code`, gives the package's and not the accessory's. Measured: the hull's code is not in the hull's label, so it is news rather than a repetition. Lost: nothing; it is absent where a table has no such column, which is a real state and never an empty string.
- **`finishLevels` was returning the empty set for every table on this dealer's file, silently (2026-09-17).** `leafValues` writes a key of `tableId:rowId` and `finishLevels` read the table back out with `slice(0, lastIndexOf(':'))` — correct only while a ROW id carries no colon. Every row id off `data/northside/` is `boat_highfield:483`, so the key held two colons and the table came back as `boat_highfield:boat_highfield`, matching nothing; a caller reads that as "no table groups by a finish" and quietly stops grouping. It had never bitten because nothing in the app called it — the barrel publishes `foldModels`, `modelOf` and `priceOf` and not this — and its own suite passes fabricated row ids with no colon in them. The configurator's chapter 01 is its first real caller. The key is matched by its prefix now, and `fold.test.ts` carries the real pack's own shape as a case, plus one proving two tables whose ids share a stem stay apart. Lost: a guard that had only ever been exercised on data the app does not have.
- **The configurator's rail comes before its stage in a hand AND in any window shorter than 700 (2026-09-17).** Measured at 390×844: the masthead is 138px of sticky and the stage 656px, so the search field — which IS the navigation on this screen — sat 822px down an 844px window, and nothing a dealer could type was on the first screen. Measured at 844×390: that width is past the 834 step so it takes the tablet layout, where the stage is a band, and a band 493px tall in a 390px window put the field 602px down. The question was never "how wide is this", it is "does the boat fit above the work", and a short window is the other way of answering no — the picker's own ladder makes the same kind of rule, its fixed-height room applying only above 1200px AND above 700px of window. The stage keeps its shape at each width and only its PLACE moves, with `order` on the floor, so document order and focus order are untouched; there is nothing in the stage to focus, and the masthead already carries the boat's name and the running total. Measured after: the field at 154 of 844 and at 129 of 390. Lost: the composition's own order below 640, which Home's ladder gave up for the same reason on the same day.
- **A render is fitted and only a photograph is cropped (2026-09-17).** The SP560's own picture is a top-down studio render on a white ground, and `object-fit: cover` filled 432×243 of a dark room with a bleeding white rectangle that had both ends of the boat cut off. Cropping a photograph taken on the water loses water; cropping a render loses the product. The packer already measured which is which and `images.json` carries the verdict per address, so the box reads it and a render is fitted with air around it. Lost: one treatment for every picture, and the pretence that a catalogue render is a scene.
- **One reason said once, many reasons said on the row (2026-09-17).** Searching "battery" on an SP560 reaches 78 rows of `Dealer Fit Packages`, and every one of them carried the same 121-character sentence — so forty identical paragraphs were drawn between forty rows and the sentence a person needed to read once became the largest thing in the chapter. It is the curated case, where the reason is a fact about the LIST rather than about any row in it: the price file never wrote the pairing down. Where a measured rule does the rejecting, each row carries its own figures on both sides of its own clause, they differ, and rule 10 puts each one on the row it is about. The test is mechanical — identical text on every row off the shortlist, and more than one of them — and nothing is ever dropped: it is either above the list once or on each row. Lost: rule 10 read as "always on the row", which on this file's commonest case is forty copies of one sentence.
- **The search counts what it SELECTED and not what it drew (2026-09-17).** Measured on an SP560 for "battery", the field read "81 rows carry those words — 170 of them the shortlist was standing in front of", because the screen counted the rows it had DRAWN (capped at forty per chapter by `OFFER_CAP`) against a `beyond` that `stepOffer` computes over the whole selection. 170 of 81 is not a sentence anybody can believe. Both figures are the selection now, and where the cap has trimmed what is on screen the sentence says so. Lost: a figure that was true of the list and read as a figure about the file.
- **A counted rail's denominator is what its own switch would show (2026-09-17).** The counts line read "4 of 1,777 offered" over a control reading "Show all 1,576", so a dealer had two totals for one question and no way to reconcile them: 1,777 is the whole table and 1,576 is what is still sold. The denominator is the live table now, which is what the switch reaches, and the rows no longer sold are accounted for in the sentence beside the switch. Lost: the pool as the headline figure, which `freeze.ts` is right to return and which is not the number a person compares a control against.
- **A chapter with no subtotal says WHICH kind of no, in the register's own two words (2026-09-17).** A chapter with nothing on it has not been answered; a chapter whose lines all carry no figure has, and the price file prices none of them. Those are different facts and `domain/quote/register.ts` already names both — `NOTHING_ON_IT` and `NOT_PRICED` — so the configurator reads them rather than inventing a third phrasing for a state the quotes register already describes. The handover is neither: it is a question about a person and owes no figure at all, so its cell is empty rather than carrying a word about money. Lost: one word ("not priced") standing for two states and for a chapter that has no price by nature.
- **The cascade is built from direction B, "Because", and is provisional until the owner looks (2026-09-17).** The owner handed the picks over, so this one is chosen rather than voted for, and the reason is the brief: a causal story, not a list. B is the only one of the four ORDERED BY CAUSE. Measured on a Highfield SP560 with eight lines moved from Cash to Trade, the engine gives three changed and five held — two cards grouped by verb, FOUR grouped by cause, and the four are the dealer's own column names (`now priced at Trade`, `now priced at Trade Price`) and the two ways a table can fail to carry a rung (`no Trade column — stays at Sell inc Rego`, `no price column on this table`). Grouping by verb throws that away and is exactly how Porsche ends up with five removed rows reading one sentence that names neither the selection nor what about it. Lost: A "the plan", which leads with a census and groups by verb, and needs a filter the moment a cascade runs long; C "in the document", the best idea in the sweep and the DOCUMENT screen's, where the frozen lines are the thing itself rather than a picture of it; D "the two builds", which on a rung move prints forty lines twice to show that three of them differ. What each lost direction was right about is kept: what stays is counted off the frozen lines and said in a sentence (A and D, and never from a `stays` field the contract does not have), the change is crossed out where it dies (C, Google Docs), and the alternatives are priced as the file prices them with the cheapest first (D, Whaler).
- **The rung's switch is the cascade, and the configurator's refusal is retired by having built it (2026-09-17, superseding the configurator's own "the switch is left to the cascade").** `/quote/$id/cascade?fix=level:<key>&from=<chapter>`. The configurator prints the rung the document is on with the engine's own count of the lines that carry it, and every other rung is a press that NAVIGATES and writes nothing. Production re-prices instantly and silently and loses the level on save; ours now shows what the move costs line by line, with the reason each line gives, and the committed total does not move until Accept. Lost: the sentence that said the sheet was not built yet.
- **`fitmentCascade` has a caller, and its own header's reason for having none is out of date (2026-09-17).** It was written for "what does this subject do to the partners already on the quote" and recorded, at length, that the event could not fire because `rootRowId` is written once at creation. `refinishSubject` re-roots a standing quote onto another row of the same register — that IS the event — so a finish that moves the total opens `?fix=finish:<rowId>`, where the hull's own from→to sits beside whatever the fitment reading makes of the trailers already on the document. Measured on this file: the reading returns no removals for any finish of any model, because a marque is a property of the model and every finish shares it; what it does return is the one thing the floor cannot check at all, in its own words, on the subject it is about. Lost: the argument for keeping an uncalled export, which is now just a called one.
- **A finish that moves the total is decided on the sheet; one that moves nothing is applied in place (2026-09-17).** Seven of the SP560's fifteen finishes cost exactly what the one on the quote costs, and `src/domain/quote/cascade.ts` refuses to build a sheet with nothing to decide on it — "a sheet that opens to say nothing happens is a full stop in the middle of somebody's work". So the press routes on the figure the row is already printing. Lost: one behaviour for every finish row, and eight sheets a dealer would have had to dismiss to recolour a boat.
- **Accepting does not navigate away, and declining writes nothing (2026-09-17).** A decision taken is not a decision forgotten: the sheet keeps its cards and becomes the record of what went onto the document, with the step's own sentence, the document's own total asked of the engine afterwards, and the way back pinned to the events the commands minted.
