That also answers the failure §5 counts on both Porsche's sheet and our own old one — 150–400px of dead space under the last card — because the decision block is pushed to the end of the sheet rather than stretched or pinned to the window, and the space a short cascade leaves sits between the reading and the act. Declining is a navigation and nothing else: the sheet never touched the document, so there is nothing to restore, which is the half of Porsche's three-part address (`feas-return=`) we do not need. Lost: a toast, and a redirect on Accept.
- **The ground is drawn at the pixels the ledger holds and dissolves into the room (2026-09-17).** The first cut used `object-fit: cover`, and at 1440×900 that scaled the SP560's 1,100×619 held copy to 1,600×900 — half as large again as the bytes on disk, on a screen whose every other picture is capped at what the ledger holds. The picture is now sized intrinsically with `max-*: 100%`, so the box can crop the measure and never stretch the picture, and the blur's own feathered edge is the treatment rather than a halo to hide: it runs off the start edge of the window and under the sheet, the way entry B spends the room its 1920 window has left over. The veil deepens TOWARDS the sheet and starts at entry B's 20% wash where the picture is a band beside the reading and three stops deeper where it is the ground beneath it. Lost: a picture that filled its box, and `scale()`, which was hiding the enlargement rather than fixing it.
- **The document is built from direction A, "The sheet, at true size", and is provisional until the owner looks (2026-09-17).** The owner handed the picks over, so this one is chosen rather than voted for, and the reason is the requirement itself: this screen's job is not "show the quote", it is that screen and paper cannot be allowed to disagree — and A is the only direction of the four where that is STRUCTURAL rather than promised. The A4 page is the object on screen, at 1:1 wherever the window allows, standing on the same dark floor the rest of the app is drawn in; print is the same nodes with the floor taken away. Lost: B "the rail and the record", which is the configurator's own shape a second time and would have made the document a second editor; C "the ledger", which is the quotes register's shape a second time, and the owner has called that shape a database twice; D "the brochure", which puts money LAST and therefore loses "the price beside who it is for", and which has to survive a render this repository cannot supply — Highfield's 115 held catalogue pictures include zero scenes. What each lost direction was right about is kept: the band head carries its own count and subtotal (B, Qwilr), the frozen line sits beside the rung it was frozen at (B), and the unpriced half is a document inside the document rather than a table of blanks (C, `porsche-pdf-5`).
- **The page assignment is measured and the page breaks are ours, because a browser will not tell a screen where a page ends (2026-09-17).** CSS `break-inside: avoid` tells a PRINTER where not to break and tells a scrolling screen nothing, so a screen that draws page boxes has to know the answer itself. `src/screens/document/paginate.ts` packs measured atoms — a table row, a heading, a paragraph — into pages of the page's own content box, never splitting one, with a `keepWithNext` that stops a section head being left alone at the foot. That is "page breaks fall between sections, not through a table row" made structural: rows are atoms, so a break can only fall between two of them. Each page is its own element with `break-after: page`, so the printed page count IS the number of elements and `e2e/flows/document.spec.ts` asserts a real A4 PDF against it. Sections FLOW rather than each starting a fresh page, which is `porsche-pdf-3` avoided on purpose — three rows on an otherwise empty A4. Lost: CSS doing the pagination, and with it any way for the sheets on the floor to be a different count from the sheets in the tray.
- **The measuring pass is the reflow ladder switched off, rather than a second statement of the A4 shape (2026-09-17).** Print is always A4 whatever the window was, so the page assignment has to be measured in the paper's own geometry at every width — including 390, where the paper leaves and the same nodes set as one column. The first cut restated the A4 geometry under `[data-gauging]`, which made three copies of it (base, gauging, print) and they drifted within the hour: the gauge stayed `display: none` from the narrow ladder, `clientHeight` came back 0, and on a phone the document never paginated at all. Every selector in the two narrow media blocks is written `.doc:not([data-gauging])` now, so gauging is the ladder absent and there is exactly one A4 shape in the file. Lost: a `[data-gauging]` block that looked like belt and braces and was a second source of truth.
- **The paper is shown at true size or it is not shown as paper (2026-09-17).** 210 mm is 793.7 CSS px, so below 826 a sheet could only be shown by scaling it — and at 390 that is a scale of 0.45, which puts this app's own 11 px floor at 6 px and makes the document a picture of a document. Under 826 the page BOXES leave and the same nodes, in the same order, at the same reading sizes, set as one column; each group keeps the page number it will be printed on, so a person in a hand still knows where a break falls. Lost: "one object, one size" at every width, which cannot be had honestly, and a phone view that would have been unreadable if it had been.
- **`Included`, `Optional` and `Not priced at this level` are three facts and the money column carries only the words (2026-09-17).** The sweep measured Porsche printing the same `Basic equipment` chip mid green on screen and neutral grey in the PDF of the identical configuration — green reads included, grey reads inert, and the meaning died in the printer. So each state is a WORD and a hue may only follow it. `src/domain/quote/document.ts` decides which: a frozen `unitPrice` of 0 is `Included` (the file states a charge of nothing), `null` is `Not priced at this level` (`model/quote.ts`'s "a REAL state, never rendered as 0"), and `Optional` is a fact about a SECTION and never about a line — every line on a frozen document is charged or included by construction, and what is optional is what the register offered and nobody took, counted from the `pickedCount` the mint froze. The reason a cell says one of the two goes UNDER THE NAME with the rest of the line's provenance: `Not priced at this level — the register carries no price column` wrapped to three lines in a 16-character column and one row stood three times the height of its neighbours. Lost: the reason living in the money column, and a fourth phrasing for a state the same three words already name.
- **The document never reads the catalogue, and its route does not even offer to (2026-09-17).** Every other screen calls `catalogue.load(repository)` on arrival. `src/routes/quote.$id_.document.tsx` deliberately does not, and that absence is the screen's whole promise: `readDocument` takes one argument and it is the frozen quote, the band order comes off `QuoteChapter.kind` which the mint froze rather than off `sectionKinds` which reads the sheet, and `Document.test.tsx` renders the screen once, empties the catalogue store, renders it again and asserts the text is identical. `invariants.test.ts` makes that promise about the engine; a route that quietly loaded the sheet here would have left it true of the engine and untrue of the app. Lost: nothing, because there was nothing to read.
- **A mark on paper needs dark ink, so the document walks its own picture ladder (2026-09-17).** The configurator's stage asks the marks ledger for the WHITE variant, because it draws in a dark room; a white wordmark on this page is invisible. `marks-ledger.json` holds five brands in dark and white, six dark only, and Mercury in white ink only — so on paper Mercury is a word, and `src/screens/document/art.ts` says so in the caption rather than leaving a hole. The cover picture is a BAND and never a bleed, and that is arithmetic: A4 at 300 dpi is 2480 × 3508 px and not one held picture in this repository is 3508 px tall, so a full-page cover would be an enlargement. And the dealer's own mark is not in the ledger at all, which bites hardest here because a quote is letterhead: the business's name is set in type, the provenance line says so, and `docs/CUSTOMISATION.md` layer 2 is where one gets uploaded. Lost: importing the configurator's `stage.ts`, which would have shipped the wrong ink.
- **A screen built in parallel leaves a refusal that rots, so every build now owes a re-read pass.** Measured 2026-09-17 by the end-to-end verification: seven screens were built at once, each correctly refusing with a sentence naming the screen that did not exist yet — and then those screens were built and nobody went back. Home's "New quote" still said the picker was not built; the register's "Open it" said the document had no screen; the configurator's finale stranded a dealer after issuing. The app contained zero links and Home had one live control. The sentences were honest when written and false when read, which is worse than a dead button because it reads as considered. Every future parallel build ends with one agent whose only job is to re-read every `refusedBecause` in the tree against what is now in it. Lost: assuming a true sentence stays true.
- **The rail is the fixed measure and the boat takes what is left (2026-09-18).** "Stage and rail" had no stage: measured at 1920, `.cfg-stage` was 512px of 1,920 (27%) and the hull inside it 412 x 232 from a held copy of 1,100 x 619 — 2.6% of the screen — while the rail stretched and 150px of ground sat unused beside it, on a direction whose own words are "the boat fills two thirds" and a screen whose only note from the owner is "More like Porsche the right side, not less. STUDY IT." Porsche's left side is a car at full bleed against a rail of FIXED width, and that is the inversion taken: a chapter row has one right line length — name, facts, code and delta on one line — and it is 704px at every desk width, so every pixel a wider window has left over goes to the hull, which is the one thing on the screen that gets better the larger it is. The plate the boat is cut from bleeds off the start edge of the window and its facts keep the gutter it gave up, so they sit on the same vertical as the masthead's name. Measured after, at 1920: the stage 1,041 of 1,905 (54%), the boat 1,041 x 448 (22% of the screen), the rail 704, no horizontal overflow at any of the six ruler widths. Lost: a rail that grew with the window, and the 112rem cap that centred the whole floor.
- **The configurator's stage draws the model's own photograph where the hero ledger holds one (2026-09-18).** The stage had been reading `images.json`, the catalogue tier, capped at long edge 1100 — on the SP560 a top-down studio render on a white ground. `heroes-ledger.json` holds eight photographs resampled to 2560 FOR A STAGE, each with the register and the model it depicts, and `tools/seed/pick-heroes.ts` takes only what `verdict.judge` calls a scene and prints "every candidate judged a studio shot" where there is none — so a hero is a scene by construction and is cropped, while the catalogue tier keeps its per-row verdict and a render is still fitted with air around it. The ladder is now photograph-on-the-water → catalogue copy → maker's mark → name in type, matched on the REGISTER and the MODEL read out of the root row's own hierarchy rather than on the label, which carries the finish and the colourway as well. Measured on the SP560 at 1920: 2,560 x 1,708 held, drawn 1,041 x 695, never enlarged, served through the ledger's own 640/1280/2560 `srcset`. A hull with no hero draws exactly what it drew before. Lost: one tier for every stage, and the argument that a stage this small was all the pack could honestly offer.
- **One reason said once above a list is a primitive's job, not a screen's (2026-09-18).** Issuing a quote printed `ISSUED_REFUSAL` five times on one open chapter — four of them 58.5px tall wedged BETWEEN two rows, so each read as though it belonged to the row beneath — and `Show all 209 in Yamaha Outboards` would have made it 209. The same screen already held the right pattern one chapter above, where the fitment refusal says its reason once with every row still live. `Tile` and `Button` gained `refusedBy`, the id of the element the screen printed the sentence in: the control is still dimmed, still focusable, still swallows the press, and `aria-describedby` still points a reader at the reason. Measured after, with the whole 209-row table open: 41 refused controls, 2 copies of the sentence — one per list, the rung list and the open chapter — and every one of the 41 describable. Lost: `refusedBecause` as the only way to refuse, and a screen reaching into `.ui-refusal` to hide the copies.
- **Pressing a second motor still fits a second motor, and now says so (2026-09-18).** Measured on an SP560 with `F90XB` fitted: pressing `F115XB` took the total from $66,584 to $83,453 and the motor subtotal to $31,400, with nothing on the screen saying a 5.66 m RIB now had two outboards on it. Making the chapter a radio group was refused: `addLine` adds, a section holds as many lines as a dealer puts on it, and on this file `Highfield ADV7` slots 4–9 are six pairings of the SAME motor told apart by six rigging kits — a dealer may legitimately quote two batteries or two trailers. So `severalOnStepSentence` sits in `domain/quote/steps.ts` beside the command whose behaviour it describes, and the configurator draws it above the rows in the act's own colour, which is the treatment the finale's double-charge flag already uses for evidence that is not a refusal. Lost: a screen that was silent about the one press that can double a quote.
- **The recommendation is named in text and the star is gone (2026-09-18).** §4 of the sweep measured Saxdor, Apple, Whaler and Porsche and concluded "None uses a star, a ribbon or a colour": the substance was already right here, because `mintQuote` brings the starred motor and the starred trailer across at mint so the recommended row is the row already on the quote when the chapter opens — and then a ★ rode on the row anyway, the one treatment every reference was measured avoiding. The table head says "The price file recommends Yamaha - F90XB for this hull." and the row keeps ", recommended by the price file" in its accessible name. Lost: the glyph, and nothing else.
- **A sticky bar carries its own ground upwards rather than guessing the bar above it (2026-09-18).** `.cfg-mast` ended at 108.69 and `.cfg-find` began at 112, both sticky and both opaque, so a 3.31px band of the rows scrolling underneath was painted between them. The offset cannot be rounded down: a masthead's height is its content's, and this one grows a line when a quote is issued. So the field paints 24px of ground above its own box, which at rest sits inside the floor's top padding and is invisible, and while the field is stuck covers the gap under the masthead's higher z-index. Measured after, scrolled to y=400: every row from y=109 to y=114 is one colour across the rail's full width. Lost: a magic offset that was right for one masthead.
- **The act of selling leads to the thing you hand over (2026-09-18).** `Give it to the customer` printed, permanently, "the document is the next screen of this milestone and it is not built yet" — so the one irreversible act in this app issued the quote correctly and then stranded the dealer on a read-only screen whose only onward control was `Make a new version`. The document is built at `/quote/$id/document`, so the refusal is retired the way the picker's and the cascade's were, by having built the thing: `Open the document` is the finale's act with `Make a new version` quieter beneath it, the masthead carries the same control on any chapter of an issued quote, and an issued quote with no chapter in its address opens the finale rather than the first outstanding decision, because on an issued document there are none. A DRAFT is offered no such control at all: a document renders from frozen lines and a draft's are still moving, so this is a control that appears when there is something to open rather than one that stands refused. Lost: a true sentence that had gone false, which `docs/DECISIONS.md` already records as the cost of building seven screens in parallel.

- **Home's four controls go where they say they go, and the three refusals are deleted (2026-09-18).** The critique measured the blocker in one line — "a dealer who lands on Home cannot start a quote, cannot reach the register, and cannot reach the drafts the same screen is counting" — and every address involved already existed. `New quote` opens `/quote/new`, each plate opens `/quote/new?brand=<that register's id>`, `All quotes` opens `/quotes`, and the card opens the document it drew. Nothing was built to make them work: four props were added to `HomeProps`, four navigations to `src/routes/index.tsx`, and `NO_PICKER` plus the film line's "neither plate opens yet" plus "the register that lists them is not built yet" were removed. Lost: keeping the sentences until some later milestone, which is how a refusal about an unbuilt screen outlives the screen it was about by a day.
- **A refusal is a thing to delete, not a thing to update (2026-09-18).** Three of the four false sentences on Home named a screen that did not exist when they were written. The rule that follows: the day a screen is built, the branch that refuses on its behalf comes out with it, and the test that asserted the apology asserts the arrival instead. Lost: a "provisional copy" register nobody would have read.
- **Home's act keeps the live amber it was given, and the quiet step stays for a refusal that is real (2026-09-18).** `--color-act-refused` was added the day before precisely because Home's one act was refused for a whole milestone; with the picker reachable it is the live `--color-act` at 8.21:1, and the token keeps its job for an act that genuinely cannot act. Lost: deleting the third amber step now that nothing on Home uses it.
- **A filed document is drawn as the empty card's own shape, filled — not as a register row (2026-09-18).** The board drew a split record card with every region named, and the first build printed that diagram over a real draft under the words "when one does, it lands in this card". Now the newest document lands in it: photograph on the left at 112 × 84, the band and the reference, the boat as the document froze it, who it is for, and the frozen total with the rung it is priced at along the foot. Lost: copying the quotes register's row into Home (a treatment stamped from another screen, which this repo refuses) and drawing a list of every draft (the register is one press away and this column has 390 px of a 900 px window).
- **The card's photograph is the ledger's or nothing, and the well says which (2026-09-18).** `pictureForSubject` answers only for the exact model, in the exact register, with the model standing as its own word in the frozen label — so `PA600` never answers for `PA600X`. Where there is no answer the well keeps its size and says "No photograph held for this model". Lost: importing the 4,768-row image ledger into Home's chunk to resolve the maker's address on a frozen line (309 KB on the screen whose first two seconds the same critique had just been about), and resemblance matching of any kind.
- **`Tile` only claims to be a toggle when it is one (2026-09-18).** `selected` defaulted to `false`, so every tile wrote `aria-pressed`; Home's card is the first that OPENS something rather than choosing between things, and an unpressed toggle promises a state it never enters. Left out, the attribute is not written. Lost: a `role`-shaped workaround in the screen, and a second card primitive.

- **The register opens what it lists, and it says which of the two things it is opening (2026-09-18).** The critique's blocker was not a stale string: `QuotesProps` declared no `openDocument` and no `newQuote` at all, and `src/routes/quotes.tsx` handed the screen only `goHome` and `openTheFile`, so `Open it` and `New quote` carried `aria-disabled` and the version the register had just minted could not be opened by the screen that minted it. Both addresses existed. One prop carries the seam — `openQuote(id, state)`, the shape Home settled the day before — because WHICH address a press lands on is a fact about the document and belongs to the route: a draft opens at `/quote/$id` where it can still be changed, an issued or superseded one at `/quote/$id/document` as the paper the customer holds. The control is named for what it opens (`Open the build` · `Open the document`) with the sentence of what that press does underneath, so nobody presses it to find out. Lost: one generic `Open it` and the two sentences saying those screens did not exist, deleted rather than reworded.
- **`Enter` opens the document and `Space` still peeks (2026-09-18).** Enter used to open the PANEL, which was the honest thing to do while opening was refused — the refusal was drawn under the control Enter was printed on. With somewhere to go, the key printed on a control does what the control does, and the sweep's own distinction survives whole: Space is "let me see", Enter is "let me read", and a second press on the row is Enter for a pointer. Lost: Enter as a second way to peek, which Space already was.
- **One amber on the screen, on whatever the screen is for at that moment (2026-09-18).** `New quote` at the foot of the register is the live `--color-act` while nothing is open, and steps down to `veiled` the moment a document is under the cursor — where `Open the …` takes the amber, because a person who has found their quote came here to open it. `Make a new version` gave up the amber it held on an issued quote. Lost: a fixed owner of the act colour, and the two ambers arguing across a 1440px screen that having a live act at both ends would have produced.
- **Neither frame is stretched to the window, at any width (2026-09-18).** `quotes.css` already argued that "a frame with 570px of nothing inside it is not a frame, it is a hole" and applied it only to the BARE register; the critique then measured the same hole with rows in it — six rows and roughly 750px of empty ledger at 1920x1080, the act row stranded at the bottom of it. The ledger is a flex column now: the list takes the height of its rows and scrolls only when they exceed the room, and the act follows the last row, which is where `live4/mercury-banking.png` puts "+ Create Account". Measured at 1920 with five documents filed: last row bottom 282, act top 282. The ROOM the list is given is unchanged — the body's own track — so the density arithmetic is the same number whether the register is full or empty. Lost: a bordered box the height of the window with nothing in most of it.
- **A key is not printed where there is no key to press (2026-09-18).** The `J K Space Enter Esc` legend and the find field's `/` are drawn under `pointer: fine` and taken away under `pointer: coarse`, and the panel's two keyboard paragraphs are replaced by the touch sentence rather than deleted — so a phone reads "press a row to read it here; the act at the foot of this panel opens it", which is true of a finger, and a 390px window on a desk keeps its keys. The touch sentence deliberately does not offer the double press: a double tap is the browser's own zoom gesture. Lost: a width breakpoint, which would have taught a tablet with a keyboard nothing and a small desktop window a lie.

- **The plate is crest, body and foot, and only the body scrolls (2026-09-18).** The picker's whole plate was one scroller — 1,093px of panel in a 692px scrollport — so at 1440 x 900 `Start the quote` stood at top 857 with nine pixels sliced, and choosing a material, which is the press that MAKES the act live, moved it to 1119: 219px below the window, and at 1920 x 1080 still below an 1,080px one. The page could not be scrolled to it, because the page was the window and the overflow was inside a panel. A floating bottom bar was refused on sight — the owner has called that shape disgusting — so the plate took the shape the sweep had already found in `picker/surtees-770.png`, a strip of facts with the act at its end: a CREST that holds the register and the hull's name still, a BODY that scrolls through the picture, the spec strip and the chips, and a FOOT that holds the figure and the act. The figure came down into the foot with the act, because the material chips are what move it and a dealer pressing HYP used to watch a number change above the fold of the panel he was reading. Measured after, on the SP560 with a row chosen: the act at 661–713 in 800px of window, 761–813 in 900, 941–993 in 1,080, and static at 1,362 of a 1,513px page at 390 where the flow was already right. Lost: the plate as one continuous read, and the money block's own washed box, which became the foot's.
- **A column anchors its notes to its own bottom edge rather than trailing off (2026-09-18).** At 1920 x 1080 the register rail ended around y=590 and left roughly 480px of dead ground under it, so the left column read as a list that had been cut off; the plate did the same at the arrival state. The rail's rows now take the top of the column and its notes the bottom, and a plate with no act spreads its figures and its provenance to the two edges the same way. What the file HOLDS BACK moved out of the middle column and under the rail while this was done, because it is a sentence about registers and their rows, and under a search that narrows the list to one name it used to hang alone in 600px of nothing. Lost: filling the room with furniture, which would have meant inventing something to say.
- **Three words on the picker that were counts have been counted (2026-09-18).** "Nothing in the seven registers is called that", "1 of 67 models carry those words" and "Six of these registers file one row per model" — a typed figure, a missing singular and a second typed figure, on a screen whose own header says not one number on it is typed. They read `fleet.brands.length`, the match count, and the registers whose rows and models are the same number. A second dealership with six registers would have been told there were seven. Lost: nothing; they were wrong.

- **The cascade's ground is a scene or it is the room, and the catalogue copy is drawn sharp on a plate (2026-09-18).** The critique measured the cost of blurring whatever picture the quote's row happened to point at: `.csc-sheet` began at x=542 of 1,425 at 1440 (38%) and x=881 of 1,905 at 1920 (46%), and all of it was a near-black smear — because 207 of the 453 rows in `images.json` are studio renders CUT OUT ON WHITE, and a render cut out on white is a technical drawing of a boat. No veil turns a drawing into a room; "Porsche's blurred stage works because there is a car in a scene behind it." So the blur is spent on the HERO tier, `heroes-ledger.json`'s eight photographs at 2,560 on the long edge, matched on the register and the model read out of the root row's own hierarchy — the only pictures in this repository that `object-fit: cover` can fill a 1,920 window with and still shrink (measured cover scale 0.670 at 1920, 0.631 at 1440, 0.351 at 834, 0.254 at 390). Where the ledger holds no photograph of the model there is no ground at all and the sheet stands on the room, which is §7's own requirement that this direction draw itself once on a flat ground. Lost: one picture doing two jobs, and the argument that a blurred catalogue copy was the honest use of 1,100 px.
- **The left of the cascade is the build, as it stands, and the words are never on the photograph (2026-09-18).** The sheet's whole promise is "the committed total does not move until you accept" and the figure it promised not to move was nowhere on the screen. It is now: the row's own catalogue copy drawn SHARP on a white mount at its own pixels (486 × 273 of a held 1,100 × 619 at 1920), the boat's name, `quoteTotals`' own total and the count of the frozen lines — so accepting moves that figure and a reader watches the committed total become the proposed one. The card under it is opaque `--color-panel`, not glass over the picture, because entry's own blocker was a 12 px caption at 4.1 : 1 on the water it was really drawn on and 11 px of provenance over a blurred scene is the identical pair. The mount is white and not `--color-paper` because the render's own ground is `#ffffff` and a warm mount would show as a rectangle behind the boat; a picture the ledger measured as a `scene` gets no mount at all. Lost: the provenance living at the foot of the sheet, four hundred pixels away from the two pictures it is about.
- **A photograph of the model is named as the model, never as the colourway (2026-09-18).** `highfield-sp560` is an SP560 on the water; this document is written against `Highfield - SP560 (PVC) W-W-WB`, and a photograph behind a sheet could imply a finish nobody bought. The provenance says it in words — "the SP560, and not the colourway on this document" — beside the plate, which IS the exact row. Lost: letting a picture stand for more than it depicts, which CLAUDE.md already forbids and which only a sentence can prevent when two tiers of picture are on one screen.
- **`totalAfterRemoval` is the engine's, not the screen's (2026-09-18).** `src/screens/cascade/proposal.ts` summed what came off a quote and subtracted it from `quoteTotals`, in a file whose own header says the screen holds no arithmetic of its own and in a repository whose CLAUDE.md says a derivation is a pure function in `src/domain`. No figure was ever wrong — `proposal.test.ts` asserts the applied total equals the promised one — the rule was. `removedValue` and `totalAfterRemoval` are in `domain/quote/cascade.ts` with five cases of their own, including the one that matters: a row the price file carries no figure for is `null`, not zero, and a line that never added to the total cannot subtract from it. `fitmentCascade` calls them too, so the one difference between the two channels — the fitment sheet adds a swap on top, the finish sheet does not — is now one line each instead of two copies of the same loop. Lost: a private loop in a screen folder that happened to agree with the engine.

- **The app declares itself dark, and paper says light to the printer (2026-09-18).** `index.html` set `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light">` and `app.css` painted the body `--color-white`, while all seven screens painted their own dark room on their `<main>` — so the disagreement was between the document and every screen in it, and the browser believed the document. What it cost, measured on the running app: a 17px classic light-grey scrollbar inside the picker's stage on a navy plate, light-scheme form controls, and white canvas behind every overscroll and anywhere a screen was shorter than the window. `:root { color-scheme: dark }` with the body on `--color-ground` fixes all three at once, because `color-scheme` is the only thing a browser reads for the parts no stylesheet can reach; a screen that wants its own scroller still sets `scrollbar-color`, and the picker and the register keep theirs. `@media print` puts both back to light, because the document screen prints the same nodes it draws and A4 is white. Lost: **"Light theme only until a theme is first-class" (2026-09-16)**, which was written about a toggle and outlived the app it described — there is no toggle, there is one theme, and it is the dark room the two picked boards drew.
- **A dead end is a screen, not a fallback (2026-09-18).** `/nope` was TanStack's own white page with the words "Not Found" on it: zero controls, no dealership, no way back, and — because the router set no `defaultErrorComponent` — where every screen that threw ended up too. `src/screens/lost/` is a built screen with its own stylesheet and its own ladder, whose SUBJECT is the address: set large in the mono face, labelled, cut at 120 characters with the cut said out loud, because a person who arrives here arrived by typing. A screen that threw is quoted word for word and offered `Draw it again`; a thrown thing that said nothing is reported as having said nothing. Lost: a generic error boundary, and the argument that a 404 is not worth a direction — it is the one surface in this app a person could reach and not leave.
- **The not-found is declared on the root route and the thrown one on the router (2026-09-18).** They are caught in different places: a route catches what its own component threw, so the root's `errorComponent` would only ever catch the root's `<Outlet />`. `notFoundComponent` goes on the root route, so every router built from this tree behaves the same — the app's and the suite's; `defaultErrorComponent` goes on the router, where every route without one falls back to it. And the router itself moved out of `src/main.tsx` into `src/app/router.ts`, because until then the app had one router and every test that drove the real tree built a different one, which is precisely why the missing error component could not have been caught by a suite. Lost: four lines in `main.tsx`, and a second router nobody was measuring.
- **A control with an address is an `<a>`, and the primitive was extended rather than bypassed (2026-09-18).** Grepped across `src/screens`, `src/ui` and `src/routes`: not one `<a>` existed in the app. Every move between screens was a button calling the router, so nothing could be middle-clicked, copied as a link or opened in a new tab — in an app where every position is already a real address and a search param. `Button` takes an optional `href` and renders the same class on an anchor; a plain left click is prevented so the router still navigates with no page load, and a middle, ctrl, cmd, shift or alt click is left entirely to the browser, which is the whole reason for the element. A REFUSED control is never a link: an address a person is invited to open and then told they may not is worse than a button. Lost: a second `Link` primitive with its own stylesheet, and converting the seven screens' existing presses wholesale — each screen's seam is a `() => void` prop by design, and that is a change to seven screens rather than to a primitive.
- **The app's front doors are one list, and a suite proves each one is real (2026-09-18).** Three screens needed the same three addresses — the lost screen and the two "no quote is filed at this address" states, both of which were dead ends of their own — and three copies would drift the first time an address moved. `src/app/ways.ts` names them once; `shell.test.tsx` mounts the real route tree at every one and asserts a real screen arrives rather than the lost screen, so a door that stopped existing fails the suite instead of printing an invitation to nowhere. Lost: each screen writing its own address, which is what put `/quote/new` in four files.
- **The density ruler reads a register with one real quote on it, and reads two things (2026-09-22).** "No fake data" means a ruler cannot plant eighteen quotes to count, and on an empty register it read "6 rows" — three band heads and three notices — a figure that meant nothing either way and was the one red line on the gate for four days. The quotes route now arrives `with-a-document`: the walk mints one, waits the write-behind out and reloads onto the register, whose `data-read` is the store's own `loaded`. `readDensity` reports the records a person can see AND the records the list would hold at the pitch of the real row it is drawing — the room the route names (`.qr-body` less `.qr-act`, the same two boxes `quotes.spec.ts` reads against the `--row-h` token) less the band heads, over the measured pitch — and the requirement is met by either. A register with no record fails outright, because a pitch nobody measured is a figure nobody has. Measured at 1280×800: 1 record, 28px pitch, 657px of room less 72px of heads, holds 20. A row is measured by the box its cells occupy before it is called collapsed, because a one-grid register draws rows with `display: contents`. Lost: a ruler that counted furniture, and eighteen planted quotes.
- **The sheet changes through commands with inverses and `CatalogueEvent`s, and a batch is the step (2026-09-22).** `src/domain/catalogue/commands.ts` is the write half of the old project store as `(data, now) => { next, inverse, said, event }`, the shape the quote commands settled; `src/state/catalogue.ts` applies them, keeps one stack fifty deep pinned to event ids, and hands the repository only the maps a command touched. Every way back puts the objects it replaced back BY REFERENCE, so an undo is byte-equal and an unflushed edit undone costs the disk nothing. Lost: the old history's `commit(op, fn)` with its microtask bursts — the grouping a levels apply needed is now said (`batch`) rather than noticed, so it no longer depends on a loop staying synchronous; `src/domain/undo.ts` stays as the ported engine its own suite proves, and lends the store its labels. Also lost: a persisted audit table for the sheet — the repository has no events store, adding one is a schema change to argue about, and the store says in its header that its `events` are this session's.
- **Deleting a row takes the pairings that named it and empties the links to it, counted first (2026-09-22).** The old `deleteRow` filtered one list and left every join row pointing at nothing; on the pack a Highfield hull is named by pairings across five joins. `cascadeOfRowDelete` counts them the way `cascadeOfDelete` counts pages, the command takes them, and the way back puts each one at the index it sat. One level only. Lost: leaving a dead id in a link cell as the old store did, and a recursive cascade nobody could read off one sentence.
- **A pairing's identity columns keep their type and their place; its link may still be re-pointed (2026-09-22).** The two links that make a join a join and the three pair columns refuse `retypeField` and `deleteField` with a sentence; `retargetField` on a join's link is allowed with its counted radius, because the old designer allowed it and its ported suite pins it. A rung of a price ladder keeps its type, and a cost column may not be declared a rung — by `isCostColumn`, the same judge the quote uses. Lost: a blanket lock on every column of a join.
- **A table the pack declares may be edited; the event carries the packer's own provenance line (2026-09-22).** The packer writes each table's workbook, sheet and rows into its `description`, and every event on such a table carries that line as `provenance`. Lost: refusing edits to pack tables (the file is the dealer's data), and a structural "declared by the pack" flag the repository does not hold.
- **`repositories(orgId)` hands back one object per organisation (2026-09-22).** The Dexie ledger that makes a cell edit write one row lives inside the repository instance, so the instance that filed the pack is the only one that knows the 15,691 rows it wrote; Entry files through this door and the catalogue store writes through it later. Lost: a fresh adapter per call, which would have made the first save after every pack load a wholesale reconciliation against the disk.
- **The row index is derived from the rows and built on first read, never copied on a write (2026-09-22).** Measured on the pack before the change: a level set over 187 Highfield variants took 3,792 ms as one batch, because every cell edit copied the 15,691-key `rowById` record to point one id at a new object (~18 ms a copy). `indexRows` is now a closure over a sheet's own rows that builds the record once when somebody reads it, and no command reads it — a command scans its own table. The record's shape is unchanged for every reader. Lost: an index kept current on every write, and a `Map` in its place (a ~3 ms copy, and a shape a screen already reads as a record).
- **History is one spine with a fold, and the fold is owned as an invention (2026-09-22).** Direction A "The spine" was ASSIGNED for this round rather than picked, because the critic counted five of the twenty Milestone 2 directions as one list-left, detail-right shape across four Cockpit screens; A is the one composition on the history sweep that is not that. B's gutter tally is kept as a figure the day head carries (`3 quotes · 11 events · 1 given · $66,584`, every one a count or a sum of frozen totals with its denominator), and C's "one quote's diary" is kept as what a folded line opens INTO — its versions as dated entries, its events oldest first, `Quote this again` or its refusal at the foot — rather than as a second pane. The fold itself has no frame: GitHub's 21 commits are open beneath their line with no fold control, and Linear's collapsed history is a changelog sentence, so `history.css` owns it as an invention with its three reasons. Lost: B "The day book" (a card per quote led by its picture — about a quarter of addresses are unheld, and cards do not hold eighteen rows), C as a pane beside the spine (the fifth master–detail of the round), D "The month" (it wins the way back to a day and loses the reading of what happened), and Linear as a reference at all — it is the register's primary and the critic found it claimed twice on this sweep.
- **A diary is cut by the day something HAPPENED, never by the day a document was made (2026-09-22).** `groupByDay` cuts by `createdAt`, so a quote started on Monday and issued on Friday sat under Monday with `… is issued` beneath it — one head over two calendars, the exact fault the sweep caught GitHub printing (`Commits on Dec 14` over rows `committed on Dec 15`) and the critic then found at home. `src/domain/quote/diary/days.ts` indexes `QuoteEvent.at` through `day.ts`'s own local-day rule: a day head stands over that day's events and nothing else, the same document is a line under every day it was touched, and the span cuts DAYS (`daysFrom`) while the customer and the typed words cut documents (`filterQuotes` with its span left open). Pinned: the two-calendars case, 23:59 and 00:01 as two days, and 02:28 local as the local day whatever UTC says. A document with no diary at all is placed under the day it was made with `no diary kept` on its line, because "hidden without a count" is on the sweep's avoid list. Lost: `groupByDay` and `filterQuotes`'s span for the diary, and a synthetic sentence for a document's birth — the screen writes sentences about addresses and absences and never one about an event.
- **A filed document carries the event that made it (2026-09-22).** `commands.ts` promises a minted or versioned document "carries the first event, because a document with no history of how it began is a document whose audit starts in the middle", and `freeze.ts` leaves `events: []` because "the mint's own event is written by the command that called this, beside the inverse it pushes" — and the store's `file` put the document away without it. Measured: every document minted through the picker, every new version from the configurator or the register, carried an empty diary until a command touched it, so a reader over `events[].at` could not see the day a quote was started at all, and the ruler's walk would have found a diary with nothing on it. `file` keeps the event once (a caller that already wrote it onto the document is not given a second copy), the store's own suite pins it, and four of its expectations moved from a log that began at the first edit to one that begins at the mint. Lost: a screen-side fallback that placed a fresh document under `createdAt` with no sentence.
- **Quote this again is not a restore, and its way back is Discard (2026-09-22).** `quoteAgain` mints a NEW draft for the same row on the same page through `createViewFor` and `mintQuote` — the two calls the picker's own act makes — at today's prices, on the same rung only where the row still carries it, addressed to the same person by re-freezing the register where the old document points at a row and carrying the typed name across where it does not; not one figure is copied, and the sentence under the control says so. The new document lands under Today as the diary's newest line, the step on the head of the spine says what the commands said, and the way back is `Discard it`, pinned to the document it made — a mint has no inverse, and undoing a document into existence is discarding it. The refusal stands where the act is: `whyNotAgain`'s own sentence when the row is off the sheet or no longer sold, and this screen's one sentence about a shut price file, which is a different fact from a missing row. Lost: opening the new build the moment it is minted (the register's version act does; a diary should record the act it just did and keep the reader's place), and copying the rung where the row no longer carries it.

- **The sheet rests as the outline, and the other two doors are a toggle and a panel, never a second grid (2026-09-22).** `/data/$table` is built from direction A "The outline" of `docs/research/refs/sheet/notes.md` §5, ASSIGNED for this round rather than picked: the critic found five of twenty directions across four Cockpit screens were one list-left, detail-right composition, so the sheet is one full-width grid ordered as the file — the hierarchy as bands with a count on every one, identity pinned, sections folding to chips, 28px rows — and keeps B's gallery as a head chip whose unit is the model (one card per innermost drawer under its branch, the held render or the maker's white mark, the count in the dealer's word, the table's own price column as a band) and C's record as the side panel that keeps the row lit (the row as a spec sheet under the table's own section headings, label over value, every value a button). D's "two identities" survives for a pairing only: `outlineLevels` hands a join the BOAT side as its one level, `buildGroups(rows, [boatFieldId])`, which the critic (§5) found the sweep never said. Lost: B as the resting door (32 of 67 Highfield models hold no picture and Parts holds none, so the common card is the empty one), C as the resting door (a rail beside a record is the shape being stamped), and D as a screen of its own (it is a level, not a composition).
- **The outline holds eighteen rows at 1280×800 by folding the add line into its head and drawing a drawer's line at 24 (2026-09-22).** The sweep's own arithmetic read 17.6 rows on the median Highfield model at the engine's heights — row 28, group 30, add 24 — and named two fixes; the first screen is worse than the median, because Roll-Up is eight models of four variants and a four-row drawer pays a whole head for four rows of return. `layoutGroups` is handed `addH: 0` and the "+ variant" act sits at the right end of the drawer's own line; `OUTLINE_GROUP_H` is 24 for both levels, told apart by ink and not height; the chrome above the grid is two lines (40 and 28px) with the key legend in the standing panel rather than a third line; and the column heads are 30 rather than the engine's 34, in 11px caps on two lines. MEASURED by the density ruler at 1280×800 on Highfield Inflatables, the file's worst table: 18 records in view at a 28px pitch, 655px of room less 168px of band heads — the seven drawer heads the virtualiser had drawn — holds 17 at the pitch, and the ruler takes the greater of the two, so the eighteen are the rows on screen and not an arithmetic. `e2e/routes.ts` names the room as the grid scroller less its sticky header. Lost: the engine's 30px group line and 24px add line on this screen (kept as `helpers.ts`'s defaults for every other caller), Retool's 32px default row (the register already measured 28), and the chrome's third line.
- **The published table numbers against the ported engine, decided one by one (2026-09-22).** `docs/reference/dense-tables-and-selection.md` was written for this screen and the sweep never cited it; read against `helpers.ts` at the build: Grafana's 150px column floor LOSES to the engine's measured 116 (`FIT_MIN_COL_W`; 33 columns at 150 is 4,950px against 1,280, and 116 was measured twice on Stacer); Retool's 32px default row LOSES to `ROW_H = 28` at rest, with 36 and 48 as the two steps a dealer may choose, each named by the picture it carries (24, 32, 44px); Observable's fixed layout above twelve columns is ADOPTED and was already true (every column has an explicit width, the sheet scrolls sideways, FIT stops at the floor); WCAG 2.1.4 is met by the criterion's own third exemption — every single-key shortcut (J, K, X, `/`, `[`, `]`) is bound on the grid alone and the legend says so — until Milestone 4's preferences carry a remap. The paragraph is `Sheet.tsx`'s header and §7 of the sweep. Lost: nothing measured; three numbers a document had and the engine had already beaten.
- **J, K and X are register keys on a sheet, and a letter typed into a cell is typed after Enter (2026-09-22).** Linear's vocabulary — J/K move, X selects the row, Space peeks (tap sticky, hold temporary), Ctrl A, Esc — is the Cockpit's, and the sweep and the critic both asked for it inline; a spreadsheet's replace-mode typing (any printable key starts an edit with that key) is the engine's (`keys.ts`) and is kept for every key EXCEPT those three, because `Shaft Lgth` holds `XL` and a dealer who types it into a cell must not select the row. So Enter opens the cell (the engine's Enter-moves-down survives only inside an open editor, as commit-and-move) and the legend reads "Enter edits, type to replace". Lost: a sheet with no register vocabulary, and replace-mode typing on the three letters.
- **A cost column shows on the dealer's own sheet and says the word at its head (2026-09-22).** The sheet is the dealer's file, so the five Highfield columns the packer marks as cost draw here in the register's tertiary ink with "cost" printed at the head and on the band, counted in the top line and in the standing panel, and the record says "cost, the dealer's own" on the section. None of it reaches a customer surface: `tools/check.ts` refuses the 139 ids and the unmistakable names in any screen source, and this screen's own source names none — the word comes from `isCostColumn` reading the file. Lost: hiding cost on the sheet (the file is the dealer's, and hiding a column from the person who has to fix it is how data rots) and a screen that names a cost column in its own code.
- **A structural change on the sheet is a sentence with the act under it, and a guard reads the source to keep it there (2026-09-22).** The only structure this screen adds is a column: the standing panel names the table, the column, its kind and how many rows it lands empty on, in one sentence built from the same variables the act reads, and nothing is written until "Add the column" is pressed — through `catalogue.apply`, so it is one step with Undo. `src/screens/sheet/structure.test.ts` is the old `structureAsk` suite ported and pointed here (docs/LATER.md owed it to the first screen that could add structure): `addField(` is made from exactly one place, inside `acceptColumn`, and `createTable`, `deleteTable` and `deleteField` appear nowhere on the screen. Lost: a "+ column" plate at the header's edge that authors a column on a click (the engine's `ADD_COL_W` is still reserved and drawn nothing), and a structural act with no sentence.
- **A picture cell draws the held copy or says "held as a link", and only while its column is in the window (2026-09-22).** The dense row carries a 24px copy (32 and 48 at the two taller heights), the record carries the copy at the panel's width, and a row whose address the ledger holds no copy of says so in words rather than drawing a hole or the maker's address. The copy is painted only when its column lies inside the scroller's window — the engine's `windowColumns` decides what is drawn, two columns of overscan either side and no overscan for pictures — because a thumbnail rendered in a column scrolled out of the clip still has a box, and the contrast ruler read the panel's key legend as standing on it (1.04:1 "on the picture", measured at 1280×800 before this). Lost: an `<img>` on every row of every column in the DOM.
- **A selection is washed with an inset shadow, not a background, so the pin stays opaque and nothing on the grid is a picture (2026-09-22).** The selection wash `--color-select` is the file's blue at 28% and translucent by design; painted as a background on the sticky name column it let the cell scrolled under it show through — two names printed over each other, measured with the identity band folded — and painted as a background-image it was a "picture" to the contrast ruler. An inset box-shadow wider than `MAX_COL_W` lays the wash over the cell's own ground and is neither. Lost: `background-image` on a cell, and a translucent pin.

- **Customers rests on one person, and the block a quote prints is drawn on paper (2026-09-23).** `/customers` is built from direction **C, "The letter"** of `docs/research/refs/customers/notes.md` §5, ASSIGNED for this round rather than picked (`critique-m2.md` §3: five of twenty directions across four Cockpit screens were one list-left, detail-right composition, and customers A was one of them). The screen opens on ONE person — the row `?who=` names, or the one touched most recently — and its head is a sheet of `--color-paper` carrying exactly what a document carries: `Prepared for`, the name, then the contact lines. Those lines are `readCustomer`'s, the function `freezeCustomer` itself calls before copying `contact` onto a quote, so the plate and the A4 cannot disagree without a test going red; the dealer's acts stand OFF the plate in the margin, and an absent phone prints nothing there because a quote prints nothing there. It is the document screen's own sentence — the paper is the customer's and the room around it is the dealer's — applied to a name and an address, and it is what makes "only C shows the dealer the typo before the customer does" true rather than claimed. **Lost:** A as a composition (its alphabet survives as a pressed control, never the rest state, because `readCustomers` refuses to alphabetise and a list that silently re-sorts disagrees with the sheet it came from); B as a screen (it is the sheet, and it survives as the letter drawing whatever columns the register has and as the door to `/data/__customers`); D as a screen (it survives as the book's grouping option, by the standing of each person's latest quote); the A4 miniature C's own board drew on every quote row (it is either a second renderer or the document's DOM scaled into a 28px row — the critic's finding, answered in `src/screens/customers/pictures.ts` by leading with the boat's held catalogue copy and saying so in words where none is held); and a second pane at any width.
- **A screen whose register is not born yet is measured by pressing the act that makes it (2026-09-23).** The book is a table that does not exist until somebody files the first customer, and nothing in the app files one for you: the build's "Who it is for" types a name onto the document and says so in its own words. So a browser walked through the whole sale arrives at `/customers` with a name at the desk and an empty book — the true state, and one with no row for the density ruler to read a pitch off. `e2e/routes.ts` grows one field, `then: 'file the name'`, and `e2e/shots/recipe.ts` presses the one act on the screen's own pile; `raise: 'the sale'` now reloads onto a route's own address instead of ending on the document, which is the same reload the quotes register already did one branch down. Three honest steps, no planted row — `e2e/mint.ts`'s own argument for pressing the act on the picker rather than writing a quote into IndexedDB. Measured at 1280×800: 1 record in view, 28px pitch, 605px of room, holds 21. **Lost:** seeding `__customers` into IndexedDB before the ruler opens, and a density number read off a book nobody could have.
- **A name already in the book refuses the filing from the block that names it, not from under the button (2026-09-23).** `Button`'s `refusedBy` exists because "one reason shared belongs above, not copied", and the duplicate block already says `A Sarah Jones is already filed.` with *Open her* and *File another* beside it — so the act points at that sentence instead of printing it a second time under itself. Pinned by the suite at exactly one occurrence on the screen. The other two refusals on that form — no sheet, and no name — have no block of their own and carry their own sentence. **Lost:** the same sentence twice on one form, and a modal anywhere near a duplicate (Pipedrive flags and never merges; Apple's *Keep Both* is the default, in two buttons).
- **Four empty grid rows were sharing out the window, and `align-content: start` ended it (2026-09-23).** Measured on the built screen at 1440×900 and at 390×844: the screen's grid declares five rows for the head, the step, the alarm, the found list and the body, and four of them are EMPTY on a first paint. Inside a `min-height: 100dvh` box under `align-content: normal` a grid stretches its `auto` rows, so those four each took a share of the leftover height — the letter sat 120px down the page and the whole book sat 400px down it, for no reason a reader could see. At 1280 and above the book never showed it, because its last row is `1fr` and `1fr` absorbs the free space first, which is why it survived every desk-width look. **Lost:** nothing; it was a defect, and it is recorded because it is invisible at exactly the width a builder checks.

- **Data is its own door, and it rests as a shelf of makers over one ledger (2026-09-23).** `/data` is built from direction **C, "The showpiece row"** of `docs/research/refs/data/notes.md` §5, ASSIGNED for this round rather than picked (`critique-m2.md` §3: five of twenty directions across four Cockpit screens were one list-left, detail-right composition, and data A was one of them). It is at the root and not under a settings screen because the owner asked for that in capitals, and `data.index.tsx` is an index route rather than a layout route so `/data` and `/data/$table` are siblings under one word and neither nests in the other. The seven boat tables are paper plates across the top with the maker's own mark large and its pairing lists as lines beneath — `Yamaha Outboards · 2,519 pairings`, ticked in the motor kind's ink, never the word for the mechanism — and every other base table is a row under its place. What it DOES, and not what it looks like: motors, trailers, fit and parts appear once as rows of their own and again as lines on the boats they fit, so a dealer sees Stacer with its three pairing lists under it instead of fifty-three rows in an alphabet. **Lost:** A as a composition (its column view survives as what a press does — the table's page opens beside the rows, with its pairings as lines, before the sheet opens); B as a screen (its ledger survives as the 1920 state, where every plate carries all of its pairing lists, its workbook sentence and its own act, so no pane is owed for a brand); D (it is a grouping key, not a composition, and the critic found it was B's own row under another head); and the three-kind tree — the manifest holds 25 base tables and 28 joins and **no view tables**, so nothing here draws a third kind.
- **The plate row is an invention and is owned as one, and neither frame it leans on is GitHub (2026-09-23).** The critic found that no frame in the data sweep shows "seven boat-brand plates across the top", and none does. `data.css`'s header says so and names the two that inform it without claiming either as its picture: `prov/dockerhub-tags.png` (one mark large at the head of a page, that page's rows beneath it, and `+5 more…` where the list is cut — which is what ONE plate is) and `live/airtable-interface-designer.png` (the linked thing drawn as a card inside the record — which is what a pairing line is). Three of the four data boards leaned on GitHub and the critic said so; this row shares no frame with the sheet's, the customers' or the history's. **Lost:** claiming a frame for a shape it does not show.
- **A count and the headings it is cut into are two cells, because one cell printed a figure the file does not carry (2026-09-23).** `3,587 products in 187 categories` in one right-aligned cell with `overflow: hidden` overflowed its START edge and drew `87 products`. A reader saw no ellipsis — an overflow to the left is not in `scrollWidth`, so the cut ruler could not see it either — and a figure nobody typed was on screen. `register.ts` now hands the screen `leafSay` and `branchSay` already apart, formatted with the dealer's own grouping (`2,937`, not `2937`, which is what `countLabel` writes and what the head beside it does not), and the row draws them in two cells. Every `.dt-cell` is a block and not a flex box for the same reason: `text-overflow: ellipsis` says nothing to a flex container, which is why the word "Accessories" was being sliced 4px short with no ellipsis and no ruler complaining. **Lost:** one cell for both halves, `countLabel`'s bare integer on a register, and a screen that cuts a domain string apart with `indexOf(' in ')` to find its own columns.
- **Eighteen rows at 1280×800 were found in gaps nobody was looking at (2026-09-23).** The ledger held seventeen and the eighteenth was clipped by the list's own scroller — inside the window, so the density ruler would have called it readable, which is the worst kind of green. The screen grid declares five rows and two of them (what the last write said, and what a narrowing did) are EMPTY at rest, and `row-gap` charged the body 16px for the two of them anyway; 8px more came off the screen's own padding and 8 off the plate's. MEASURED after, at 1280×800 in Chromium through the blue door: `.dt-body` 547px less a 34px legend is **513px of room, a 28px pitch, holds 18**, and on this dealer's file all eighteen registers are drawn at once with nothing to scroll (25 base tables less the 7 that are plates). `data.css`'s header carries the arithmetic as measured and no longer as an estimate — the fault the critic counted across four sweeps, three of which used a guess where a measurement existed. **Lost:** `row-gap` on the screen grid, 24px of padding, and a comment whose numbers nobody had read off a browser.
- **A place head leaves the margin wherever the margin cannot hold its name (2026-09-23).** The place is a cell in the margin of its run's first row, which is what buys the ledger its eighteen rows — band rows would have cost eight places 192px. It needs 148px to say "Parts & Accessories" in caps; 770px of tablet measure cannot spare that and still call a table by its whole name, and at 104px the head itself read "PARTS & ACCE…", which is a head that has stopped heading. So under 1200 — and while a page is open beside the rows, where the ledger is 824px — the head comes out of the margin onto a line above its run, the same shape the phone uses. **Lost:** an elided head, and a margin column at a width that cannot pay for one.
- **One screen, two rows in `e2e/routes.ts`, because a ruler can only open one state (2026-09-23).** `/customers` rests on ONE PERSON and `?book=all` is the door to the register of everybody. Density has to be pointed at the book, because that is the part with rows — and with only that row in the list, the four rulers that measure contrast, truncation, overlap and the type ramp would never once have opened the state a dealer actually arrives in. That is how the configurator shipped 11px labels at 2.99 : 1: a screen nobody's ruler opened. So the letter is `customers`, `foundation` (a letter about one person has no rows, the same reason `/nope` is `foundation`), and the book is `customers-book`, `cockpit`, carrying the eighteen. Measured with both rows in: the letter 36 text nodes, 0 below threshold and 0 cut at six viewports; the book 27 and 27, and 1 record · 28px pitch · 605px room → holds 21 at 1280×800. **Lost:** one row measuring the book only, and calling the letter a Cockpit register to collect a ruler it cannot answer.

- **The shell is direction A, "The pill", and it is assigned rather than picked (2026-09-23).** One floating object over every screen but Entry — crest, `‹` named for where it goes, `Home · Quotes · Customers · Data · History`, the finder as a trailing bubble — translucent over a photograph and opaque over a register, mounted once at `src/routes/__root.tsx`. It is the only one of the four compositions in `docs/research/refs/shell/notes.md` §5 that leaves the showroom full-bleed AND the register's eighteen rows whole, and the only one that keeps Data a door AT REST, which is the one instruction the owner gave about this screen in capitals. **Lost:** **B, "The rail and its counts"** as a composition — a 72px rail takes that width off Home's two-photograph fold and the critique (`critique-m2.md` §9) found the sweep never said whether the fold loses it or the rail floats over the picture — but its two best ideas are kept: the count on the door, read off the store, and a section that fills itself with the places this browser has been. **C, "The finder is the shell"** — §9: no door at rest, so Data is a row that appears after a keystroke. **D, "The masthead"** — a two-tier top bar is §0.5's own measurement of ~200px and §4's own first avoid, and it buried Data behind `Menu` on five of eight screens.
- **Ctrl K is the finder's, everywhere, and the two screens that owned it keep `/` (2026-09-23).** `Home.tsx` and `Configurator.tsx` each bound Ctrl K to a field of their own; one palette reachable from twelve screens is the whole point of having one, so the chord is the shell's and each field keeps the key every other find field in this app already answers to. The collision is resolved by Linear's scope chip rather than by taking anything away: on those two screens the finder opens with THAT screen's own rows first, under a chip — "The desk" and the build's own reference — published through a React context (`src/screens/shell/scope.tsx`) and not a fifth store. **Lost:** giving the finder a second chord (a shortcut nobody presses is a shortcut nobody learns); and leaving Ctrl K on the two screens (a palette that is absent on two of twelve is a palette a dealer cannot rely on).
- **The pill costs no layout height, and `--shell-inset` is the clearance it asks for (2026-09-23).** One token in `tokens.css`, 56px, read by every masthead as `padding-block-start: max(<its own>, var(--shell-inset))` — a FLOOR and not a tax, so a head that already starts below the pill gives up nothing. Measured on the running app at 1440×900 and again at 1280×800, identical at both: Home 16 → 56 (**gave up 40**), the quotes register 12 → 56 (**44**), the picker 24 → 56 (**32**), Data 8 → 56 (**48**), Customers 12 → 56 (**44**), History 12 → 56 (**44**), a sheet 8 → 56 (**48**), the dead end 48 → 56 (**8**). The build, the cascade and the document read the same token off their own stylesheets at 20, 24 and 40 → 56 (36, 32 and 16). **Lost:** a wrapper around the outlet that padded every screen (each one declares `100dvh` for itself, so a padded wrapper is a scrollbar and a cut last row); and a bar with a track of its own, which is direction D.
- **cmdk is adopted, and its filter is turned off (2026-09-23).** `shouldFilter={false}`: the library owns the combobox and listbox roles, `aria-activedescendant`, the cursor that walks groups without walking into them and the row kept in view; what matches is `domain/catalogue/search.ts`, `domain/quote/find.ts` and `domain/people/customers.ts`, arranged by `domain/shell/finder.ts`. **Lost:** cmdk's own fuzzy matcher — a second opinion about what "sp560" hits, and the day the two disagreed the palette would be quietly wrong about the app.
- **The finder is a native `<dialog>` shown modally (2026-09-23).** A div wearing `role="dialog"` and `aria-modal="true"` claims a focus trap it does not have; `showModal()` is the only one in the platform, and it brings the top layer and a `::backdrop` with it. The five overlays in `src/ui` are pinned at `z-index: 60` in the same change, because a portal paints at `z-index: auto` and the pill at 20 was being drawn across the head of a modal. **Lost:** a `role="dialog"` div with a hand-rolled trap.
- **At 390 the pill drops to the foot as tabs, and the alternative is drawn in the stylesheet header (2026-09-23).** HIG's phone form; the owner's "disgusting" was an ACTION bar in a quote flow, and the sweep's §1.4 says so. Two things change with it: the counts become dots, and a dot is drawn only for WORK WAITING (open drafts) because a dot cannot print a zero and cannot say whether 51 is an alarm; and the words are set as they are written rather than in caps, because caps plus `--tracking-caps` cost about 52px across those thirty-four letters and History fell off the end of a 358px bar. `--shell-foot` is the room every screen reserves at its foot at that width. The critic (§8) asked for the top form to lead; it is written out in full in `shell.css`'s header as three rules the owner can swap in, with what it costs (48px at the head instead of 56px at the foot) and what it loses (four doors).
- **The shell's class prefix is `way-`, and it is not `sh-` because the sheet already owns that (2026-09-23).** Found by measurement, not by reading: the pill on `/data/$table` reported `position: absolute; top: 900px` at 1440×900, three lines tall and below the fold, because `src/screens/sheet/sheet.css` styles `.sh-grid`, `.sh-head` and `.sh-cell__text` and the shell had written `.sh-pill`. Two screens' stylesheets are compiled into one document; a prefix is a namespace and this repository now has a second one that says so.
- **The register's own `Home` button stays, and the pill's Home stands beside it (2026-09-23).** `critique-m2.md` §4 asks what happens when the shell adds a second way back to a register that already carries one in paid head height. The answer for this round is: nothing. The button is a SEAM — `goHome` is a prop the register's own suite presses, and it costs the list no row because it sits inside the head's existing height — while the pill's Home is the app's. It is the one duplication this shell leaves standing, and it is written down here so the owner can say whether to cut it rather than finding it.
- **The pill costs the sheet two rows, and that is the one thing this round could not fix from the shell (2026-09-23).** Measured at 1280×800 on `/data/boat_highfield`, twice, with the token zeroed in the running page for the second reading: with the pill, 16 records in view, 28px pitch, 607px of room less 168px of band heads → holds 15; with `--shell-inset: 0`, 18 records, 655px of room, holds 17. So the sheet met the eighteen with no margin at all before the shell existed — its own sweep predicted 17.6 — and 48px of clearance takes two rows off it. It is not fixable from this side: measured at all five widths, the pill at inset 0 is painted over the sheet's own head (`in 7 series · 33 columns…`, 219×17 at 1280, 346×17 at 834), so the clearance is real. The other four Cockpit registers are clear of it — quotes holds 19, history 19, the customers book 20, and Data shows 18 records in view — and the sheet's own eighteen has to come from its head or its resting grouping, which belongs to that screen.
- **The unit suite runs in the dealership's timezone on every machine (2026-09-23).** `vitest.config.ts` sets `TZ=Australia/Brisbane`, as `playwright.config.ts` already sets `timezoneId`. The engine reads a day in the reader's own zone on purpose (`src/domain/quote/day.ts`), and every test was written in Brisbane; on GitHub's UTC runner `src/screens/picker/mint.test.ts` read `20260915-01` for a Brisbane 09:00 and CI was red on every push for it. Measured after, with the machine clock at UTC: the mint test and all of `src/domain/quote`, 27 files and 414 tests, pass. Lost: a softened assertion, and a suite whose answer depended on where it ran.
- **The density ruler is the one reading of a register's eighteen rows, and it asks the layout for the room instead of adding it up (2026-09-23).** It asserted `max(records, capacity)` over a room each route declared as a box less named furniture, and on this tree that passed Data on 18 records in the window when two of them sat under the list's own scrollport (16 readable), passed the customers book on 561px when a full book there has 430 (the pile of typed names stands under the list and was never declared), and failed the sheet at 15 by subtracting drawer lines the virtualiser had drawn below the port (16 readable, 16 held). Now a route names only its list (`density: { list }`); `readDensity` lays one invisible 100,000px block at the list's end for the length of one synchronous measurement and takes it away, reads the room from the first row to the lowest edge a person can see of that full list — its scrollport, every scroller round it, the window, and anything `position: fixed` painted over it, which is the pill — subtracts only the band heads standing inside it, and the ruler asserts that one figure; a row counts as readable only inside every scroller round it and clear of the pill, and two soft checks fail the ruler itself (a readable record outside the named list; a full list holding fewer than are already readable). The quotes and Data flows' own room arithmetic is deleted; the customers and history flows keep their builders' stricter bars — four desk groups standing, two more day nodes — as readings OF the ruler's measurement on the ruler's own walk. Measured at 1280×800: quotes 19, history 19 (17 three days deep), the sheet 16, the customers book 15 (11 grouped), Data 16. `fixture.spec.ts` gains five pages built to fail: a scrollport that hides half its rows, a rigid margin beside an auto one, a list nothing frames, a fixed bar over the list with a layer behind the page, and a grouped list whose lower heads the port cannot show, with a stray record beside it. Lost: `max()`, the `room`/`minus` declarations and `countReadableRows`, and with them two green density readings, Data's and the book's, that were not true.
- **This is Northside Marine's app and nobody else's, and simple wins (2026-09-23).** The owner: "this is a northside marine app yeah so we can remove the unnecessary stuff that is like for anyone that isn't northside marine. remember also simple wins. this app is complex topics made beautiful." Cut from Milestone 4 before it was built: a second organisation and its round trip, sub-dealer organisations, the wizard for a business with no price file, invites, a roles-and-permissions matrix, and the integrations, modules and margin tabs. Its exit is now Northside's own: its details, mark and colour reaching every screen and the letterhead of quotes issued after; a person as the quote's "prepared by"; a new price file diffed, applied and undone with no issued quote moving; a backup restoring into a wiped browser. Cut from what is built, in a round of its own (`.claude/workflows/hl2-northside.js`): the door's "start a blank sheet" and the states it leads to. Kept: the organisation key on every record (invisible, and how Milestone 6 will file Northside's records), the sub-dealer PRICE rungs (Northside's own levels), and customisation (Northside setting its own look). Every direction's judges now break a tie toward the simpler board. Lost: the plan's second-dealership generality, which served nobody who will use this app.
- **The sheet is the price list (2026-09-23).** `/data/$table` is redrawn as direction A of `docs/directions/sheet-redesign/` (judges 21 · 15 · 17): each innermost drawer is one block whose spine says once what its rows share and carries the model's held render, and the rows say only what differs. Measured at 1280×800 on the ruler's own `readDensity`: Highfield 20 records, holds 20 (was 16); Highfield × Yamaha 20, holds 21; Parts 21; Stacer 21. Lost: the outline — one grid in the file's order under series and model drawer lines — and its right-hand record panel at `--spacing(90)`, the composition /quotes and Data's plate share.
- **A series is a chapter, read one at a time (2026-09-23).** On a table whose spine sits under an outermost level of two to twelve values (Highfield's seven series), the list shows one of them, named in the blue chapter bar and kept in `?in=`; the find field reads across every chapter, and a row found in the finder opens its own chapter. Lost: all 588 variants in one scroll, and the browser's find-in-page across series.
- **Inside a model the rows run one material at a time (2026-09-23).** Judge two's change: a leading word that two to four values take across most rows, and that splits at least three models and half of them (`leadSplit` — PVC and HYP on Highfield's Variant, and nothing on any other table), is a level of `buildGroups` beneath the spine, said once in a spanning cell; the rest of the variant prints in the cell, and a variant the word does not lead prints as typed. Lost: the file's own order inside a model on the price list (WH PVC, WH HYP, LG PVC, LG HYP in 40 of 52 two-material models); the Every column door keeps it.
- **A column that says one thing is said once (2026-09-23).** `saidOnce` (src/domain/catalogue/table, measured against a second count on the pack) finds what the whole table, each series and each model hold in common over two rows or more; the head says the table's (Matrix, Base Freight, Other Charges on Highfield, cost marked), the band the series', the spine the model's. The grid draws what a dealer reads first (`readingColumns`: the name, the code he orders by, the price ladder, a varying mark, then what else varies and is not money, while the width allows) and holds the rest one press away, each said where its words now are. Lost: Matrix, Image Link and the spec columns as columns on the resting screen; they are one press away and all behind Every column.
- **A picture is drawn at a size worth drawing, or not in the grid (2026-09-23).** The held render stands on a model's spine at up to 92 px tall (124 at 1920, 72 on a tablet or in a hand), never enlarged past its copy and captioned with the variants it depicts ("WH · 2 of 4 variants"); `spineFit` gives it only to a block tall enough to hold it, and a block whose spine is its table's outermost level (a Stacer series of five hulls) carries none, because one hull's photograph beside five is a stand-in. Lost: the 24/32/44 px thumbnails in a row and the row-height ladder (`?rows=`) that existed to make room for them.
- **What is shut is the address (2026-09-23).** `?read=models` shuts every block to its spine line and `?flip=` names the blocks that differ from the reading, so a write — which rebuilds every row and drawer — cannot open or shut anything (critique §12). A hand rests shut to the model (judge three's graft); a desk rests open. Lost: the depth ladder (Series · Model · Every row) as three chips; its model rung is the "Only the models" press and its series rung is the chapter.
- **The record opens under its row; the structural act is behind a press (2026-09-23).** On a desk the record is a row of its own under the lit row, beside the spine, in `--duration-press`; in a hand it is the screen, with "← Back to the price list" first. "Add a column" is the last line of the columns menu, drawn only when "Add a column…" is pressed, and its act refuses only after an empty press (critique §11). Lost: the resting panel and its always-refusing amber form.
