- **The sheet's head repeats no door the pill carries (2026-09-23).** The screen's own Data and Home buttons and the "Data ·" eyebrow are gone (rule (a), critique §13). What remains is the pill's: its `‹ Data` back chip and its lit Data door still both say Data, which is `src/app/ways.ts`'s to settle and not this screen's. Lost: a way home drawn by the sheet itself.
- **The column heads stand over the scroller, not inside it (2026-09-23).** react-virtuoso wraps whatever it is handed as a header in a box of that header's own height, where `position: sticky` has nothing to stick to: measured on the built sheet, the heads scrolled away with the first model. The heads are now a strip above the list that follows it sideways, inside the one element that is the grid. And a row's last cell, where the lit row prints its keys and its record button, is a `gridcell`, so a pairing row with one data column (every row of Highfield × NSM Custom) reads as a row and not as a band head. Lost: nothing a person could see; the header-inside-the-scroller argument in the old `Outline.tsx`.
- **Four comments in `helpers.ts` corrected, no number moved (2026-09-23).** From the redesign's reconciliation (board 0): `ROW_H`'s docblock cited a 40 px row and a `table.css` this repo does not have; `GROUP_H` said ROW_H + 4 and is ROW_H + 2; "the column floor, and there is only one" stood beside the drag's floor of 76; `DEFAULT_COL_W.boolean` (88) starts under the reading floor. Lost: four sentences a later reader would have trusted.
- **A tablet's spine is words, a hand's render is 54 px (2026-09-23).** Measured at 834 × 1112: a 300 px spine with a render beside its words cut the figure per material to "PVC $2,770 · HYP $…", the one thing the spine exists to say first; so on a tablet the render is the record's and the Pictures door's, and in a hand it is drawn 54 px tall (96 wide) so the head keeps the 210 px its figures need. This corrects the sizes in the entry "A picture is drawn at a size worth drawing" above. Lost: a boat on a tablet's resting price list.
- **History orders a day by instant, then by the quote's own log (2026-09-23).** `inDiaryOrder` in `src/domain/quote/diary/days.ts` is the one place the order is decided, and the day index, the counted words and the opened diary all read it: where events share an instant — every minted walk does — the tie falls to the event's place in `quote.events`, which the engine only appends to. The critic read `addressed · started · issued` at 1440 and `issued · addressed · started` at 390 on one tree (critique #4); two runs must now agree, and `days.test.ts` proves it with ids that sort against the log. Two quotes touched in one instant are told apart by reference, newest first. Lost: the tie-break on the event's id, which is `newId()` and random, and on the quote's id.
- **History's spine has an end, and a young diary's floor is the spine drawn down to it (2026-09-23).** Under the last day the port's leftover height is `.hy-end`: the spine carried on from the rule's grey to the accent, the last fourteen days as a dot for every event kept in its strand's ink (`rhythmOf`, a day before the diary began drawn as not kept), and at a blue ring the day and time the diary began with what it has held since (`diarySince`: quotes written, given, and the sum they were given at). It is outside the grid and inside the port, so a full diary pushes it below the fold and the density ruler neither counts it nor takes it off the room (critique #17, rule (e)). Lost: a content-sized spine with ~700 px of floor under one line (the Milestone 2 build); a Today hero (it would stand in the ruler's room and cost the eighteen); centring a short diary in the window; opening the only line by itself.
- **History's counted words carry their strand's ink (2026-09-23).** `strandOf` groups the twenty-two event kinds into six strands a dealer already says — begun, built, priced, addressed, given, taken back — and each counted word, each sentence in an opened line, each dot in the rhythm and each word in the key at the foot carries a pip in `--accent-blue/teal/ochre/violet/viridian/graphite`; the standing word takes the same inks and the amber stays the act's. The words stay white, so no pip is the only way anything reads. Lost: one white weight for every word on a line; inking the words themselves, which read as a rainbow at 13 px.
- **History says every day, place and key in the dealer's terms (2026-09-23).** A day head is `Today · Wednesday 16 September`, or `Monday 14 September`, with the ISO day kept only in `<time dateTime>`; the four sentences that printed `/quote/$id`, `/quote/$id/document`, `/quote/new` and `/customers` are words (critique #14); the head's own Home is gone because the pill carries it (rule (a)); every keycap is a `.hy-cap` that `pointer: coarse` removes, with a touch sentence in the legend's place, and an empty diary draws no legend at all (critique #18, rule (b)); while the file is read the head says "Looking for a price file", in one line that never wraps, where it said "No price file is open in this browser" in a sentence that wrapped — so the head is one height in every state of the file. Lost: the mono ISO stamp; the head's Home; caps inside controls on a phone.
- **The Quotes door counts what the register counts (2026-09-23).** It printed OPEN DRAFTS under the word "Quotes", so a desk with one issued quote read `Quotes 0` beside a register whose first line is "1 quote is filed in this browser" (critique #9) — the same fault the shell had already fixed for the tables count. The figure is now the register's own `held`, read by `readDoorCounts` in `src/domain/shell/doors.ts` with its suite; open drafts are said aloud with it ("Quotes — 3 filed, 1 of them an open draft") and are the only thing the phone's dot stands for, and `e2e/flows/shell.spec.ts` mints and issues a quote and reads the two figures off the two surfaces. Lost: the open-draft count at a glance at the desk, which Home's drafts column already prints.
- **The crest is never a hole: before a file names a business it is the helm (2026-09-23).** `initialsOf(null)` drew an empty blue disc on every cold load and for good after the blank door (critique #21). `crestOf` in `src/domain/shell/crest.ts` gives the business's initials once the file names one and otherwise the helm HelmLogic is named for — drawn as an SVG, said as "HelmLogic — Home", the name the browser's tab already prints (`APP_NAME`, held to `index.html` by `ways.test.ts`). When the name arrives the initials settle in once per load, 200 ms, a cut under reduced motion. Lost: the person's initials from the session, which would draw an avatar where the dealership's mark belongs; the product's name as letters ("HL"), which reads as some business's initials and turns into other letters a moment later.
- **The crest is the pill's own leading cap, a 40px medallion with a ring (2026-09-23).** It was a 32px disc inside the capsule's padding, the same size as the lit door beside it, so on Home the mark and `Home` read as two blue blobs of one size. It is now the full inner height of the capsule, pulled over the padding it already had, concentric with the rounded end, with a hairline ring in `--color-edge` 3px inside its edge and the initials at 13px — no taller and no wider a pill. Lost: the flat disc, and a crest the same size as a word.
- **The pill carries the doors and no `‹` (2026-09-23).** Every named back said a destination the same window already said: `‹ Data` beside a lit `Data 53`, `‹ Quotes` beside a lit `Quotes 1`, `‹ The build` above the paper's and the cascade's own "Back to the build" (critique #13; rule (a)). The lit door is the way back to its register, a screen inside a document keeps its own way back (the cascade's returns to the chapter it was raised from), and `backFrom` is gone from `src/app/ways.ts`. The pill is now one object on every screen — 558px on all seven screens walked at 1440, where it had been 554 to 632, and 558px on Home at 1280 and 1920 — and only which door is lit changes. Lost: direction A's back named for its destination (sweep §1.5), and the ever-present way back at a phone's foot, which the browser's own back already is.
- **The keycap takes itself away on a coarse pointer, in the primitive (2026-09-23).** `src/ui/kbd.css` hides `.ui-kbd` under `pointer: coarse`, so no screen has to remember rule (b); the shell's own rule had lost to the primitive on source order and `Ctrl K` was drawn on every phone (measured at 390x844: one cap in the pill on all seven screens walked, two on Home; zero after). A cap inside a sentence still owes that sentence a touch twin, which is the screen's. The finder says "Press a row and it does what it says" on a finger and "Enter does what the row says" at a keyboard; `?` opens nothing on a finger; at 390 the finder's bubble is the glyph alone whatever the pointer, and the doors went from 11px to 12px on the room it gave back. `Kbd` gained `tone="quiet"` — the room's wash and strong rule — for the pill and the finder, where two paper caps were the loudest marks on the bar. Lost: four per-screen rules doing one job, and the light cap on the navigation.
- **A third shadow step, `--shadow-sm`, for a float on paper (2026-09-23).** The sheet's redesign put the pill over white paper for the first time, and `--shadow-md` — set for a dusk photograph, 18px down with a 40px blur at 45% black — hung a grey smudge under a 42px capsule that was heavier than the capsule (seen at 2x on the sheet at 1440x900). Over a register the pill now takes `0 6px 16px / 22%` with a 1px contact line; tokens.css had said the first float on paper would measure this step rather than guess it. Lost: one shadow for every float.
- **The picker's question stands in the plate's foot, beside the act it gates (2026-09-23).** On the ADV7 — seven rows, one material — the act refused under "Choose a colourway above and this becomes live" with the seven colourway chips 326px down a 320px scroller and no scrollbar showing (critique #3, the blocker): the 2026-09-18 foot kept the ACT on screen and left the QUESTION in the part that scrolls. The foot now holds the material, then its colourways, then the figure and the press, in that order; only the hull's picture and its figures are in the body. To earn the room a colourway chip is its code alone, and one line under the codes names the chip the pointer or focus rests on, else the one chosen, with its figure where the colourways differ in price. Measured on the built screen: ADV7 at 1440x900 — chips 636–745, act 757–809, body 326px of 326 (nothing scrolls); SP560 with a material chosen at 1280x800 — chips 452–645, act 657–709, the page 800 in 800. Lost: the chips in the scrolling body, and the decoded name printed on every chip.
- **The picker's plate grows with the window and the index takes what is left (2026-09-23).** `clamp(30rem, 34vw, 38rem)` — 480px at 1280, 490 at 1440, 608 at 1920 — where it was 27rem at every desk width, so the index at 1920 was 800px carrying a name and one figure while the plate holding the whole decision had 432. The plate lays itself out by its own width (`container: plate`): from 28rem the picture stands beside its three figures as one spec card and the figure beside the act, surtees-770's strip; narrower, both stack. Lost: the 27rem plate, and the full-width act on a desk.
- **The picker at rest draws the shelf instead of leaving the plate empty (2026-09-23).** Nothing chosen, the plate printed three figures and two sentences in a 683px column with about 350px of nothing between them (built-m2 walk; rule (e) in spirit). It now draws one bar per register, largest first, each its rows over the largest register's rows (`shelfOf`, counted and tested), with rows and models printed beside every bar in text ink — so "588 of 810 rows are one maker's, and they are 67 models" is the first thing the plate says. One hue, the file's blue, thin marks; the bars grow once over --duration-sheet and are still under reduced motion. Measured: 474px of body in 474 at 1280x800, 534 in 534 at 1440x900. Lost: the empty plate, and a figure the screen is built around living as a sentence at its foot.
- **The picker's false refusal is retired with the test that held it (2026-09-23).** `NO_CONFIGURATOR` ("the configurator … is not built yet") had been untrue since 2026-09-17, could not render in the app, and was asserted by `Picker.test.tsx` (critique #27). The constant, the assertion and the route's comment defending it as "the path a component test exercises" are gone; the test now asserts the press hands the new quote's id to the build and prints no route. `Made` no longer writes "It opens at /quote/…" (rule c). The plate's last line, "SP560 · Highfield Inflatables · boat_highfield:483", went too — the register and series were already in the crest and a row key is the sheet's word. Lost: the refusal kept alive as a "real path".
- **The configurator measures its own masthead (2026-09-23).** The search field and the stage stuck at a typed 112px; the masthead grew to 144.69px to clear the pill, so the field ran 32.69px up behind it at 1280, 1440 and 1920 and the stage's photograph lost its top 33px the same way (critique #8). A ResizeObserver on the head writes `--cfg-mast` onto the screen; the field sticks at it and the stage at it plus the floor's own 24px, so the boat does not move while the rail scrolls. Measured scrolled 400px at 1440 and 300px at 1280: head ends 144.69, field begins 145.00, stage 169. The flow test now asserts field and stage never begin above the head. Lost: offsets typed into the stylesheet, and the case that required a gap between the bars.
- **The stage caption says whose rig is in the photograph and what is on this quote (2026-09-23).** The SP560's hero is Highfield's own photograph with a Mercury on the transom; the quote may carry a Yamaha F90XB and another colourway (critique #24). The picture belongs to the model and is neither swapped nor cropped; under it `pictureSays` prints the ledger's own subject "…, as its maker photographed it — the maker's own finish and rig." and, in the file's blue, "This quote: PVC W-W-WB (White / White / White/Blue), with the Yamaha - F90XB from 02 Motor." — read off the document's lines and the rail's chapter, or "no motor yet" where the chapter is empty. The ledger records which model a photograph depicts and nothing about its outboard, so the caption claims nothing about the motor in it beyond whose choice it was. The specification strip went three across on a desk (two rows for the SP560's five facts, not three). Lost: a caption that gave only the picture's pixels, and naming the brand of the motor in the photograph, which no ledger carries.
- **The configurator's words: one verb per count, no plan word, no key on a finger (2026-09-23).** "1 of them carry no price at all" is `linesSay`, with a case per count and a test (critique #25; the document's own "N of the lines below carry" is the document's to fix). "…arrives with the backend at Milestone 6" is now what is true today: a quote is kept in the browser it was written in, so a link opens only on the computer that wrote it (rule c, #14). The find field's placeholder no longer begins "/ —"; the `/` is a quiet `Kbd` beside the label, which the primitive withdraws under `pointer: coarse` (rule b). Lost: the key written into the field.
- **The file's own name reaches the letterhead through the context the app builds (2026-09-23).** Every quote minted on the Master Price File froze no organisation, because `freeze.ts` reads `ctx.org?.name` and the pack carries no organisation record; page 1 of the customer's quotation read "This business has not been named yet" under a pill saying Northside Marine (critique #2). `ctxFrom` in `src/state/catalogue.ts` — which every screen that freezes already calls — now carries `org`: an organisation record when one is handed in as its second argument (Milestone 4's `/manage`), and otherwise `orgNamedByTheFile`, the manifest's own name with its provenance (`namedBy: { source: 'the price file', version, from }`), the tenant key as its slug, the one industry `INDUSTRIES` says this build is for, an empty `createdAt` because the file does not say when the business was set up, and no terms because the file carries none. Proved in `catalogue.test.ts` on the real pack: a quote frozen through the app's composition prints the manifest's name, and a record's name and terms win over it. `freeze.ts`, `document.ts` and `golden.test.ts` are untouched and green; the engine was right to freeze nothing from nothing. A quote frozen before this date keeps its empty letterhead — a document renders from frozen lines only — and the note beside the sheet says so. Lost: filling the name in at print time from `catalogue.business`, and teaching the engine a fallback it has no business knowing.
- **The customer's copy says only what the dealership says to a customer (2026-09-23).** Read as the person handed it, the paper carried the app talking to itself: "Every figure on this document was frozen when the line was picked and cannot move" (which reads to a buyer as a price guarantee nobody wrote), "4 rows were offered from Dealer Fit Packages and none is on this quote", "6 more were offered from Yamaha Outboards", the prop part number, engine hole and slot under a motor, the dealer's codes as a column, "this register carries no price column at all", a legend defining "the level below", a paragraph on how tax is stored, a Terms section whose only content was that there were none, and "the file can be reimported twice and nothing here moves". All of it moved to the note beside the sheet (`src/screens/document/paper.ts` decides which is which, tested on the real file); a register with nothing on the quote is off the paper and a band left empty is off it whole; the cover carries the letterhead, "Issued <date>", Prepared for and Prepared by, and one sentence under the total only when an item is unpriced; "Your price" lists the bands and adjustments above the total so the page adds up where it is read; terms print only when typed; the column row prints only where a band has a quantity over one. The Highfield SP660 walk went from three pages to two. Lost: the stated absences of 2026-09-18 ("Not taken.", "Optional.") on the paper, the legend, the Code column, and the page-1 foot paragraph.
- **The paper's word for a line with no figure is "Not priced on this quote" (2026-09-23).** The engine's `NOT_PRICED_HERE`, "Not priced at this level", is the dealer's word and stays on the build and the note; a buyer does not know what a level is, and the legend that explained it is off the paper. `NOT_PRICED_ON_PAPER` lives in the screen's `paper.ts` beside the only surface that prints it, the wording the quote spec (§5) names. Lost: one word shared by cell and legend out of `document.ts`, which this round did not own; the proposal's Paper step may move it there.
- **The document's chrome carries no door the pill carries (2026-09-23).** `Home · The register · Start a quote` under the document's line (added 2026-09-18 when the paper was a dead end) were a second copy of the pill two inches above — "five ways off a screen whose subject is one sheet of paper" (the walk; critique #13, rule a). The chrome keeps `Back to the build` and `Print`, and the print sentence is a salesperson's ("Print puts these pages on A4. To keep a PDF instead, choose Save as PDF as the printer.") rather than the direction's thesis. The dead end (a quote this browser does not hold) offers only the ways the pill does not carry, filtered against `DOORS`, and no longer offers `Back to the build`, which led to the configurator's own dead end; its sentence says what happens in the dealer's words instead of "…at Milestone 6" (rule c). Lost: the 2026-09-18 ways row.
- **Entry speaks the dealer's words, and its fit is asserted (2026-09-23).** "Sign-in that really checks who you are arrives with the backend at Milestone 6" is "Each person gets a sign-in of their own once quotes are kept online" (critique #14, rule c); the panel names the photograph's table by its own name ("Stacer · 91 rows in the file") rather than the packer's key `boat_stacer`; the door's "28 of the tables are fitment joins" is "28 of them say what fits what"; and the unheld-mark sentence says "…own mark has not been added yet" instead of "not in this repo with provenance". `e2e/flows/entry.spec.ts` now asserts `scrollHeight ≤ innerHeight` at 1280×800, 1440×900 and 1920×1080, where `entry.css` claims the composition fits (rule d; measured 800, 900 and 1,080). Lost: the key in mono as evidence; the count beside the name is still the manifest's.
- **A saved quote is named for the dealership and the quote, not the app (2026-09-23).** The print window proposes the page's title as the PDF's file name, and the title was `HelmLogic` on every screen, so Save as PDF gave `HelmLogic.pdf` for the one object that leaves the building. While the paper is open its title is `paperTitle` — "Northside Marine quote 20260923-01 – Highfield - SP660 (PVC) W-W-WB", the quote spec's §10 shape — and it is the app's again when the paper closes; a quote that froze no name is "Quote <reference> – <boat>". Set by the document screen rather than its route, beside the paper it names. Lost: nothing; no screen set a title before.
- **In a hand, the picker lists no models until it is asked for some (2026-09-23).** At 390x844 with nothing chosen the page was 17,325px — all 289 models in one column, twenty windows of thumb with nothing to jump by (built-m2 walk). Under 834px the first stage is now the counted rail, the find field and the drawn shelf; the list arrives with a register's press or a typed name, and the sentence under the field says so ("Choose a register above, or type a name, and its models are listed here — 289 in all") where the desk's says every model is addressable. Measured: 1,439px of page at 390x844. The tablet and the desk keep the whole list, where it scrolls beside the plate. A register's own plate draws its series the same way — largest first, capped at eight with the rest counted in a sentence (`seriesShelfOf`; Stacer's 22 series are 8 bars and "And 14 more series, 45 rows between them") — and holds 494px of body in 494 at 1280x800. Lost: every model in a phone's first screenful of scroll, and the register plate's empty middle.
- **A colourway's name line holds its height (2026-09-23).** The line under the code chips names the chip a pointer rests on; the foot is anchored to the plate's bottom, so a line that grew on hover lifted every chip under the pointer. `min-block-size: var(--text-xs--line-height)` had been dropped (the token is a unitless ratio) and the flow test's press on the first chip never wrote its row; it is `1lh` now, and exactly one line on a plate 28rem and wider. Measured at 1280x800 resting on each code of the ADV7 (7), the PA700EW (5) and the SP660 in Hypalon (9): the first chip moved 0px and the line's scrollWidth never passed its 430px. Lost: a hover preview that moved the thing being pointed at.
- **Home's empty drafts are a sentence and three steps, not the board's diagram (2026-09-23).** Critique of Milestone 2, #23: the empty card — labelled boxes reading "THE BOAT'S OWN PHOTOGRAPH", "MOTOR", "WHO IT IS FOR", "WHERE THE ACT THAT OPENS IT WILL SIT" — was "the weakest object on the best screen", where every other empty state in the app is a sentence. An untouched desk now says "No quote has been started in this browser yet.", then the sale as a numbered list in the words the screens that make a quote actually use — Choose the boat · Build it · Give it to the customer, each with one line under it — then "New quote starts the first. The newest waits here, with its boat and its total." The numerals are the plates' blue at the draft count's own size, drawn by a CSS counter so no ordinal is ever read back as a figure. `All quotes` is drawn once something is filed: on an empty desk it led to three zeros, and the pill's Quotes is there either way. The kept line is a dealer's ("Quotes are kept in this browser, each price fixed when it was picked") rather than "a card is written from frozen lines — never from a live price read". Lost: the board's own empty card, and its argument that the diagram promised the filled card's shape.
- **Home is one screen at 1280×800, 1440×900 and 1920×1080, and a flow test holds it there (2026-09-23).** Critique #10 measured 822 in 800, 915 in 900 and 1,084 in 1,080 with the drafts' last line sliced by the bottom edge, against a status file claiming all three fitted. Measured after, empty desk: the lowest thing Home draws plus its margin is **778 in 800, 880 in 900 and 1,048 in 1,080**; with a draft on the desk the tallest column is still "What it sells" at 1280 and 1440 (374) and the drafts at 1920 (359). `e2e/flows/home.spec.ts` asserts `scrollHeight ≤ innerHeight` and every column's last child inside the window at those three sizes — on an empty desk, on the second visit, and with a minted draft on the desk. What paid for it: the caption under the photographs is one line naming what they show, where it was two lines of pixel arithmetic ("held 2,560 × 1,706, drawn 770 × 513 … neither drawn past its own size") — the promise itself is measured off each photograph in the flow test at six sizes; the desk's line says what the two controls under it are for instead of counting the file's split a third time; the makers' eyebrow is "The boat makers · 7" and its credit is one sentence instead of "13 makers checked, 12 held, 17 files … sha256"; and at 1200–1439 the desk gave 24px to "What it sells" (76 · 62 · 61 · 1fr), which stopped its eyebrow and its longest label wrapping. Sixteen of the pixels won back went to the fold at 1280 (232 → 248). Lost: the film line's measured sizes on the screen, and the masthead rising into the pill's row — measured and refused, because at 1280 the stamp would start at x 889 under a pill that ends at 919 and an unnamed business's sentence would too, which would make Home the one screen that ignores `--shell-inset`.
- **Home's search sentence says where a found row opens in keys to a keyboard and in touch to a finger (2026-09-23).** Critique #18 and rule (b): "Ctrl K opens the finder" was printed at 390. The sentence is two twins — `<Kbd tone="quiet">Mod K</Kbd> opens the finder…` and "⌕ Find, on the bar, opens…" — and `home.css` draws one per `pointer: coarse`, `display: none` on the other so the field's description never reads it aloud. The cap is the quiet tone because two paper caps were the brightest marks in the desk after the act and broke the sentence's line spacing. The flow test presses `/` for the field and asserts Ctrl K opens the finder and not the field — the Truth agent's failure at all six sizes was this case pressing Ctrl K since the shell took it, which also meant the search, page-error and picture checks after it had not run anywhere. Lost: Ctrl K on Home's own field, which DECISIONS already gave the finder.
- **The dead end has one loud way out and prints no address but the one asked for (2026-09-23).** Rules (a), (c) and (d) on Lost. - **Home's photographs fade in when they arrive (2026-09-23).** `--duration-reveal: 400ms` (M3 medium4) joins the motion tokens; the fold's pictures are drawn at opacity 0 and set `data-arrived` on their own load (or at once when already cached), then fade over it on `--ease-out`, and cut in under reduced motion. - **The app is the day, blue and white, and the dark room is the night, offered and not default (2026-09-24).** The M2-close critique's finding 5: PLAN.md's standing rule is "Blue and white was the brief… dark mode is offered, not default", and eleven of thirteen screens rested on near-black navy with the one colour at rest an amber act; the 2026-09-18 line that made dark the only theme named an old internal rule as what lost, not the owner's brief. It could not be flipped in one file, because a screen said "ink" by naming a step (white, neutral-300, neutral-400, blue-300) that was only ink on a dark ground. So `tokens.css` now declares ROLES — `--color-ink`, `-ink-2`, `-ink-3`, `-ink-file`, and the ground, panel, rules, wash, edge, glass, hush, shadows and the kinds' inks — with the day as their values (the room blue-50, a plate white, ink neutral-950/700/600 and blue-700: 16.15, 8.70, 6.52 and 7.07:1 on the room, measured) and the dark room of 2026-09-17 byte for byte under `:root[data-theme='night']`, set only by a pref a person chose (`src/app/theme.ts`, tested; its control is the appearance panel of docs/CUSTOMISATION.md). Eleven stylesheets read the roles now (home, quotes, history, data, customers, lost, the document's floor, the cascade, the build, entry's card, the pill and finder) and the four room-toned primitives with them; `index.html` and `app.css` say `light`. Four things the day needed that the night did not, each measured: a control's edge (`--color-control-edge`, 3.92:1 on white, where neutral-300 was 2.11), the act as a word (`--color-act-ink`, 4.76:1 on the room, where amber-400 is 1.77), day inks for the six kinds (5.21–5.71:1), and softer navy shadows (the boards' 45–55% black was drawn over dusk). Three surfaces keep a dark ground on purpose, because of what is drawn on them: entry's room is the sea at dusk (blue-950) under a dusk photograph, with the card and the second door now white glass and the flag the dealership's blue; the build's mark well is blue-900, because the stage draws the maker's WHITE mark; and the customers' card stays the file's deep blue, declaring the night's roles on itself so a rule shared with the room reads the ink its surface needs. Lost: the dark room as the app's default, and entry's navy flag. The owner has still not been asked; the night is kept whole and needs only the control that offers it.
- **The sale screens speak the dealer's words, and a test holds them to it (2026-09-24).** M2-close critique #4 quoted the engine on the build ("A quote is written against ONE row … re-roots the document", "no price column on this table" six times on one SP560, "699 of this table's 2,937 rows", "on the shelf", "rung", "frozen lines"), the cascade ("no picture this repository holds a copy of", "lines stand on this document", "carry that rung", "the committed figure", "the row you asked for", "No load column has been named for this project, so the capacity floor cannot run") and Customers ("A row in a book that is a table on the sheet", "not to this row", "5 columns"). Every one is now said as a dealer says it — a hull, a finish, a price level, what is paired with this hull, the book — and `ENGINE_WORDS` in `src/screens/configurator/say.ts` is read against the whole rendered text of every chapter of three builds (SP560, SP660, Stacer 519; draft and given), both cascade channels before and after the act, and Customers' pile, book and letter. What lost: the rule that these screens print the engine's sentences verbatim ("NOTHING ON THIS SCREEN COMPOSES A REASON"). The facts stay the engine's; five of its sentences (a held line's two, the unrunnable load check, a bare stop's instruction, the several-line double charge) are worded on the screen, each matched exactly so a change of wording in the engine fails a test instead of guessing.
- **A list on the build says "4 of 209 paired with this hull", and the rule's file-wide rate leaves the build (2026-09-24).** The reason line under a motor or trailer list read "Highfield × Yamaha — Motor Fitment names which ones go with this one. It holds at 100% across the price file — 1,424 of 1,424 live standard and first-alternative motors." The list's workbook name and a rate measured across the whole file are the rule's evidence, not a reason a customer at the desk needs, so the counted line says "paired with this hull" and no rule is shown (CLAUDE.md: a rule SHOWN carries its numerator and denominator — none is shown here). A list a rule narrows keeps the rule's own clause. What lost: the measured rate on the build; it belongs with the rules (Milestone 3), where it can carry its caveat, which it never did here.
- **Chapter 01 draws the hull once (2026-09-24).** The subject's own list has no shortlist, so under the SP560's fifteen finishes it drew "0 of 0 offered", "This chapter has nothing left on its shelf" and the hull again under "Also on this quote … not in the list above" with −$41,340 beside it; on the Stacer 519 it was all of chapter 01. `readRail` no longer draws the subject's list: the hull is the head's "chosen:", the masthead, the stage, and the lit finish where there are finishes. What lost: the press that took the hull off a boat quote, which nobody makes and the cascade already refuses to reason about ("This quote has no hull on it").
- **A picture's provenance on the build and the cascade is where it came from, in one line (2026-09-24).** "Stage copy 2,560 × 1,708, drawn here at 624 × 416, never enlarged · from media.highfieldboats.com" and the cascade's "On the plate: this row's own copy, 1,100 × 619, never enlarged — … Behind the sheet, blurred: …" are now "Photograph from media.highfieldboats.com" and "Picture of this boat from www.highfieldboats.com. Behind it, the SP560 on the water, from media.highfieldboats.com — the model, not the colourway on this quote" (M2-close critique #4, #21 on these two screens). The ledger keeps the provenance; the build's line carries `data-held` and `data-drawn` so "never enlarged" is still checked, by a test rather than by a customer. What lost: the printed pixel arithmetic. Entry, the picker and the document keep their own lines (their screens).
- **Home says lists and lines, and where its copy came from is data (2026-09-24).** The masthead read "Master Price File · read from this browser" over "53 tables · 15,691 rows · 28 of them pairing lists", the plates "588 rows in that register, and 7 of them are this model", the makers "588 rows", the field "Search 15,691 rows", and the panel taught "A row is a line of the price file" in order to take the word back. The figures are unchanged; `data-from` carries pack or repository and `pack-loads.spec.ts` reads it. - **Customers dates a thing as a person does, and its head says what the book keeps (2026-09-24).** `FILED 2026-09-24` and `given · 2026-09-24` beside `Given · today` (M2-close critique #19) are now "today" or the diary's own "Wednesday 16 September" (`daySaid`, `src/domain/people/book.ts`); "A table on the sheet · Customers · 5 columns" is "Kept for each: Name · Phone · Email · Address · Notes", a dealer's own heading included the minute it is added (`keptSay`). - **The quotes register shows the newest quote's cover, and an empty one offers the makers (2026-09-24).** M2-close critique finding 6 (#17 still open): at 1920 with a model the heroes ledger holds no photograph of, the room under the rows was a bordered empty frame about 690px tall beside a panel whose middle 500px was white. The room now always holds the newest quote, read down a ladder (`src/screens/quotes/cover.ts`, tested): the photograph on the water (unchanged, and it still wins over a newer quote without one), the row's own catalogue copy at no more than its pixels, the maker's dark mark set large with the hull's name and "No picture of this boat is held here, so Stacer's own mark stands for who built it", or the name in type — under one band on `--color-accent` carrying the state, the hull, the reference and customer and the total. With nothing filed the room offers every boat maker on the file as a door into the picker on that maker (`newQuoteOf`, the picker's own `brand` position), and the wireframe "A row will read like this" and the paragraph about photographs are gone (minor 17). - **History's fortnight is a calendar of two weeks at a desk, and its key says the lines' words (2026-09-24).** M2-close finding 6: "one line, then about 300 px of nothing above the rhythm and 280 px below it" at 1920. At 1200 and wider the rhythm is seven columns by two weeks of day tiles that take the run (weekday and date at the top, the day's dots at the foot, today ringed in the act's ink, a day before the diary began an outline), between a floor that keeps them days on an old diary and a ceiling of `--spacing(120)`. - **The cascade stands on the maker's mark where no photograph of the model is held (2026-09-24).** M2-close finding 6: "the left 896 px, 47% of the window, is empty navy with a small card at mid-height" on a no-picture model at 1920. - **A customer's one or two boats are drawn large, and the book's room shows the person under the cursor (2026-09-24).** M2-close finding 6: "Customers' book at 1920: the last text ends at 229 px of 1,080. On the letter, a card of one or two quotes draws each boat the card's width (16:9, 4:3 at 1920, at most 28–34% of the window's height) down the same ladder — the row's copy, the maker's white mark, or the words — so the letter ends at 829 of 1,080 instead of 611, and still fits 800 at 1280. At a desk the book runs to the window's foot and the room its rows leave shows the person the cursor is on, as their page begins: the paper their quotes print and their newest quote with its boat, with "Open their page" (drawn only in 280px or more, so a full book pushes it away whole and the density ruler reads the same room). Measured with one person: the last ink at 884 of 1,080 (was 249). Lost: the 176px thumbnail row for a person with one or two quotes at a desk. - **The person the dealer has just quoted is a customer, and there is no second act to make them one (2026-09-24).** The M2-close critique's finding 7: give a quote to M. Duffy, press Customers, and the screen said "Nobody is filed yet" over three headed paragraphs teaching that a typed name is not a customer until a second act FILES it into "the book". Customers now reads everyone a quote names as a customer the moment the name is typed (`readEveryone`, `src/domain/people/book.ts`): one person per exact name (trimmed, any case — `exactCustomer`'s rule, a claim about the name that links nothing), on the same page with the same acts as a person the book keeps, and nothing on the screen says which. The book keeps a name the first time its page is given something — a phone, an address, a note — in that one press: the row (and on the first day the book itself, in one batch) with the name and the quote's typed line placed under the column its look says it is (`placeLines`: an email is not offered as a phone), then every draft of theirs addressed to the row so it prints the change; one Undo takes it all back and the page stays on the same person. A change to a kept person reaches their drafts the same way; a draft printing older details says so once with "Bring it up to date"; a given quote keeps what it was given. The empty desk is one sentence ("Everyone you quote appears here.") beside the form to add somebody not yet quoted. The list's rest order is the last touched first, not the order people were filed. Words: "filed", "the book", "by name", "Kept for each: …" and "Open the book as a sheet" are gone from the screen ("Add a customer", "Every customer", "Edit everyone at once"). The paper carries the dealership's letterhead as page 1 of a quotation does. What lost: the pile of typed names and its "File M. Duffy" act; the by-name section and "Address X to Y"; filing as a word and as a step; the register order at rest; `then: 'file the name'` on the two customers routes (the rulers now reach a customer with nothing pressed). Still owed by the shell, not this screen: the pill's Customers door counts only kept people (`countCustomers` is the one-line fix), and Ctrl K finds only kept people.

- **A name is matched as it is typed (2026-09-24).** "yamaha f90" put four Pre-Delivery packages above the F90 motors: the file prints "Yamaha - F90LB", so the run was only in the package's name. `search()` now reads a row's name and the query `spaced` — " - ", "·", brackets and commas are one space, a dash inside a word (`B-B-B`, `TA600-MOB`) is kept — so the motors START with what was typed and lead; the mark is mapped back onto the name as printed. - **"Trailer for sp560" is a question the finder answers (2026-09-24).** `askedForFits` reads a kind (trailer, motor, engine, outboard) before or after a boat, with "for", "to fit", "that fits", "on"; the boat half is searched and `fitsFor` answers the kind half with the quote engine's own `relatedRows` over the boat's view block — the call the build makes when it offers a trailer — reading a stored view or the default blocks without writing one. The answer is the fitted things (the file's pick first, "fits all 15"), then the boat with its own verb; where the file pairs nothing, one sentence says so above the boat. - **The finder draws a price list, not a report (2026-09-24).** Each line is name · code · what is counted · price · verb, and each group is a grid its rows are subgrids of, so prices stand in one right-aligned column and verbs in another; a group of a kind wears the kind's own ink (Data's tick and caps word, from `--accent-*`); the price is the list's first declared rung through `priceReadOf` (never a cost column, never a zero), a boat's the picker's; the sheet is 800px at a desk. Under a hovering pointer a verb is drawn only on the row under the cursor or the mouse; the sale's verb ("Start a quote", "Choose the version") is always drawn, in the act's ink, filled under the cursor. Words: "Lists" and "lines" for tables and rows, the file's own "no longer sold", and a nothing-found sentence that names what a dealer types. Lost: 720px; each row sized to itself; the ellipsised fact; OPEN IT ON THE SHEET printed eight times down a group; "Tables", "rows", "a table or a row by what it is called".

It now paints on its doors and acts and folds one frame later, keyed to the opening so a later file is folded again: measured on the dev server, opening 23–84 ms, and HBS126 typed to its answer 234–421 ms on a quiet machine. What lost: the plate at the head of the page on a tablet; the photograph first on a plate in a window under 600 tall; two cards abreast at 844 × 390; the router's scroll to the top on a refinement, which the screen now undoes rather than the route passing `resetScroll: false` (src/routes is not this screen's file).
- **The reason under a refused control is a role, named for the ground its control is drawn for (2026-09-24).** built-critique-m2-close-2.md blocker 1: `src/ui/button.css` inked the sentence under an act, a veiled control and a door, and `src/ui/tile.css` the one under a room tile, with `--color-neutral-100` "for the dark room"; the day became the default that morning and none of the four rules turned, so "This quote is addressed to nobody" under Give it to the customer read 1.23 : 1 on the white plate (reproduced by the new refusal ruler on the old tree: 1.23 by day, 13.48 at night) and 1.08 : 1 on the day's blue room. Three tokens in `src/styles/tokens.css` now carry it: `--color-ink-reason` (the room's second ink, `--color-ink-2`, so it turns with the theme: 9.93 : 1 on a plate by day, 7.88 at night) for an act, a veiled control and a room tile; `--color-ink-reason-paper` (neutral-600, the ink it always had, 7.13–7.44 : 1) for the paper controls — primary, secondary, quiet, a paper tile, a menu's or a select's item — whose ground does not change; `--color-ink-reason-picture` (neutral-100) for a door on a photograph. Measured after, at six sizes in both themes: the finale's sentence 9.93 / 7.88, the given peek's 9.93 / 7.88, every board context 7.13 or more. What lost: a scale step chosen per rule; the night's brighter reason under an act (13.48 → 7.88), which is now the night's second ink and not louder than the ink.
- **The contrast ruler reads every route in the day and the night, and a refusal ruler reads every reason where it is said (2026-09-24).** Why the ruler said "0 below threshold" while the sale's one refusal was invisible, measured rather than guessed: it walked resting screens only, and not one `.ui-refusal` is on any of the fourteen routes at six sizes in either theme; and it read only the default theme, which the rule at fault had been right in until that morning. (A ground it never composited was checked and is not it: pointed at the finale, the sweep reads 1.23 : 1.) So `e2e/rulers/contrast.spec.ts` wears both themes on every route exactly as `src/app/theme.ts` does and refuses a pair of readings whose room did not change; `e2e/rulers/refusal.spec.ts` reaches the finale of a quote addressed to nobody, the peek of a given quote and entry's two doors while the file is held at the wire, at six sizes and both themes, and must read each reason BY ITS TEXT with a figure; and a board plants every refusal context the primitives declare — read off Button.tsx's and Tile.tsx's own unions, which stop the board with a sentence when a new one has no stated ground — on the ground each is drawn for. `fixture.spec.ts` proves it: a resting page says no refusal and fails nothing, the board fails the room's reasons at exactly 1.23 : 1 by day and passes them at night (the theme the rule was written for), and a role that turns passes at 4.54 : 1. What lost: about two and a half minutes of browser gate per run; the single-theme walk.
- **A picture's ground is read with the glyphs taken off it, under the glyphs only (2026-09-24).** The first night reading failed two legible runs off the pixels: Quotes' teaching paragraph at 390 (2.91 : 1 in one 24px column, 7.9 in the next — the densest column of light 13px text was more letter than ground, so its median pixel was a letter) and Home's `Quote the 519 Sea Ranger SDF` at five sizes (3.01 : 1 — the run was the button's border box and its last tile a 2px sliver of its own 55% white border). `measure/read.ts` now makes every run over a picture transparent for the shot (layout unmoved, only the ink goes, the page given back after) and reads each inside the box its own text nodes are painted in (`measure/contrast.ts`, 6b), keeping the median so one dark patch of a photograph still cannot vouch for a caption. A fixture plants both — full-block glyphs at about 1 : 1 the old way, a 217px bordered button at 3.37 : 1 the old way — and reads 17.02 and 21 : 1. With it, all fourteen routes are clean in both themes at six sizes. What lost: the letters and a control's padding and border counted as its ground, a strictness that was an accident and failed only light text.
- **A door's refusal carries its own veil (2026-09-24).** The refusal ruler's first press of entry's blue door found its sentence, "The Master Price File is being read now.", across the lit hull at 844 × 390 at 3.94 : 1 in both themes — no ruler had ever pressed that door. A door stands on a photograph a dealership may replace, so no ink can promise it; the sentence now hugs its words on entry B's own `--color-veil-72` (src/ui/button.css), where neutral-100 is 6.76 : 1 even over a white picture. - **The cascade reads a line the way the customer's paper does; `Standard` is gone (2026-09-24).** `cascadeOfConflict` inferred `standard: true` for a held line whose register has no price column, and the sheet printed `Standard` for the ADV7's DEC rigging kit while the paper printed the same line "Not priced on this quote" (critique of the M2 close, blocker 3). No column on the price file says a line is standard, so the inference is deleted and every row writes `standard: false`. Which of the three a line is — charged, included, not priced — is now `linesAsRead` in `src/domain/quote/cascade.ts`, an index of `readDocument`, the one derivation the paper prints from; the word is the paper's own `cellWord`; the "after" side is read off the document `setLevel` itself writes, so a line the new level prices at nothing reads `Included` exactly as the paper would after accepting. The held card's heading said "no price of its own", which carries the same implication, and now says "the price file has no price for it at any level, so it is not in the total". Pinned three ways: the domain (the inference's own input comes back not standard), `proposal.test.ts` (on the SP560 every unpriced line reads the paper's word on the sheet, carries no figure on the build, and all three count the same lines out of the total), and `cascade.spec.ts` (the held words on the sheet are the words on the draft's paper, at six sizes). - **Every boat is named the way a person says it, from one derivation and a ledger of the maker's own words (2026-09-24).** built-critique-m2-close-2.md's one thing to change first: `Highfield - ADV7 (HYP) B-G-B` was the headline of the build, the register's row, History's line, the customer's card, the cascade and 36px on page 1 of the quotation. `spokenBoat` in `src/domain/quote/spoken.ts` now reads a quote's frozen `rootTableId` and `subjectLabel` into "Highfield ADV7 · Hypalon · Black / Grey / Black", and every screen asks it: the build's headline, stage and shut hull chapter, the cascade (its card, titles and rows), the paper (cover, hull line, the saved PDF's name "… – Highfield ADV7"), the register's row and peek, History, a customer's card, the finder, Home's cards and plates, and the picker's cards, plates and "made" line. Each word has a source: the maker is the prefix the register's rows write; a model the file names in words keeps them, punctuation tidied ("495 - Pro Fisher." is "Surtees 495 Pro Fisher"; a typo such as "Surtess" stays the dealer's to correct); a Highfield code is named by the maker's own page for it, recorded in `data/northside/names.json` with its address, heading and title, read 2026-09-24 (SP560 "Sport 560", PA600 "Patrol 600", RU230 "Roll Up 230"), and the letters the page does not name are kept as the file writes them ("Patrol 600 ST", "Roll Up 230 KAM"); SP300 has no page and is shown as SP300; HYP is Hypalon on the maker's "ORCA® Hypalon or PVC tube", the decals column (307 of 328 HYP rows) and the production review; a colourway is `colourwayOf`, all or nothing, so O-G-DG, R-B-B, I-B-C and WH stay codes. THE MAKER CALLS IT "ADV7" — adventure.highfieldboats.com/boat/adv/adv7/ is headed ADV7 and titled "Highfield ADV 7" — so the critic's example "Highfield Adventure 7" was not the maker's name, and the heroes ledger's "Highfield Adventure 7 on the water" (a caption written by `tools/seed/pick-heroes.ts`) is corrected to "Highfield ADV7 on the water" in both hero ledgers. The file's own string is kept for the dealer: the register's peek and the note beside the paper print it as "In the price file", the sheet and the finder's "as … lists it" line keep it, and the audit's minted sentence says the spoken name. - **A colourway is drawn as colour, from one token per colour the decode names (2026-09-24).** built-critique-m2-close-2.md major 6: "the colour choice has no colour". `swatchesOf` (src/domain/quote/spoken.ts) maps each part `colourwayOf` names to a swatch name, a part named as two ("White/Blue") to both halves, and an undecoded code to nothing; twelve `--swatch-*` tokens in `src/styles/tokens.css` are a neutral rendering of each named colour (the file carries no paint chips), each declared exactly once (a test reads the file). `Swatches` in src/ui draws them as squares or as a striped flag, hidden from a reader, never the only statement of the colour, kept by the printer. A customer's card says the boat's name, then its material and colour drawn on a line of their own; the well under a one-quote card gives that line back (24px) and two cards' wells cap at 25dvh, from 28, because the first cut wrapped the whole name and the page ran to 810 of 800 at 1280 × 800. Printed on the build's stage, the paper's specifications (the document derivation hands them over measured), the picker's figures and the sheet's spine. Jeanneau's Draft (the maker states 1.17 m for the DB/37 OB where the file writes 1.03, so the two are not the same measure) and Stabicraft's Int. Beam (no page read) keep their bare figures. What lost: bare figures, and file headers such as "INT LENGTH CM" over them.

- **The build offers a way back only where one can work, and a new version is a new screen (2026-09-24).** built-critique-m2-close-2.md major 2: after `Give it to the customer` the rail's head pinned "20260924-01 is issued · Undo"; `issue` hands the store `NO_WAY_BACK`, so the press could only raise the third copy of "nothing can go back on it" on one screen. Driven on the dev server before the fix, a second fault on the same step: `Make a new version` opened the new draft 20260924-02 under the given quote's "is issued · Undo" and its refusal, because the route keeps the configurator and hands it the new id, and that Undo answered "There is nothing to go back to on this quote." Now `wayBack` (src/screens/configurator/step.ts, pure, beside `say.ts` because it reads the screen's own step) offers Undo or Put it back only on a draft whose diary ends on that step — `apply` refuses every command on an issued quote, and the store refuses a step something has happened since — so the given step is its sentence with nothing beside it and the finale's `Open the document` and `Make a new version` are the way on; `Configurator` keys its screen by the quote's id, so a new version opens with no step, no refusal and no search carried from the old one. step.test.ts walks a real Stacer 529 through the real store and holds each answer against what the store then does with the press (Undo and Put it back taken; a moved-past step refused with STEP_MOVED_ON; the given step refused with ISSUED_REFUSAL; a new version's own Undo taken); two render cases and configurator.spec at six sizes give a quote, find no Undo and no alert, make the new version, find it clean, and press a pick's Undo there. The unit and render cases were run with the fix taken out and fail (2 of 5, 2 of 2). What lost: a way back drawn on every step whatever it would meet, and the old document's last sentence standing on the new one.
- **A material or a colour chosen on the picker's plate asks the route to leave the window where it is (2026-09-24).** The picker's fixer left it in notFixed because the route was not its file: the router's scroll to the top on every new address was being undone inside the screen after it happened (the tablet's list 143 → 0, the phone's act off the screen). `goTo` now takes `{ stay: true }` for a refinement, and `src/routes/quote.new.tsx` turns it into the router's `resetScroll: false`; a new maker or a new boat is still a new page and still starts at the top. What lost: the jump to the top and back on every colour.
- **The build says what the paper says about a line the file does not price, and the cascade's contract can no longer say "Standard" (2026-09-24).** The cascade's fixer left two things in notFixed that were not its files. The build's chapter head said "Not priced yet", its line under the total "1 of them carries no price at all" and its finale "Carrying no price", for the rigging kit the paper prints "Not priced on this quote" and the cascade now reads the same way; all three now use the paper's words (`NOT_PRICED_ON_PAPER` on the chapter head, "1 of them is not priced on this quote" in `linesSay`, "Not priced" over the finale's count). And `CascadeRow.standard` is deleted from `src/domain/model/offer.ts`: after the inference was removed it was declared, written `false` five times and never read, a field waiting to print a claim the price file cannot make. `cascade.test.ts` now holds that no row carries the property at all. The register and History keep "Not priced yet" for a whole draft with no total, which is a different fact (no figure for the quote), and the golden proof is untouched. What lost: three phrasings of one state on one screen; the Porsche flag in the contract.
- **A given quote's build stops inviting a motor and a return (2026-09-24).** built-critique-m2-close-2.md minors 13 and 19: on an issued, read-only quote the stage caption still said "with no motor yet — 02 Motor is where one goes on" and the note under the price level "Saved as you go — close this and come back to it any time". `pictureSays` now says "with no motor" on a quote that is not a draft, and `savedSay` says "Kept in this browser exactly as it was given."; a storage fault is still passed straight through. Pinned in `say.test.ts`. What lost: two invitations to change what nothing can change.
- **The shell does not print (2026-09-24).** Found by the verifier reading the PDF the customer is handed: both A4 pages of R. Kelleher's quotation carried the pill — "NM · Home · Quotes 1 · Customers 1 · Data 53 · History · Find Ctrl K" — above NORTHSIDE MARINE, because the pill is `position: fixed` and a printer repeats a fixed box on every sheet. The document screen took its own room away in print; nothing took the shell's. `src/screens/shell/shell.css` now hides the pill and the finder under `@media print`. The print gate only counted pages, so it could not see it: `document.spec` "prints the sheets and nothing of the app around them" emulates print on the given quote's paper and fails if the pill is visible or any words paint outside the sheets. It fails on the old stylesheet ("Received: visible") and passes on this one; the PDF read after has nothing on it but the two sheets. What lost: nothing a customer should have seen.
- **In the build's band the maker's name keeps its distance from the caption above it (2026-09-24).** Under 1200px the stage's words stand beside the photograph and the band set the maker's line flush to the top, which was right when nothing stood above it; with the caption above it, "HIGHFIELD INFLATABLES" sat 4px under "This quote: … with no motor." at 834 x 1112 and read as the caption's last line. `.cfg-caption + .cfg-stage__over` gets the 16px it has on a desk. What lost: nothing.
- **The finder and Home's search find a boat by the name the app prints (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md, blocker 2: the renaming round changed what a found boat is CALLED and not what is MATCHED, so Ctrl K answered "Nothing matches" for "sport 560", "patrol 700", "roll up 230", "haines signature fisher 525f" and "jeanneau merry fisher 605", "adv7 black" found no boat, and Home said "Nothing on the price file is called that." for the Sport 560 while "SP560" found 35 lines. `buildSearchIndex` now folds a third reading beside the file's label and its code: `saidOf`, which is `spokenBoat(...).say` spaced as typed, on a boat register's rows only and only where it reads differently from the label (629 of 810; about 9 ms on a 60–100 ms fold, paid once per opening). A tie goes to the file's name, then the said name, then the code, so no reading that answered before is re-ranked; the words-in-any-order tier reads the file's words and the said ones together, so "adv7 black" is the ADV7 in its five black versions and "sp560 b-b-b" still lands. The same fold serves Ctrl K and Home, so the two cannot disagree. Home now says "15 lines answer to that." rather than "carry that word", because no line of the file carries the words "Sport 560". Pinned in `search.northside.test.ts`, `lines.test.ts`, `Home.test.tsx`, and in `shell.spec` and `home.spec` at six sizes. What lost: a matcher that read only the file's strings, and one sentence that stopped being true.
- **A quote is found in Ctrl K by the file's string for its boat too, and the finder lights what the name it prints holds (2026-09-24).** The register prints the boat as a person says it, so the finder's Quotes group could no longer find a document by "SP560" or "B-G-B", which the register's own field (`domain/quote/find.ts`) still did. `QuoteFacts` carries the file's `label` beside the said `subject`, the adapter is one pure function (`domain/shell/documents.ts` `quoteFactsOf`), and a document holding every typed word, not side by side ("adv7 black"), answers as `where: 'words'`. The mark on a boat line is `markBest`, the longest run of the typed words that the printed name holds, so "adv7 black" lights "ADV7" and "highfield sport 560" lights "Sport 560" (it had lit nothing). A Model Code that reads the same as the printed name is not printed beside it. Haines files "Fisher 525F" as its code and the line read "Fisher 525F  Fisher 525F" at 834. What lost: a Quotes group that knew one of the boat's two names; a mark that needed the whole line; one chip saying the name twice.
- **A boat the price file holds at nought has no price, and the build is where one is put on (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md blocker 1: the picker marked all nine Haines Signature models "No price on file" and promised "you put the price on it", the build had nowhere to put one and read the hull at $0, and the given paper for a Haines Signature Fisher 525F printed "01 The hull $0 · Included" with "Your price" the trailer alone — because the hull froze at `unitPrice: 0` and `readDocument` reads a frozen 0 as the file stating no charge. Measured on the file: of 810 boat rows, 792 carry a figure and no nought, 18 (nine Haines Signature, nine Formosa) carry 0 in every rung and every cost column, none mixes the two. `src/domain/quote/nought.ts` `boatRungs` freezes a boat register's nought as no figure where `mintLine` and `priceChanges` freeze a row's rungs, so the build's chapter, the finale's count, the cascade, the paper and "Your price" all say "Not priced on this quote" from one fact; every other register's nought still reads Included. Chapter 01 opens first on such a quote and offers "The boat's price, tax included" with "Put this price on the boat" — `setOverride`, the engine's own command with its inverse (the step line's Undo takes it off; the clearing sentence now says "has no price on this quote again" where there was never a file price), the reason recorded as the fact that offered the act ("The price file holds no price for this boat."), a blank, a word or a nought refused with a sentence under the field (`readHullPrice`). `issueBlockers` refuses a quote whose boat carries no price (refusal 5, said only where the nought-total refusal has not already said it). The customer's copy prints a price put on an unpriced line as the line's price with no "Price agreed at" bargain and no "price file"; the dealer's note says "priced by hand at $X — the price file holds no price for this boat". The masthead says "The boat has no price yet, so this is everything but the boat" until it has one, and the finale no longer claims every price is the file's once the boat's is his. Golden untouched and green (the Stacer 529 carries figures). Pinned: `nought.test.ts` (12, on the real file), `paper.test.ts`, `say.test.ts`, `Configurator.test.tsx` (5) and `document.spec` at six sizes (picked, priced at the build, given, and the paper read). What lost: freezing a boat's rungs exactly as the cell reads (a Haines hull's frozen rungs now say null where the file says 0 — deliberately, since the picker and the engine's own refusal 3 already read that nought as "we do not know"), and drafts minted before today, which keep the 0 they froze.
- **The build moves on to what is left, never back to the hull, and brings it to the hand (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md major 3: from the picker (no `?at=`) the ADV7 in B-G-B opens on 02 Motor, the one band the picker leaves empty; pressing the F250XCB left no band empty, the reading fell back to chapters[0], the motor list folded and "ADV7 — 7 finishes" opened under the hand at 1440, 834 and 390 — and, measured before the fix, the pressed tile left the keyboard's focus on the page's body at every size. The open chapter is now `chapterToOpen` (`src/domain/quote/opening.ts`, pure, 11 cases): the address's chapter, else a given quote's finale, else an unpriced boat's hull, else the first empty band with something to offer, else Who it is for while nobody is named, else the finale; chapter one only for a build with neither closing chapter. So the sale reads motor → name → finale: after the motor Who it is for opens, after "Address this quote" the finale does. When a write moves the open chapter (address naming none, no search running), `useOnward` brings the new chapter in by the least move — its head clear of the masthead and, at a desk, the sticky search field (measured where they stick, written as `--cfg-cover`), its foot clear of the phone's tab bar (`--shell-foot`, as `scroll-margin`) — and a chapter taller than that room by its head (the least move alone left the finale's head under the masthead at 844 × 390); smooth, instant under reduced motion; where the press left the focus nowhere it goes to that chapter's head. Measured after on the dev server: Who it is for open and whole in the window at 1440 (511–892 of 900), 834 × 1112 (723–1104 of 1112), 390 × 844 (360–780, above the tab bar), 1280 and 1920; its head at 169 of 390 at 844 × 390. A chapter the dealer opened himself (`?at=`) stays open under every press, as before. Pinned: `opening.test.ts`, `onward.test.tsx` (6, on the real pack: moves on, focus, finale after the name, Undo reopens the motor, a pinned chapter stays) — 7 of their cases fail with the old fallback — and `configurator.spec` "pressing a motor moves the build on…" at six sizes, which asks the page what is under the head's and the act's centre. What lost: the rule "else chapter one", and a screen that never scrolls on its own; a dealer adding several dealer-fit parts to a build that opened on an empty Dealer fit sees it fold after the first and presses its head once to keep it open — the engine has no "one of" to tell a motor from a part, and none is invented here.

**A motor, a trailer and a kit are said as a person says them, on the build, the cascade and the paper (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md major 4: page 2 of the ADV7's paper printed "Yamaha - F250XCB", "REDCO Custom / Highfield ADV7 Aluminium - TA700T-EH" and "6X6 Sng Key Switch", and the build's row "Yamaha - F250XCB F250XCB" — the file's key string, then the Model Code column's same six characters. `lineSaid` (`src/domain/quote/spoken.ts`, pure) now joins the file's own words the way `spokenBoat` joins a hull's: a kit's "|" is " · "; the file's " - " is " · ", except that where the one word before it is the maker the line's own register is named for ("Yamaha" of Yamaha Outboards, "EPROPULSION" of ePropulsion Outboards) the two are one name, "Yamaha F250XCB", and a hyphen between two figures stays a range ("4.9 - 5.3 m"); a bracket that ends a part is said after it ("Yamaha F40SA · Tiller"); a figure and its unit are set apart ("8.0 m", "1,300 kg", "70 HP"); a Highfield code inside a trailer's name is said from the same ledger entry as the hull ("REDCO Custom / Highfield Sport 560 Aluminium · TA600-MOB" beside the "Highfield Sport 560"); and a shortened word the FILE ITSELF also writes in full is written in full from a new `words` section of `data/northside/names.json`, each with its cells — "Sng" is "Single" (Dealer Fit Module!R2177 "Sng Eng" beside R428 "Single Engine"; the single-engine kit's "6X6 Sng Key Switch" R429 beside the twin's "6Y8 Twin Key Switch" R394) and "Mnt" is "Mount" (R2177 writes "6x9 Side Mnt" and "6X9 DEC Flush Side Mount Control Box" in one row). No word is added, dropped or guessed: "6X6", "CL5" and "16 Pin" are part names and stay, and "Alum" or "Surt", which the file never writes out, stay as written. `codeBeside` prints a dealer's code only where the name does not already carry it, so the build's and the cascade's motor rows say "Yamaha F250XCB" once while a kit keeps "6XB-CL51L-00-08"; `saidOnQuote` is the one name for any line of a quote — the hull as the boat, a picked line with its register, a typed line exactly as typed — and the paper, the build's rows, chapter heads, step line, stage caption, "already has registration in it", "2 lines from …", the finale's typed-price refusal and the cascade's rows and alternatives all ask it; a pairing's facts under a build row are said the same way ("Helm Master L2 · 6X9 Binnacle"), and the build's reason for a row off the list names the hull as the boat ("… does not pair it with Highfield ADV7 · Hypalon · Black / Grey / Black"), not by the file's key. Measured over the file: all 4,234 distinct motor, trailer and kit labels (241 motors, 439 trailers, 3,554 parts and kits) said with no pipe, no key " - " and no word that is not the file's or the ledger's (`spoken.test.ts`); the ADV7's paper, build and cascade driven at six sizes (`document.spec`). What lost: "only the separators moved" as the paper's contract for these lines (its test is rewritten to the new one), a maker joined to its model without the register saying so ("Battery - AT12260D" is "Battery · AT12260D"), and the finder, which still shows and matches the file's strings for motors and trailers — that is the shell's.
- **One price level, one name, on every dealer screen (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md major 5: the price file writes one level under a different column in almost every list (a boat's cash price is `Cash`, a motor's `Sell Price`, a trailer's `Sell inc Rego`, a part's `Sell`; trade is `Trade` and `Trade Price`), and the screens printed the column, so one ADV7 quote showed four level names: the build's "$31,850 at Sell Price", the cascade's "Sell Price → Trade Price", "now priced at Trade Price" beside "now priced at Trade" as two headings over one decision, and "it stays at Sell inc Rego", the finale's "is priced at Sell inc Rego, and that number already has registration in it", beside the declared "Cash" and "Trade". `src/domain/quote/levelSaid.ts` says a level by the name declared for its KEY (`LEVEL_TITLE`: Cash, Trade, Warranty, Fitted), and by the file's own column only where a key has no declared name; `fileLevelNames` reads the columns that are said another way off the declared ladders (measured: Sell inc Rego, Sell Price, Trade Price, Sell, Sell inc Install (if appl.)), so the guard follows the file. The build's rows read "$31,850 at Cash" and a row with an empty cell "No Cash price"; the cascade reads "Cash → Trade", one "now priced at Trade" heading over the hull and the motor, and "no Trade price on the price file — it stays at its Cash price"; the finale reads "Yamaha F250XCB already has pre-delivery in its Cash price"; `DocumentLine.rung` is the declared name its own comment always claimed. What the charge is inside a price is still said (registration, pre-delivery), because that is the fact the column name was carrying. The engine's own sentences (`conflict.ts`, `cascade.ts`, `chargeAlreadyInSentence`) and their ported tests are untouched: the screens match them EXACTLY and fall back to them, as `heldSay` already did. Pinned: `levelSaid.test.ts` (6, on the real file), `proposal.test.ts` (an SP560 with its motor and trailer moved to Trade: no file column in any heading or row, one moving heading), `say.test.ts`, `Document.test.tsx`. What lost: the column a figure was read from on the dealer's screens — it is the file's bookkeeping, and the sheet under Data still shows every column by its own name.
- **The register, History and the note beside the paper speak the dealer's words (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md major 5. Quotes' and History's heads count the file as Home and Entry do: "53 lists · 15,691 lines", "53 lists" (were "53 tables · 15,691 rows"). The register's panel says "1 quote, by where it stands." (was "1 quote, in three bands.") and "The figure above is its total, at the prices it was written at." / "Each figure above adds up the quotes in that group…" (was "…the sum of that document's own frozen lines"); its help says "Press Space on a quote" and "this list" (was "a row", "the register"); the empty state says "the list's three groups" and "the last line of the list". The peek says "Priced at · Cash" (was "RUNG cash"), "4, 1 of them not priced on this quote" (was "carrying no price", now the paper's own words) and "It opens as the sheet the customer was given, exactly as it was given" (was "the same frozen lines"). The note beside the paper: "Why a line reads as it does" names a line as the paper does (the kit's " · ", never its pipes) and says "the price file has no price for it at any level, so it is not in the total" (the cascade's own words; was "this register carries no price column at all"); "Priced at · Cash, on 3 of the 4 lines." (was "Cash — 3 of the 4 lines carry that rung."); the workshop's facts part a kit's pieces with " · " and the facts with ";"; and "Pair it on the subject's own page and it shows here." is gone — it names a page this app does not have, in the engine's word for a hull, and the build dropped it for the same reason (M2-close critique #4). History says "as it was written" for "as it was frozen", and its two stranded sentences say "Quotes" for "the quotes register". The engine's `DocumentTable.next` and `document.ts`'s reasons are untouched; the note translates them by exact match (`deskWhy`) and falls back to them. Pinned: `Quotes.test.tsx` (the panel empty, at rest and on a draft's and a given quote's peek, against `ENGINE_WORDS`), `History.test.tsx` (the head and an opened line), `Document.test.tsx` (the ADV7's and the Stacer 529's note: no engine word, no file column, no pipe), `paper.test.ts` (5). What lost: "row" and "register" as this screen's own nouns for its lines and itself.
- **A given quote states its level; it offers no other (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md minor 14: "See what Trade does" stood on every given quote as a refused control under "This quote has been given to the customer, so nothing can go back on it." The level a quote was given at is a fact, not a decision still open, so on a given quote the build's "Priced at" says the level and its count ("Cash · 3 of 4 lines") and nothing else — no refused offer, no second copy of the issued sentence, no note about re-pricing. The way on is a new version, which the build's head says. - **A name never opens a line on its own separator (2026-09-24).** m2-last-critique.md minor 8: at 834 the cascade's card read "Highfield ADV7 · Hypalon / · Black / Grey / Black" and at 1440 the register's peek "…Black / Grey / / Black". `src/domain/quote/wrap.ts` `keepSeparators` puts a no-break space BEFORE each spaced "·" and "/", so the separator travels with the word it follows and a line can only break after it; the words are unchanged and every whitespace-blind reading (search, find in page, a screen reader, Playwright's text matching) reads the name as before. Used on the cascade's card and its rows and the register's peek; the picker's chips are the picker's. Considered and lost: spans with `white-space: nowrap` around each "word + separator" (the DOM text is split across elements, so every `getByText` over a name stops matching); and CSS alone, which has no rule for "never break before this character". Pinned: `wrap.test.ts` (3), `Quotes.test.tsx` (the peek's heading is the name with its no-break spaces).

- **The picker's act waits in the foot's blue, and is amber only when it can act (2026-09-25).** The M2-close-2 critique's minor 21, repeated as m2-last-critique.md minor 11: on a many-colour plate `Start the quote` rested aria-disabled in the refused amber, the loudest thing on the plate before a colour was chosen, when amber on this screen means "press this". While the act waits, its block (`.picker-act[data-waits]`) re-declares the primitive's two refused-act tokens — `--color-act-refused` as blue-100, one step down from the foot's blue-50, and `--color-ink-on-act-refused` as the plate's second ink — so the act keeps its place, size, name, focus and sentence, nothing moves when it comes live, and the question (the chips above) is what the eye meets. Measured: the whole page read at 4.5 : 1 or better by day and by night with the act waiting (`picker.spec.ts`, the contrast reader), and the act's fill is the act's amber the moment a colour is pressed. Considered and lost: switching the act to `secondary` while it waits (36px against 52, left-aligned, so the foot moves under the finger when it comes live); drawing no act until a colour is chosen (the act's place jumps, and a keyboard reader loses the control and its reason). What lost: the refused amber at rest on this screen; the build's finale keeps its own.
- **On a tablet held upright the colours are named on their chips; on a phone they are not yet (2026-09-25).** m2-last-critique.md minor 11: "at 834 and 390 the chips are swatches alone", and a finger has no hover to name a chip before it chooses it. From 834px wide in a window 900px tall or more, a plate 18rem or wider names every colour the decode names beside its swatches, one chip to a row at 834 (the ADV7's act at 934–986 of 1,112, its name and seven named chips above it, measured).
