What lost: nothing on a desk; the tablet's one-line row of unnamed swatches.
- **No register answers a single character, and no keycap is drawn on the registers (2026-09-25).** m2-last-critique.md major 7 (the specification's major 11): Data, History, Quotes and Customers taught 25, 23, 19 and 17 keycaps at a desk, the Quotes panel spent two paragraphs on why a single letter does nothing unless the list has the focus, and J, K, N, V, Q, B, T, W, M, Y and `/` still owed WCAG 2.2 SC 2.1.4. The owner's words were "i can't stress enough how easy this system has to be to use". Every single-character shortcut is gone from the four registers and the sheet: what is left is what every list already has and the criterion does not ask about — the arrows, Home and End, Space (read here; on the register held, to glance), Enter (open), Escape (step back) — and on the sheet Shift Space takes a row (a spreadsheet's own; it was X), `[` still shuts the block the cursor is in (an act on the focused grid itself), Mod D and Mod Z are unchanged, and a letter typed on a cell starts an edit as on every spreadsheet. No legend, no cap on an act, no `/` beside a find field: each register says how to open a line or a quote in one sentence a finger and a mouse share ("Press a line to open it where it is…"), at every size. The `?` sheet (`src/screens/shell/vocabulary.ts`) lists the keys that remain, with ↑ ↓ for J K. Pinned: each screen's component test presses the retired letters and asserts nothing happened and no `kbd` is drawn; `quotes.spec`, `history.spec` and `data.spec` read 0 visible caps at six sizes. What lost: the Linear vocabulary the sweep took for the registers, and the sentence that justified it by the criterion's "active only on focus" exemption. Not this change's: the shell's global G-then-letter chord (`Shell.tsx`), which is the one character shortcut left in the app and answers on every screen.
- **The sheet names a model the way every other screen does, and finds it by that name (2026-09-25).** m2-last-critique.md major 7: "RU230KAM is named by code on its spine while every other screen says 'Roll Up 230 KAM'". `src/domain/catalogue/views/spineSaid.ts` `spineName` asks the one namer (`spokenModel`) and re-says a model only where the maker's own page names the file's code, so the spine, a shut line, Only the models and the Pictures door read "Roll Up 230 KAM", "Sport 560", "Patrol 600 ST", with the file's code said quietly beside the count on an open spine at a desk ("RU230KAM · 4 variants") for the dealer who orders by it — not on a shut line or a hand's head, whose name column is a third of the list and was cut by it at 834 ("Sport 700 WL (Windlass) SP700WL(Windlass)"); ADV7 stays ADV7 (its page says so), SP300 stays SP300 (no page names it), and a register that names its models in words keeps the file's own string, because the sheet is where the file is read and corrected. The find field now finds what the spine prints: `alsoBySaid` adds the rows whose model is said in words holding the typed phrase to what the file's own search found ("roll up 230" finds RU230KAM and RU230AL), the fault the finder and Home had (blocker 2). - **A horsepower range says its unit once, after its figures, and a spine says a column's name once (2026-09-25).** A fresh critic on the sheet raised m2-last-critique.md minor 10 ("Max 250 HP HP" on the ADV7's spine) again; its single-fact path was already answered above ("Four small ones"), and in the built app at 1440 × 900 and 834 × 1112 the ADV7's spine reads "Max 250 HP" (at 390 it stands behind "+12 more"). What lost: "450–450 / 2 x 300 HP" on the ADV9 and "2 x 250–2 x 300 HP" on PA860EW and PA860ST, which now read "450 HP–450 / 2 x 300 HP" and "2 x 250 HP–2 x 300 HP", each end as the file typed it.

The chord is gone from `Shell.tsx`, its line from the `?` sheet, and the `key` from every door in `src/app/ways.ts`. Finder rows now carry no key (`FinderRow.key`, `FinderDoor.key` and `FinderAct.key` removed, the row's grid four columns, not five). Pinned: `shell.spec` "no letter is a door, with or without a G in front of it" (G D C leaves the address where it was; the finder's door row is there with no `kbd` on any row) at six sizes. What stays: `Mod K`, `?` (the sheet, desk only) and `/` into the field on Home and the build — punctuation, refused inside a field, still owed under 2.1.4, and left for the owner to rule on. What lost: Linear's go-to chord, and the seven caps the finder printed on its doors and acts.

- **The audit's states are photographed on the running build and measured against rest (2026-09-28).** `vite preview` on 6201, one Chromium at 1440 × 900 walking the real door, file and sale; frames at CSS scale, crops at device scale 2; a press is held and dragged off the control so nothing acts; focus is keyboard focus. The ground is soft light, so a quarter of the pixels is enough. A phone's GPU was not measured. Lost: a sharper ground nobody could see was sharper.

- **The kit boards are assembled by a scratch builder that is not committed (2026-09-28).** `docs/directions/components-boards/.tmp/` holds the sources, the builder (the token block copied from tokens.css, springs sampled, Phosphor paths copied from node_modules, and a guard refusing a literal or named colour outside the token block, an undeclared token and type under 11px) and the measuring scripts (text contrast on the real pixels, overlap, cost). The committed boards are self-contained and are edited as boards, like every earlier round's. Lost: rebuilding a board from its source after this session.
- **The component kit is board C, "Signal", built into src/ui with board A's water under the band (2026-09-28).** The judge's pick (owner 8, dealer 9, designer 6) and its graft: colour and icon, blue and white, round white plates that are the day wherever they stand, a Phosphor glyph on every act, chip, segment, row and toast, and the amber act ending on its glyph in a dark disc — with board A's live WebGL water as the one living ground, under Northside's name only. `src/styles/tokens.css` "THE KIT" holds every material, light, radius, glyph size and the motion vocabulary with its source; `/kit` draws all of it. Lost: board C's 800 weight on every word (names are 600–700, the price 700), its faded marquee of makers' marks under the name (the judge: it competed with the showpiece, added a tab stop and cut marks at the edges), and its static grain as the band's only ground.
- **Every ink has one job, and a token was written only after its job (2026-09-28).** The judge's one change to board C: the accent now means where you are and what is chosen (the chosen ring, tick and chip, the lit door, the current row, the band); the amber is the one thing you press; the ink as a fill is a firm act that is not the one thing (`primary`, navy by day and white by night); a kind wears its fixed ink; a quote's state wears its own. Lost: board C's blue `primary` and blue boat beside a blue "chosen", and its draft dot in the act's own amber.
- **A boat is cobalt, no longer the accent's blue (2026-09-28).** `--accent-blue` named blue-700, 0.078 from the accent in OKLab, so a boat's glyph and a chosen ring read as one blue. It is now `--color-cobalt-700`, oklch(38% 0.14 268): 0.137 from the accent, 9.07 : 1 on the ground and 10.36 : 1 on a plate; the night keeps blue-300. The kinds' inks are named steps (`--color-carmine-600` / `-300` and their siblings), the same bytes as before, so THE NIGHT and THE PLATE move an alias and never retype a value. Lost: a boat that moved when the accent moved.
- **A quote's state has its own inks: draft rose, given leaf, superseded graphite (2026-09-28).** `--status-draft` oklch(52% 0.17 350), `--status-given` oklch(50% 0.13 135), `--status-superseded` the counted ink — hues chosen so no two jobs share one (carmine 22, act 73, ochre 95, leaf 135, viridian 163, teal 197, accent 256, cobalt 268, violet 300, rose 350), each always a dot or glyph beside its word (`StatusDot`, `BandHead`). Measured: rose 5.31 : 1 on the ground and 6.06 on a plate, leaf 5.01 and 5.72; at night 8.09 and 9.29 on the ground. Lost: draft, given and superseded in one navy, and board C's draft in the act's amber.
- **Northside's accent is refused, with a sentence, when it would take another ink's job (2026-09-28).** `accentRefusal` in `src/domain/appearance/accent.ts` refuses a grey (chroma under 0.05), a colour white cannot stand on at 4.5 : 1, a hue within 15° of the act's amber or of a kind's or a state's ink, and anything within 0.09 of one in OKLab — the boat's cobalt is guarded by nearness, because it is set apart from the accent by lightness, which is what keeps the default blue allowed. Its reserved inks are pinned to tokens.css byte for byte by its test. The settings panel is Milestone 4's; `/kit` lets a person try the file's blue, plum and rust, and shows amber and carmine refused with their reasons. Lost: an accent taken as given.
- **A white plate is the day wherever it stands, by a ground attribute, and the night is a ground as well as a theme (2026-09-28).** `[data-ground='plate']` (tokens.css, THE PLATE) sets every role back to the day's step by name, and `[data-ground='night']` wears THE NIGHT on one element; every primitive that IS a white surface (plate, card and chip tiles, a row on paper, option tile, chip, segment, popup, dialog) carries the first, the toast the second. Because a custom property naming another resolves where it is declared, each scope re-declares the aliases it moves (`--color-ink-reason` among them), and the day's literal lines are named once (`--day-wash`, `--day-rule`, `--day-rule-strong`, `--day-edge`, `--day-act-ink`). Lost: a kind's night ink drawn on a white plate at 2.3 : 1.
- **A primitive reads its own shape tokens, so a screen can no longer square one (2026-09-28).** Nine screens set `--radius-sm: 0px` in their own scope and forked the Button and the Input through the cascade (audit.md defect 3). The kit's primitives read `--radius-pill`, `--radius-plate`, `--radius-field`, `--radius-tile`, `--radius-glyph` and `--radius-key`, which no screen sets, and their heights did not move (28, 36 and the act's 52), so no register loses a row to a new button. The screens' own overrides are the adopters' to delete; a guard refusing a screen that redefines a primitive's token is owed and not written, because it would be red on nine screens today. Lost: square buttons on nine screens.
- **An overlay's stacking is on its positioner (2026-09-28).** `.ui-popover`, `.ui-select-popup`, `.ui-menu` and `.ui-tooltip` carried `z-index: 60` on a static popup, where it did nothing: the sheet's "26 more" opened under the pill and the Select's list in Data's "A new register" under its own dialog (audit.md defect 1). Every positioner now wears `.ui-positioner`, z-index 70, above a dialog's 60 whatever order their portals were appended in. Lost: the z-index that was never applied.
- **Motion has one vocabulary, in tokens.css and in src/ui/motion.ts, and one gate (2026-09-28).** press 100, hover 150, enter 200, exit 140 (exits faster than entries), route 220 ms; settle, travel and morph are critically damped springs of response 0.2, 0.3 and 0.4 s, sampled once into `--ease-spring` (they settle in 294, 441 and 588 ms) and handed to `motion` as stiffness ω² and damping 2ω — `motion.tokens.test.ts` reads the stylesheet and fails if the two drift. `MotionRoot`, mounted once at the root, tells `motion` `reducedMotion="user"`, and `"always"` while a caret is in a text field, and writes `data-still` on <html> for the CSS and the water. Lost: `spring.press / pop / open / layout / drawer` in motion.ts (the CSS ladder's names stay).
- **A new screen crossfades in, and a shared picture morphs, on a change of screen only (2026-09-28).** `defaultViewTransition` in `src/app/router.ts` starts the browser's View Transition when the PATH changes and not a search param (a register's cursor, `?at=`, `?brand=` are positions inside a screen, and a crossfade per arrow key would animate a keyboard action), never while a caret is in a field and never under reduced motion. The root fades over 220 ms (`src/ui/route.css`); a `Picture` given `shared="boat-<Model Code>"` morphs on the 588 ms spring. Only a picture is ever named, so no words and no price travel; a price is in the root's fade and simply in its new place. The picker, the build and the paper are the adopters' to name. Lost: a route that cut.
- **The water caps luminance, not each channel (2026-09-28).** Board A held white at 5.1 : 1 by capping each sRGB channel at 0.43, which suits a navy ground and turns a blue band teal wherever the blue channel clips. `src/ui/water.ts` mixes in linear light and scales each pixel so its luminance is at most 0.13, the accent's own (blue-600 is 0.132), keeping its hue: white on the brightest ripple is at least 5.8 : 1 and blue-50 at least 5.2 : 1 whatever Northside's accent. One still frame under reduced motion; no frames while the tab is hidden, the band is off the screen or a caret is in a field; not drawn under reduced transparency, where the grained gradient stands. Lost: board A's 0.43-per-channel cap.
- **GSAP's ScrollTrigger and Lenis are imported from '@/ui/scroll', never from '@/ui' (2026-09-28).** `useChapterProgress` reads which chapter the window's middle is in and how far through (a position, so it runs under reduced motion too) and `Dashes` draws it as Saxdor's foot pill whose dash fills; `useSmoothScroll` smooths the wheel on a fine pointer only, never under reduced motion, and stops while a caret is in a field. Kept out of the index so a screen that draws a button does not download them; `/kit` uses both today and the build is the adopters'. Lost: the two libraries' 0 imports.
- **The one authored moment is the band's reveal in CSS; Rive and Lottie are not installed (2026-09-28).** An authored Rive or Lottie moment needs a file drawn in its editor, and none exists; a hand-typed Lottie JSON would be the library for its own sake at 46 KB gzip, and Rive's runtime is 94 KB of script and 360 KB of WASM (measured 2026-09-28). Northside's name rises out of a blur, its two lines 90 ms apart, once per mount — board C's reveal — and when an authored file exists, the band is where it plays. Lost: a Rive state machine for the mark and for "given".
- **The kit's specimen at /kit reads five tables of the file itself and keeps nothing (2026-09-28).** Every other screen reads this browser's copy and only Entry's door reads the file; the kit has to draw on real content cold, so `src/routes/kit.tsx` fetches the Stacer register, its two pairing tables, the Yamaha and Stacer Trailers registers, the table definitions and the picture ledger, and `specimenOf` (`src/domain/kit/specimen.ts`) reads the 529 and its siblings through the declared price levels only, each hull with its own pairings. It asks for no name, writes nothing, and joins `e2e/routes.ts` as a foundation route. Lost: a specimen that needed the door first.
- **The Toaster, the tooltip delay and the motion gate are mounted once, at the root (2026-09-28).** Sonner was wrapped and mounted nowhere, so no toast could ever be seen; it is now one stack in `src/routes/__root.tsx`, drawn headless (`toast.custom`) as board C's navy capsule with the kind's glyph and a white Undo, the night wherever it stands. `say` and `undo` keep their signatures and take an optional kind. Lost: an `undo()` nobody could see.
- **A picture is fetched at once unless it asks to be lazy (2026-09-28).** `Picture` draws `loading="eager"` by default and `lazy` only when a long list says so: a lazy picture far below the fold on a phone is never asked for, so the rulers' `settle`, which waits for every picture to decode, waited out its 30 s on /kit at 390, 844 and 834 wide. An option tile's picture is decorative (`alt=""`), because inside a button a picture's words open the button's name before the option's own. Lost: lazy loading as the default.
- **A segmented choice is real radio buttons under a legend; a toggle is a button with the switch role (2026-09-28).** The browser then owes the segment its arrow keys and entering on the chosen answer, and a refused answer is still reached and says why without moving the choice; its thumb travels by `motion`'s shared layout. The toggle is a plain `<button role="switch">` in a `<label>`, so its word presses it. Lost: a hand-written roving tab index.
- **Motion is skipped under test (2026-09-28).** `src/test/setup.ts` sets `MotionGlobalConfig.skipAnimations`, so every `motion` component lands at once in a component test: a test asks what a person can reach and read, and happy-dom rejects a cancelled animation's `finished` promise, which `motion` leaves unhandled when a test unmounts a chapter mid-open. Lost: nothing a test asserted.
- **A veiled, a secondary and the one act carry the day's inks inside them (2026-09-28).** Found by the desktop contrast walk of every route after the kit landed: a veiled act is now a white plate in both themes, and the screens' own words inside two of them were set in the room's roles — Customers' door count "1" in the night's third ink at 2.46 : 1 and Lost's "Start a quote" white on white at night. `Button` marks those three intents `data-ground='plate'`, so what a screen sets inside one reads the day's roles; a refused veiled door keeps its white plate. The walk then read 0 below threshold on every route by day and by night, and cut and overlap 0 on every route. Lost: the night's inks inside a white button.
- **The shell speaks the kit (2026-09-28).** Every door on the pill carries its Phosphor glyph beside its word, one glyph per place in `src/screens/shell/glyphs.ts` that the finder's rows and the dead end's door read too (a test fails a door added without one, or two places sharing one); the lit door is one accent capsule that travels between doors on the travel spring (`motion`'s shared layout), drawn as the band's gradient so the contrast ruler reads the white word on its pixels; counts stand in the kit's small well; the magnifier replaced `⌕`; a press sinks to .97. On a phone a tab is its glyph over its word, and the count is a dot on the glyph's shoulder only when a draft waits. Measured: 701px at 1280–1920 (was 558), 645 at 834 and 844 × 390, 358 × 42 at 390 with scrollWidth equal to clientWidth, still 42 tall and ending at 50 under the 56 clearance everywhere. Lost: words alone on a bar the owner called bland.
- **The crest is the band in a roundel, and the flag flies into it (2026-09-28).** The pill's crest wears the kit's live water (`Water`) under the accent deepened across it, the initials rising out of a blur once when the file names the business; Entry's pennant wears the same water and the same view-transition name, so opening the file morphs the flag into the roundel on the route's crossfade. The crest takes the name in `shell.css` and gives it up while a flag is on the page (`:has([data-mark='flag'])`), because the pill is drawn for `/` before Entry leaves and the two together made the browser refuse the whole transition ("Snapshot capture failed", measured); `mark.test.ts` holds the two spellings together. Lost: a flat accent disc, and a flight that never flew.
- **The finder opens without an entrance from the keyboard (2026-09-28).** Ctrl K and Home's Enter open it at once; a press on the pill's bubble hangs it down from the pill in `enter` (on a phone it rises from the bar), and it always closes at once. A palette pressed a hundred times a day must be there when the keys come up (emil-design-eng: never animate a keyboard-initiated act). Its groups lead with the kit's `KindMark` where they hold one kind and with a glyph where they do not; door and act rows lead with the place's glyph; the cursor row is the kit's current line; Close is the kit's quiet capsule. Lost: an entrance on every Ctrl K.
- **Entry fades in; nothing on it travels while its field holds the caret (2026-09-28).** The field autofocuses and nothing moves while a caret is in a field, so on arrival the flag, the card, the door and the evidence fade a press apart and the name comes out of a blur, and the flag's water waits (one still frame) until the caret leaves. The card is the kit's `Plate`, the honest line its pale well with an open lock, the door carries the file's drum and ends on a white disc that nudges forward, the name's refusal is the kit's `Refusal`, the steps tick with a turning notch and a check on the settle spring, and the field is read-only while the file is read — the name given is the one typed, and with no caret on the page the crossfade and the flag's flight run. Lost: a pennant on a flat fill, the glass card, and a board that claimed no motion.
- **The dead end writes its address in, and its thrown rule is no longer amber (2026-09-28).** The specimen is a plate whose address reveals a character a step over one `morph`; the band arrives head to foot a press apart (there is no field on this screen, so it may travel); the act ends on the kit's disc and the door leads with the glyph the finder gives starting a quote. The rule beside a screen's thrown words was the act's amber, which the kit reserves for the one thing a person presses; it is the room's strong rule, led by the refusal's warning glyph. Lost: `&rarr;` characters and an amber that meant two things.
- **The picker carries a maker's mark by the browser's own View Transition, and never a card's photograph (2026-09-28).** A door's mark lands at the head of its maker's boats (a rail row's the same way, and back to its door on All makers): `src/routes/quote.new.tsx` asks for the transition only on a change of maker, never on a boat, a material or a colour, under reduced motion or with a caret in a field (`canMorph`), and the picker names, for one frame, only the mark pressed (`Lift` in Picker.tsx). Measured in Chromium at 1440 and 390: the mark in flight at 60 ms and landed by 180 ms, no duplicate-name refusal. A card's photograph flew to the plate too until the flows were run: a transition holds the new page back until the old one is photographed, the build's, the cascade's and the paper's walks press a material the instant the card lands, and on the laptop and the wide screen the plate was not there yet, so no quote was started (three failures in one run). Lost: the card's photograph landing on the plate — the plate fades in, and the photograph travels once, on the act — a name on every card's picture, and the plate's 12 px slide.
- **A boat's photograph travels under the row its quote is written against (2026-09-28).** `boatTravel(table, row)` in `src/screens/picker/travel.ts` is `sharedName('boat-<table id>-<row id>')`; the picker's plate names its photograph by the version it shows and the build's stage by `rootTableId`/`rootRowId`, and the build imports the spelling rather than typing it again, as it imports the cascade's `fix` grammar. So the act carries the plate's photograph onto the stage across the route's crossfade (a test reads the name off the quote the act really filed). Where the build puts its stage below the rail — 390 × 844 and 844 × 390 — the stage takes no name and the photograph fades with the page in the route's 220 ms (`view-transition-class`, `:only-child`) instead of flying off the bottom of the window. Lost: the kit's `boat-<Model Code>` spelling, since a quote carries its row and not its code.
- **The build's chapter head is the screen's own, drawn in the kit's chapter language (2026-09-28).** A disc with the chapter's kind in its own ink (`KindMark`: hull cobalt, motor carmine, trailer ochre, dealer fit viridian), a person and a chequered flag in the plate's well for the two closing chapters, the engine's number in its own column so the names stand in line, the subtotal in the kit's bold figure and a caret in a well that turns over. It is not the `Chapter` primitive because that primitive draws a dash where this head says "Nothing on it yet" or "Not priced on this quote", is not a heading, and toggles itself where a search opens every chapter with an answer at once — all three said in notChanged for the kit. Lost: the typed "+" and "–", and the primitive itself on the build.
- **A chapter opens out of its head, and the build waits for it before bringing it in (2026-09-28).** The body grows by height and opacity on the travel spring and closes on the exit tween; under reduced motion or a caret in a field it only fades; on a given quote it is drawn without motion at all, because every figure on it is final. The onward move after a write now brings the next chapter into the window after `settlesIn(0.3)`, 441 ms, since measured at the press the opening chapter is a head and no more; and it takes the keyboard off a control that is folding away inside another chapter, which a spring keeps in the page for a moment (the first run lost the focus to the body). The waiting move is cancelled only by the next move or by leaving the screen, because the same press re-renders the build before the chapter has opened. Where the chapter opens at once (reduced motion, a caret in a field) the one it folds away leaves at once too, and the move waits 100 ms for it to be gone: brought in while that body was still in the page, the chapter was moved 556 px out of the window when the body went (measured at 390 × 844 under reduced motion; after, the window stands at 525 with Who it is for at 356 and its act at 671–723, clear of the tab bar at 794, with motion and without). Lost: an instant open, and "only when the focus is on the body".
- **The build takes GSAP's ScrollTrigger and leaves the kit's Dashes and Lenis until they change (2026-09-28).** `useChapterProgress` drives a reading ring on the disc of the chapter the middle of the window is in, from a component that draws nothing, so the per-frame answer never re-renders the rail; its triggers are measured again once a change has settled and the page has stood still for 200 ms, never on every frame — a refresh puts the page at 0 to measure and back, and refreshing on each frame of a chapter's growth stopped the build's own move to Who it is for dead (measured at 390 × 844: the window stayed at 0 with the chapter at 881; after, it moved to 525 with the chapter's act at 671–723, clear of the tab bar at 794). `Dashes` numbers chapters by their place, so on the build it would print "05 / 06 Who it is for" beside a head that carries no number on purpose (05 is Administration's) and "02 / 05 Trailer" beside "03 Trailer" on a quote with no motor. `useSmoothScroll` stops Lenis while a caret is in a field, and a stopped Lenis swallows the wheel: measured on /kit at 1440, a 600 px wheel moved the page 599 px, and with the caret in the find field it moved it 0 px — on the build, whose search field is the navigation, a dealer reading the results would be locked. Lost: the foot pill and the smoothed wheel on the build, owed to the kit (notChanged).
- **The build's sentences are led by the glyph of their kind, its rows by a tick, its level by the chosen capsule (2026-09-28).** A lock where a given quote shuts a list, an i where a list is explained, stacked lines where one table holds several, coins where a price already holds a charge, a warning where a press was refused — each in a 12 px well, where a 2 px coloured rule down the sentence's edge used to carry the kind by colour alone. - **The crest wears the mark's name only on the pill's first address (2026-09-28).** Named on every screen, the crest made every route crossfade a second group, which the kit's route.css runs for the 588ms morph instead of the room's 220ms, and a view transition holds the page's pointer for its whole length: the pill's five doors walked in 4.3–5.6 s against 1.8–2.0 s before (measured on one build with and without, the water making no difference). The cover photograph carries the build stage's `boatTravel` name, so the finale's picture lands on the cover (PLAN.md: "the finale's boat → the document's cover"). Everything is `@media screen`; print resets the clip and the animation, so a printer is handed whole pages whatever moment the dialogue opened at; reduced motion runs none of it. Lost: "Nothing on this screen animates".
- **The cascade clips its own inline overflow while the sheet arrives (2026-09-28).** The flows caught it at 1280, 1440 and 1920: measured on arrival, the page scrolled sideways by 48px, because the sheet stands 48px past the end edge for the length of its spring. `.csc` now sets `overflow-x: clip` — `clip` and not `hidden`, so the screen does not become a scroll container of its own — and the sheet comes in from behind the window's edge. Lost: nothing; the 48px were never meant to be reachable.
- **The quotes register speaks the kit, and the ledger stays a ledger (2026-09-28).** The ledger and the panel are plates (the panel's white, `--radius-plate`, `--shadow-plate`) where hairline boxes stood; a row is a rounded wash inside the plate with no hairline between two rows, and the row the panel is reading wears the kit's chosen wash and the accent's edge. Each band's state is the kit's `STATE_GLYPH` in the kit's `--status-*` ink — draft rose, issued leaf, superseded graphite — beside its word, with the count in a small well of the same ink; the band head is drawn by the screen in `BandHead`'s shape at 24px, because the primitive's 40px would cost three heads 48px of the eighteen rows owed at 1280×800. The three screen-local `--radius-*: 0px` overrides are deleted. Lost: the square cockpit, and the CSS ring, disc and barred ring.

Lost: "no transition on a row, no entrance on the panel".

The room's caption band wears the kit's band material (`--grain` over `--band`) rather than the flat accent, and the quote's standing on it is a capsule that only darkens the blue, so its white never reads under the band's own 5.77 : 1. Lost: the live water there — the judge scoped the water to the band Northside's name stands on.
- **History speaks the kit, and Today is where you are (2026-09-28).** The spans are the kit's `Chip`s (the chosen one filled with the accent); a quote's standing is the kit's `StatusDot`, so draft, given and replaced read in the same three inks the register and a customer's page wear, and the strand that ends in "issued" takes `--status-given` so a pip and a dot say "given" in one green; Today's node and today's fortnight tile wear the kit's chosen ring in the accent where they wore the act's amber, which is the one thing a person presses; the fold is a plate under its line with the accent's edge; the last step is the kit's note and a refusal the kit's `Refusal`. The three screen-local radius overrides are deleted. Lost: the amber node and the rule drawn under a pressed span.

Every keyframe fills `backwards` and moves `translate`, `scale` or `opacity`, so nothing is held on a line once it has landed and `motion`'s own `transform` for a line that later travels is never fought. The fold grows out of its line on `travel` and shuts on `exit`; a closing fold is out of the grid at once. Nothing runs under reduced motion or with a caret in a field. Lost: "only two things move".

Not changed: 19 pointer-state transforms in screens' own stylesheets (data, sheet, picker, quotes, lost, shell, configurator, entry), each its screen's and not measured here; the guard reads src/ui only.
- **One reader for every picture of a boat, and the paper's cover is the boat (2026-09-28, components critique blocker 3).** Measured before on a private build at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844, walking the Stacer 519 Sea Ranger SDF (Centre Console) from the picker to the paper: the picker and the build's stage drew `hero-images/stacer-519-sea-ranger-4ea6e75c.webp`, then the cascade said "No picture of this boat is held yet … Above it, Stacer's own mark" (no mark drawn at 390), Customers said "no photograph held", and the paper printed Stacer's logo on a pale box (`data-art="mark"`) beside a note saying "no copy of it is held here". The ledgers were right; seven screens each read them, and three asked less than the build: the paper and Customers read the catalogue ledger alone by the row's address, which for the 519 is not held, and the cascade matched a hero only where a name of the row EQUALLED its model, which "Stacer - 519 Sea Ranger SDF (Centre Console)" never does. `src/data/pictures.ts` now reads the three ledgers once and answers the build's ladder — the model on the water by `depictionOf`, then the row's own copy, then nothing — for a row or a quote (by its row where the catalogue is open, by its frozen label where it is not, which `pictures.test.ts` proves gives the same picture on every boat row and the same picture the stage draws), plus the maker's mark in the ink its ground needs or the reason in a sentence. The paper, the cascade and Customers ask it, and `document/art.ts` keeps only the paper's ladder; `cascade/ground.ts` and `customers/pictures.ts` are gone. Measured after: the paper's cover is the 519's photograph, whole, 571 × 378 of 2,560 × 1,694, the held copy and never a narrower one so the printer has every pixel, under the stage's travelling name, still inside page 1 of 2; the cascade's card and ground are the photograph (the row's own copy stays on the card where it is held, as the exact colourway); Customers' well is the photograph, filling it as a scene rather than letterboxed on the white a studio render sits on. A photograph of the MODEL says so on the customer's paper in one line, "Pictured: the Stacer 519 Sea Ranger SDF on the water, in its maker's own finish and rig.", as the stage's caption does and a brochure's "shown with optional equipment" does; the row's own copy is the exact boat and gets none. The cascade's "Above it, <maker>'s own mark" is said only where the mark is drawn (1280 and up). A flow walks the boat the ledgers measure to be photographed only on the water through the picker, the stage, the cascade, the paper and Customers and holds all five to the same bytes and the same view-transition name. The picker, Home, Quotes, the sheet and History still read the same ledgers for themselves by the same rule and agree today; folding them into the reader is theirs, not this round's, because other rounds are writing those screens now. Lost: three per-screen readers, the cascade's test that asserted the untrue sentence for the 519 (it now holds the Stacer 539 Rebel, which has no picture on either rung), and a cover that only ever showed the row's own render.
- **The press beating the lift is proved in a browser and guarded in the kit (2026-09-28, components critique major 6; tools and e2e, src/ui untouched).** The critique held the mouse on Entry's "Load the Master Price File" and the finale's "Give it to the customer" and read `translateY(-1px)` with `:active` true: the lift at (0,4,0) outranked the press at (0,3,0). The cascade was already mended in the tree by the round that closed blocker 4 (the lift names `:not(:active)`), but nothing proved it on those two controls or could see it come back: `movement-is-asked-for` is quiet when both movements sit inside `no-preference`, as they did. Measured on a private build at 1440 × 900: held, the door reads `matrix(0.985, 0, 0, 0.985, 0, 0)` with its shadow gone, and the act `matrix(0.97, 0, 0, 0.97, 0, 0)` with its lip and drop gone and one amber-500 edge left; under reduced motion both read `none` held and their shadow still sinks. `e2e/flows/press.spec.ts` holds both controls down with and without reduced motion and lets go elsewhere so the quote stays a draft; against a build with the critique's cascade planted back it fails at 1280, 1440 and 1920 with the door at scale 1 and rise −1. `tools/check.ts` gains `the-press-beats-the-lift`: a transform on the hovered element itself must name `:active` in its `:not()`, and a child's nudge or /kit's inert frozen hover is left alone (26 kit stylesheets, 0 failures). Its fixture plants the critique's cascade and fails on it, and dropping `:active` from the real button, option or tile lift fires it at lines 280, 63 and 85. `movement-is-asked-for` now shares its reader of declarations with it, and its own fixture is unchanged. Gate on 6322: the press and Entry flows 42 passed and 6 skipped; contrast, overlap, cut, ramp, focus and refusal on Entry, /kit and the build 63 passed, 51 skipped and 0 failed. Not measured: a phone's press. At 390 × 844 a held touch sends pointerdown and touchstart, but Chromium's emulation never sets `:active`, with raw touch events or a synthesized long tap, so the flow skips the touch projects and says why. Lost: folding the clause into `movement-is-asked-for`, which would have rewritten that rule's fixture assertions, and any claim about the press on a phone.
- **Home is in the kit's language: Northside's name on the live water, the room round and lit, every count a `Stat` (2026-09-28, components critique blocker 5; `src/screens/home` only, src/ui untouched).** The critique: "Typed '→' arrows on New quote and on both cards, six file counts set as plain text, and keycaps ('Ctrl K', '/') drawn at 390." Measured before, on this tree at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844: 3 typed arrows, 0 SVG glyphs and 0 canvases on Home, and both keycaps drawn at 390 wherever the browser reported a fine pointer (the phone project included). After: 0 typed arrows, 16 glyphs, 1 canvas, and 0 keycaps at 390 with either pointer. What changed, with the same four columns and the same two photographs: the masthead row is gone and the kit's `Band` (the judge's graft: "the live WebGL water … becomes the ground of C's blue band only (Entry, Home's head, the mark)") stands as the first of three panels at the photographs' height, the file's stamp in white at its foot; the photographs are rounded plates on the plate's shadow, each caption the kit's white `Plate` laid inset on its corner, each picture the kit's `Picture`; the six counts are `Stat`s led by their kind's glyph in the kind's ink; the makers' shelf is a `Plate`; the drafts head leads with the draft's glyph in its rose, the empty desk's steps with a boat, a hammer and the finale's paper plane, and a filed card carries `StatusDot`; New quote ends on the new paper in the act's disc, each photograph's door leads with it, All quotes with Quotes' own paper, and the find field with its magnifier. The `--radius-*: 0` override that made Home the one square room is deleted. Keys follow the sheet's rule, `(pointer: coarse), (max-width: 639.98px)`: a hand reads the same whatever its pointer. Motion, measured at 1440: on arrival the name rises on the water (the band's own 900 ms), the pictures fade in as they land, the frames and columns rise 8 px over 200 ms a half-press apart; the water changed over 700 ms with no caret and not at all with the caret in the search field, with 0 animations running while typing; under reduced motion 0 arrival animations ran and the water held still. A photograph's door names its picture with the picker's `boatTravel`, so the press carries it onto the picker's plate, and a filed card's picture carries its own name onto the build's stage or the paper's cover; a frame showing the card's own row gives its name up, since two of one name skip the whole transition. Fit held: the lowest column ends at 773 in 800, 873 in 900 and 1,049 in 1,080, empty and with a draft. Gate on 6317 against a private build: Home's unit tests 72 of 72; `check` 16 rules, 0 failures; Home's flow and pack-loads at six sizes 48 of 48; the shell's two Home cases 12 of 12; contrast, cut, overlap, focus, ramp, density and the shot recipe on Home 22 passed, 14 skipped by design, 0 failed (contrast 0 below threshold by day and by night, 18 text nodes over pictures read off the pixels; 11 Tab stops, 0 without a ring). The first run of the flow was red twice, at 1440 and 1920, and the red was real: the next decision. One tablet case timed out under another gate's load and passed alone in 9.7 s. Not done: the makers' marks are still not doors; at 1280, where the head is 240 tall, each caption plate reaches the top of its boat, as the old plates reached a stern. Lost: the name set as type in its own row, the caption line naming both boats a second time under their plates, the square room, the six counts as bare numbers over capitals, and the typed arrows and ⌕.
- **Home's photographs are hinted by the size they are drawn at, height included, and the Stacer takes the wide frame (2026-09-28, the same round).** With the band beside them the two frames stopped being letterboxes, so a 3 : 2 photograph now fills its frame by height as often as by width: the narrow frame at 1920 is 531 × 512, and covering it draws the picture 773 wide. The old hints (58vw and 42vw, then 42vw and 30vw) were widths only, and `e2e/flows/home.spec.ts` read the ADV7 drawn 512 tall against 383 held at 1920 and 336 against 287 at 1440: a photograph drawn past the pixels it arrived with, which this fold never does. One hint now serves both frames, `(max-width: 1199.98px) 100vw, 42vw`, the wider of each frame's width and its height at the picture's proportion at 1280, 1440 and 1920 (at most 40.3%), rounded up; the case passes at all six sizes. The Stacer 519 moved to the wide frame because in the narrow one its caption plate lay across the angler on its deck at 1440; wide, the open water beyond its bow holds the plate clear of both men, and the ADV7, narrow, keeps half a frame of sky above its T-top for its own. Both plates hang at their frame's top end. Lost: two hints that had to be kept in step with the stylesheet, and the ADV7 as the first photograph.
- **Entry's flag is the showpiece, and it moves because nothing takes the caret on arrival (2026-09-28, components critique major 7; src/ui touched: `Water` takes `swell`).** Measured before on a private build at 1440 × 900: the field autofocused, so a caret was on the page from the first paint and the flag's canvas changed 0 of 6,912 pixels in 700ms (0 of 4,320 at 390 × 844); the flag was 192 × 144 with the name at 16px, and its water, sized in drawn pixels for a register-wide band (one swell every 220px of page), showed less than half a swell — a sheen. Now the field waits for the person: a press, a Tab, or the first letter typed anywhere on the screen puts the caret in it and the letter lands (a modified key, a space and every key while something else holds focus are left alone). The flag is 240 × 174 from a tablet up with a tall window and 176 × 110 in a hand and on a phone turned sideways, the name set as the kit's band sets it (the first word 26px, light, tracked 0.2em; the rest 13px, 600, tracked 0.28em), it drops from the rule on the travel spring with the name out of a blur behind it, and its label hangs beside it under the rule from 1200 up instead of under its foot. `src/ui/water.ts` gains `SWELL` — broad 220px for a band, close 76px for a flag or a crest — so the flag holds two or three swells and their threads; a band's default is unchanged. Measured after: 9,223 of 10,440 flag pixels changed in 700ms at rest, 0 with the caret in the field, 8,739 once it left; the composition still fits 1280 × 800, 1440 × 900 and 1920 × 1080 exactly, the card moved from 315 to 294 at 1440 and from 229 to 194 at 844 × 390. A flow reads the flag's canvas the critic's way at six sizes. Lost: the autofocused field, the 192px flag with its 600-weight first word, and the label under the flag's foot on a desk.
- **Entry's door is its own progress while the file is read, not a refusal (2026-09-28, components critique major 11).** Measured before at 1440 × 900 with the file held at the wire: the door went navy under the warning glyph with "The Master Price File is being read now." beneath it, the two steps ticked in capsules at about x 1,045–1,340, y 812–870, some 600px from the press, and the refusal's row moved the card up 9px and the door 19px under the pointer (315 to 306, 663 to 644). Every title stands in one grid cell with the others held unseen and unread as its measure, and the facts line stays as the landed count's measure, so the door is 112px at rest and pressed at 1440 and 109px at 390; a flow holds the card, the door, the foot and the panel to the same boxes pressed as at rest. The steps' sentences go to a reader through an `<output>` under the door. A second press while it reads changes nothing and the face is the sentence that says why. The refusal ruler's Entry walk now holds the door to saying no refusal while it reads and reads its face on the water in both themes (0 below threshold at six sizes). Lost: the refusal while reading, the visible list of steps, and a face for "putting it into the app", which measured seven milliseconds against the read's two seconds and was one frame of flicker.
- **The pill is not drawn while a screen that stands without it is on the page (2026-09-28, components critique major 11; src/screens/shell/shell.css).** The router draws the shell for the address it is going to a few frames before the screen there replaces the one leaving: measured on a built app at 1440 × 900, Entry and the pill were on the page together for 70ms outside any transition, and the crossfade's old picture then carried the pill over Entry for its whole length. Entry's root says `data-shell="none"`, and `:root:has([data-shell='none']) .way-pill` is hidden, so the pill is neither drawn nor captured until Entry has gone and arrives with Home, crest and all; a flow reads every frame from the press to Home and finds 0 with the pill drawn over the door. Lost: nothing drawn; the shell still reads the router's `location`, because reading `resolvedLocation` would move every door's lit capsule, which is the shell's to decide.
- **The scrim behind Entry's caption ends before its own edges (2026-09-28, components critique minor 16).** Its ellipse was 66% by 58% of its box, wider than half of it, so the wash was still a sixth of its veil at the box's left edge and a quarter along its foot: differenced against the same photograph without it at 1440 × 900, the sharpest step in the wash was 33.5 of 255 in luminance along y 366, the hard foot the critic saw (x 822–1404, y 90–365). It is `closest-side` now, easing out through the room's faintest veil, over a box that bleeds further so the words stay as deep in it; the same difference finds no step at any edge at 1280, 1440 or 1920 (the sharpest left are the hairline rule and the photograph's own edges under the wash), and the caption reads 0 below threshold at six sizes in both themes. Lost: the ellipse that ran off its box.
- **The build's head is the same height given as draft (2026-09-28, components critique major 8).** Giving the quote grew the masthead an "Open the document" row under the pointer: measured on the Stacer 519 Sea Ranger SDF, the head was 160.8px as a draft and 188.8 given at 1440 × 900, and 176.7 and 219.5 at 390 × 844, where it is sticky and stayed 26% of the window for the rest of the quote's life. The row is gone, and the eyebrow says the kit's word for the state, "given", because "given to the customer" alone took it to a second line in a hand (191.5px). After, on the Stacer and on the ADV7: the head 152.3, 160.8, 160.8, 176.7 before and after the press at 1280, 1440, 834 and 390, the finale's head where it stood, and no button in the head. The way to the paper is the finale's act, where the press was, and an issued build opens on its finale. Lost: a second way to the paper from the head of every chapter, and the sentence in the eyebrow (the finale's head and its seal still say it).
- **Giving the quote is stamped by an authored Lottie, and lottie-web is adopted for it (2026-09-28, components critique major 8).** The critique: "There is no authored moment: Rive and Lottie are not installed, and the plan named 'the quote being issued' as the place for one." `src/screens/configurator/seal.ts` is its source: one drawing — a twelve-lobed rosette filled in `--status-given` and a tick stroked in `--color-panel` — from which both the Lottie timeline and the still SVG are made, so the stamp's last frame and a quote given last week are the same drawing point for point (`seal.test.ts` holds it). The timeline is the kit's own motion: the rim fades in over the press and lands from 1.35× and −10° on the settle spring, sampled one key a frame so nothing bounces; the ring it presses into the paper leaves on the same spring and is gone by frame 14; the tick draws itself over the enter tween on `--ease-out`; eight rays go out as the tick ends; still by frame 36, 600 ms at 60 fps. Drawn frame by frame before it was kept: a ring and rays on screen together read as the ticks of a dial, so they were parted in time. lottie-web's light player (SVG only, no expressions) is its own chunk — 169.42 kB, 47.57 kB gzip as built — fetched by a draft's finale ahead of the press and never under reduced motion; it plays once per give, keyed by the give's own event; a caret arriving stands it at its last frame; reduced motion, a failed fetch and every quote given before the build was opened draw the still SVG with no player. The timeline carries black on every fill and stroke and configurator.css paints each layer by its class from tokens, day and night; Northside's accent is not in it, because given is a state with its own ink. Proved at six sizes in `e2e/flows/configurator.spec.ts`: the seal is the player's drawing, its rim and tick computed in the kit's inks rather than black, and under reduced motion it is the still seal and no `stamp-*.js` is requested. Lost: the previous round's sprung SealCheck glyph in a ringed disc, that round's "Lost: Rive or Lottie for this moment", and Rive, whose runtime is 94 KB of script and 360 KB of WASM and whose file needs its own editor; a Lottie is JSON, and this one is authored in code.
- **A name typed in Who it is for goes on the quote when its chapter closes (2026-09-29, components critique major 10; supersedes the entry of 2026-09-28 that refused it).** Measured on a built app at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844 with that entry in place: typing "Jordan Pike", Tab, and opening The finale kept the name in the field. The give was still refused, in two sentences for one cause ("“Jordan Pike” is typed in Who it is for and is not on this quote yet. Press Address this quote there first. This quote is addressed to nobody…"). The folded head read "nobody named yet", and the stage beside it says "Saved as you go". Now closing the chapter does what the press does. It runs the same `setCustomer` command, as one step in the toast with its Undo ("For Jordan Pike · Undo"), written before the paint. So the finale's first frame has a live "Give it to the customer" and the head reads "for Jordan Pike". Enter in either field presses it. Leaving the build with a name still in the field writes it. Only a difference is written, read off the document as it stands, so the fold and the unmount write once between them. While the fields differ from the quote, the line under the button says "What is typed goes on the quote when you press this, or when this chapter closes." Lost: the refusal that named a typed name and still sent the salesperson back up the page to press. Saving on blur stays lost, because Tab from the name to the contact line must not write a step.
- **The build says each step in the kit's toast, and the toast's Undo holds the list still (2026-09-29, components critique major 9; src/ui touched: `say` and `undo` take an `id`, a test handle and "Put it back", `unsay` takes a toast away, and a toast wears the kit's ring when Tab reaches it).** Measured before on a private build of this tree at 1440 × 900, on the Highfield Sport 660 (the deepest model on the file, its starred F175XC already on), pressing the F175XCB grew the rail head from 106 to 162px with the step line. It is raised under the document's own id, so the next step changes the card where it stands and two are never shown. Giving the quote takes the toast away, because the seal says it, and so does leaving the build. A keyboard reaches it with Alt T (Sonner's own key), which holds the clock and gives the caret back to the row. The toast's Undo holds the control the step was pressed on, or else the option nearest the middle of the window. Before that hold, the list rose 70px at 1440, 118px at 390 and 69.6px at 834 × 1112 as the engine's sentence left; a first try held the control nearest the middle, which at 834 was the chapter's own head above the sentence, and the gate was red there. The toast stands at x 1068–1424, y 833–884 at 1440, and above the pill at y 725–776 at 390. The Undo leaves the tile at 648.2 → 648.6 and 340.8 → 340.8. Gate, private build: on port 6333 the build's flows at six sizes passed 116, skipped 3 and failed 1. The failure was a timeout at 1920 while another round gated the same screen (21.2 minutes against 12.8), and it passed alone with a 120 s timeout. On port 6334 the build's contrast, cut, overlap, ramp and focus rulers and the sale's refusals passed 24, skipped 18 by design and failed 0. The build's and the Toaster's unit tests passed 126 of 126, and `check` found no failures in 16 rules. The four other flows that read the build's step passed 7 and skipped 1 at 1440 and 390. Lost: the step line in the rail's head and its given leaf, a toast for giving the quote, and the last round's reason for keeping the step inline (a way back that leaves after eight seconds). Alt T answers that reason, and so does the row, which is its own way back.
- **History's fortnight shows the boat it was for, over one strip of fourteen days, and nothing is stretched to fill the floor (2026-09-29, components critique major 13; src/ui not touched).** Measured before on a private build of this tree at 1440 × 900, on the critic's walk: the Stacer 519 Sea Ranger SDF, two motors with one put back, addressed and given. The fortnight was 1,159 × 459. It held a 144px dashed cell and a tile of about 1,000 × 430, and the tile held "29 Tue", the boat's name, "R. Kelleher" and six dots. The 519's photograph is held and is drawn on the picker, the stage, Home and the register. History read pictures through its own copy reader, which asked the catalogue ledger alone, so it drew the name alone. Now there is no tile. The boats the fortnight quoted stand under its caption, once for each boat and person and newest first, drawn by the one reader (`@/data/pictures`, so `history/pictures.ts` is deleted). The photograph sits in a 3 : 2 frame whose height is a share of the window: 330 × 220 at 1440, 480 × 320 at 1920 and 210 × 140 at 1280. On arrival it rises out of the frame's foot on the kit's morph spring, and nothing moves under reduced motion (measured: the seven `hy-` animations run on arrival and none run under `reduce`). It carries the name that hull travels under (`boatTravel`), so Open the build or Open the document morphs it into the stage or the cover. A boat with no photograph held is its maker's white mark on the band and grain, on a shorter 5 : 2 band that says "no photograph held". Under the boats is one strip of fourteen days. The days before the diary began are one cell, as wide as the days it covers (160px at least on a desk), with a broken line, and its dates and words are said once. Every kept day is a column of its dots over its date, and today's column wears the accent's wash. The fortnight is exactly as tall as what it holds, and the run centres it on the spine. With one line, the space over and under it on the walk's boat and on the 519 measured 42/34 and 77/69 at 1280, 52/44 and 103/95 at 1440, and 92/84 and 163/155 at 1920, every one inside the fifth of the window the floor rule allows. Two versions of one conversation drew one photograph twice side by side on the first try, so a card is a boat for a person. The cards first stood right to left over today's end of the strip; at 1440 that left an 840px blank under the caption, and the eye arrives at the caption. Lost: the tile per kept day with its boats listed inside, the thirteen dim dates on the phone's strip, History's own copy reader, and the right-to-left order.
- **A History line's counted words wrap whole and are never cut, and the reference holds its version (2026-09-29, components critique major 13; src/ui not touched).** Measured before at 1440 × 900 on the critic's walk: "started · 2 picks · 1 put back · addressed · issued" needs 349px, and what happened had 341 (one share to the boat's 1.2), so the word that says where the quote got to read "iss…", and the same with the fold open. At a desk the hull is set a step quieter (12px) than the person it is for. It had been 12px anyway, by accident: the fortnight's tile styled `.hy-boat`, which is also the line's boat. The reference column is 112px, because "20260929-01 v1 of 2" ran 16px into the standing word at 1280, 1440 and 1920. The total gives back 8px that "Nothing on it yet" does not use. On a phone the person carries to the next line whole ("R." had ended one line and "Kelleher" started the next), and the separator ends the boat's line. Gate on port 6346, a private build: History's flows at six sizes passed 52, skipped 5 and failed 3. The three were this round's own new 3 : 2 assertion reading the walk's boat, which has the 5 : 2 mark band, so the test was at fault and now reads the frame's art first. The rerun of that case and History's contrast, cut, density, focus, overlap and ramp rulers at six sizes passed 23, skipped 19 by design and failed 0.
